Naïve co-contra correspondence for 𝒟 \mathcal{D} -modules
Leonid Positselski

Abstract
. We construct a triangulated equivalence between the derived categories of quasi-coherent and contraherent 𝒜 \mathcal{A} -modules for a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra 𝒜 \mathcal{A} over a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme X X . The sheaf of rings of differential operators 𝒟 \mathcal{D} (or fiberwise differential operators for a smooth morphism of schemes) is the thematic example of a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra.



Introduction

The concept of
Koszul duality
goes back to
Chevalley–Eilenberg
[
14
]
, Koszul
[
38
]
,
Eilenberg–Mac Lane
[
18
]
, Adams
[
1
,
2
]
,
Quillen
[
65
]
, and Priddy
[
64
]
.

Derived Koszul duality
means triangulated equivalences of
module categories over Koszul dual algebras; this topic goes back to
Bernstein–Gelfand–Gelfand
[
10
]
and Beilinson
[
6
]
.
For recent surveys penned by the present author,
see
[
50
, Prologue]
and
[
53
]
.

The duality between rings of differential operators and DG-algebras
of differential forms is one of the primary examples of Koszul duality
in algebraic and differential geometry.
The
\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality is an instance of
nonhomogeneous
Koszul duality
, in that the rings of differential operators are not
graded, but only filtered.

The relations defining the rings of differential operators involve
functions and vector fields.
These relations are
nonhomogeneous quadratic
, in that they have
principal quadratic parts (with respect to the vector fields) and
additional linear and scalar terms.
The differential on the de Rham DG-algebra corresponds to
the lower-degree summands in the relations defining the ring of
differential operators.

Attempts to work out triangulated equivalences of the derived

\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality were made by Kapranov
[
34
]
and
Beilinson–Drinfeld
[
8
, Section 7.2]
.
The current state of the art can be found in the present author’s
memoir
[
45
, Appendix B]
and book
[
50
]
.

As established by Hinich
[
28
]
, Lefèvre-Hasegawa
[
40
]
,
Keller
[
37
]
, and the present author
[
63
]
,

[
45
, Section 6]
, Koszul duality is naturally viewed as
connecting
algebras
with
coalgebras
.
Following an observation going back to Eilenberg and Moore
[
19
]

and developed in
[
44
,
47
]
, there are
two
kinds of
natural module categories over a coalgebra: in addition to the more
familiar
comodules
, there are also
contramodules
.
Accordingly, the Koszul duality as worked out in
[
45
]

happens on the comodule and contramodule side, connected with each
other by the
comodule-contramodule correspondence
.
We refer to
[
47
]
and
[
53
, Sections 8–9]
for
introductory survey expositions.

The main innovation of the modern theory of derived nonhomogeneous
Koszul duality is the
derived categories of the second kind
.
The basic idea and the terminology of the
first
vs.
second kind
in this context goes back to Husemoller, Moore,
and Stasheff
[
31
]
, but the actual definitions of weak equivalences
of the second kind are due to Hinich
[
28
]
,
Lefèvre-Hasegawa
[
40
]
, Keller
[
37
]
, and the present
author
[
44
,
47
]
.
The terminology is a bit misleading, as “derived categories of
the second kind” is a generic name for several different constructions,
the most important of them known as the
absolute derived
,

coderived
, and
contraderived categories
.
We refer to
[
59
, Remark 9.2]
and
[
45
, Section 7]
for
historical and philosophical discussions.

Following the philosophy elaborated upon in
[
44
,
45
]
, one
is generally supposed to consider the conventional
derived

categories of modules, the
coderived
categories of comodules,
and the
contraderived
categories of contramodules (though there
are many exceptions to this rule).
So the “co” side of derived Koszul duality features the coderived
category of comodules, while on the “contra” side one considers
the contraderived category of contramodules.

In addition to the distinction between the
homogeneous
Koszul
duality (as in
[
10
,
6
,
9
]
) and the
nonhomogeneous
one
(as in
[
28
,
40
,
37
,
63
,
45
,
53
]
), there is also
a relevant distinction between
absolute
and
relative

Koszul duality.
Here “absolute” means “over a field” (or in the most complicated
cases, over a semisimple ring, as in
[
9
]
, or over a semisimple
coalgebra, as in
[
29
]
).
“Relative” means over a more or less arbitrary (certainly
nonsemisimple) ring
[
44
, Section 0.4]
,
[
50
]
or
coalgebra
[
44
, Chapter 11]
.
The
\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality is a species of relative nonhomogeneous
Koszul duality, in that it happens over the base ring of functions.

The derived nonhomogeneous Koszul duality theory developed in the main
body of the memoir
[
45
]
and surveyed in
[
53
]
is
absolute over a field.
The relative case over a base ring is more complicated in many
respects, and first of all, in that it needs to be properly understood
what constitutes the coalgebra side.
The answer is that the ring of differential operators
\mathcal{D}
stands
on the algebra side, while the de Rham DG-ring
\Omega^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
plays
the role of the coalgebra.
Accordingly, one needs to consider the coderived category of
DG-modules over
\Omega^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
on the “comodule” side, and
the contraderived category of the “contramodule” side.

Generally speaking, in the context of relative nonhomogeneous Koszul
duality over a base ring, one has to consider comodules and
contramodules over graded rings
[
50
, Chapters 5-7]
.
This distinction does
not
manifest itself in the case of
the
\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality, as the de Rham DG-algebra
\Omega^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is bounded in its cohomological grading.
But the distinction (and the connections) between the
coderived

and the
contraderived
category of DG-modules over
\Omega^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is important.

The relative nonhomogeneous Koszul duality theory spelled out in
the book
[
50
]
is affine; there are
no
nonaffine
schemes in
[
50
]
.
The earlier exposition on
\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality
in
[
45
, Appendix B]
covers nonaffine algebraic varieties,
but only on the “co” side.
All the assertions about the contraderived categories
in
[
45
, Appendix B]
apply to affine varieties only.
The reason is that the definition of the contraderived category
does
not
make sense for quasi-coherent sheaves.

In fact, there are two such definitions.
The approach of the present author, as per
[
44
,
45
]
,
uses infinite products, and makes sense when the infinite product
functors are exact.
But infinite products of quasi-coherent sheaves are not exact.
The alternative approach, named after Becker with the reference to
his paper
[
5
]
and going back to Jørgensen
[
33
]
,
Krause
[
39
]
, and Neeman
[
42
]
, uses projective objects
or DG-modules whose underlying graded modules are projective.
But nonzero projective quasi-coherent sheaves usually do not exist.

Contraherent cosheaves
were invented in
[
46
]
in order
to rectify the situation.
For any scheme
X
, the exact category of contraherent cosheaves

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
has exact functors of infinite products.
Under mild assumptions (e. g., for quasi-compact semi-separated
schemes
X
), this exact category also has enough projective objects.
So both the Positselski and Becker constructions of contraderived
categories are applicable to contraherent cosheaves.

In the context of
\mathcal{D}
-
\Omega
duality, the constructions of
the derived categories of the second kind are applied to DG-modules
over the de Rham DG-algebra
\Omega^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, while for modules over
the sheaf of rings of differential operators
\mathcal{D}
one is mostly
interested in the conventional derived categories (or in their
modifications called the “semiderived categories”).
In the present version of this paper, we work with what we call

quasi-coherent quasi-algebras

\mathcal{A}
over quasi-compact
semi-separated schemes
X
.
These are natural generalizations of the sheaves of rings of
differential operators
\mathcal{D}
.

In this setting, we construct a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}})
between the conventional derived
category of the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent sheaves
of
\mathcal{A}
-modules and the conventional derived category of the exact
category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
of contraherent cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
The equivalence holds for the bounded and one-sided bounded as well as
for the unbounded conventional derived categories.
This is an instance of what we call the “naïve co-contra
correspondence” phenomenon and a far-reaching generalization
of the similar result for quasi-coherent and contraherent

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
[
46
, Theorem 4.8.1]
.

The word “naïve” means precisely that this version of
the co-contra correspondence connects the conventional derived
categories rather than the coderived and contraderived categories.
We refer to the introduction to the paper
[
48
]
for a discussion
of the philosophy of co-contra correspondence, and to
[
46
, Section 0.11
of the Introduction]
for further comments on the naïve
co-contra correspondence and an explanation of how it arises.

In the final Section
6
, we introduce
quasi-coherent curved differential graded (CDG) quasi-algebras over
schemes and construct the DG-categories of quasi-coherent and
locally contraherent CDG-modules over such CDG-quasi-algebras.

1.
Module-Theoretic Preliminaries

The first half of this section is mostly an extraction
from
[
46
, Chapter 1]
and
[
55
, Section 4]
.
The second half of the section, starting from
Section
1.4
, expands and improves
upon the first half of
[
52
, Section 2.3]
.
One of the most important new results is
Lemma
1.17
.

1.1.
Homological formula of derived Hom-tensor adjunction

The following “homological formula” is useful for proving many
lemmas in Section
1
.

Lemma 1.1
.

Let
R
and
S
be two associative rings.
Let
E
be a left
R
-module,
F
be an
R
-
S
-bimodule
and
G
be a left
S
-module.
Let
n\geq 0
be an integer such that
\Ext^{i}_{R}(F,E)=0=\Tor^{S}_{i}(F,G)

for all
0<i\leq n
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of abelian groups

\Ext^{n}_{S}(G,\Hom_{R}(F,E))\simeq\Ext^{n}_{R}(F\otimes_{S}G,\>E).

Proof.

This is a variation on the theme of
[
13
, formula (4) in
Section XVI.4]
suggested in
[
46
, formula (1.1)
in Section 1.2]
.
The formula is easily provable by a spectral sequence argument
similar to the one in
[
13
]
.
Alternatively, here is a more elementary proof.
Pick a projective resolution

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muQ_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muQ_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muQ_{0}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muG\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

of the left
S
-module
G
and an injective coresolution

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muE\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muJ^{0}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muJ^{1}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muJ^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb

of the left
R
-module
E
.

By assumption, the complex
F\otimes_{S}Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is exact at all
the homological degrees
0<i\leq n
; so its terms sitting in
the homological degrees from
0
to
n+1
form an initial
fragment of a resolution of the left
R
-module
F\otimes_{S}G
.
Extending this initial fragment to an actual resolution
C_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
we obtain an exact complex of left
R
-modules
C_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow F\otimes_{S}G\longrightarrow 0
together with a commutative diagram of morphisms of
left
R
-modules

\textstyle{F\otimes_{R}Q_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\otimes_{R}Q_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dotsb\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\otimes_{R}Q_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\otimes_{R}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\dotsb\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{n+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dotsb\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\otimes_{R}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Dual-analogously, the complex
\Hom_{R}(F,J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is exact at all
the cohomological degrees
0<i\leq n
; so its terms sitting in
the cohomological degrees from
0
to
n+1
form an initial
fragment of a coresolution of the left
S
-module
\Hom_{R}(F,E)
.
Extending this initial fragment to an actual coresolution
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
we obtain an exact complex of left
S
-modules
0\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(F,E)\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
together with a commutative diagram
of morphisms of left
S
-modules

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{R}(F,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{R}(F,J^{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dotsb\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{R}(F,J^{n+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom_{R}(F,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dotsb\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{n+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dotsb}

Now we have natural isomorphisms of abelian groups

\Ext^{n}_{S}(G,\Hom_{R}(F,E))=H^{n}\Hom_{S}(Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\Hom_{R}(F,E))\\
\simeq H^{n}\Hom_{S}(Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=H^{n}\Hom_{S}(Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\Hom_{R}(F,J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\\
\simeq H^{n}\Hom_{R}(F\otimes_{R}Q_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=H^{n}\Hom_{R}(C_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
\simeq H^{n}\Hom_{R}(F\otimes_{R}G,\>J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=\Ext^{n}_{R}(F\otimes_{R}G,\>E).

Here the total complexes of the bicomplexes of
\Hom
are presumed in
the notation.
The first and last equalities hold by the definition of
\Ext^{n}
, while
the two middle equalities follow from the commutative diagrams above.
The inner isomorphism is the usual Hom-tensor adjunction, while
the two outer isomorphisms hold due to the quasi-isomorphisms

\Hom_{R}(F,E)\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
C_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow F\otimes_{S}G
.
∎

1.2.
Flat and cotorsion modules

Let
R
be an associative ring.
We will denote by
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
the category of left
R
-modules and by

{\operatorname{\mathsf{Mod--}}}R
the category of right
R
-modules.

A left
R
-module
C
is said to be
cotorsion
(
in
the sense of Enochs

[
22
]
) if
\Ext^{1}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all flat
left
R
-modules
F
.
Clearly, the class of cotorsion
R
-modules is closed under
extensions, direct summands, and direct products.

Lemma 1.2
.

(a)
One has
\Ext^{n}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all flat
R
-modules
F
,
all cotorsion
R
-modules
C
, and all
n\geq 1
.

(b)
The class of cotorsion
R
-modules is closed under
cokernels of monomorphisms in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Proof.

Part (a) follows from the fact that, in any projective resolution
of the
R
-module
F
, the
R
-module of cycles are flat.
In other words, the point is that the class of flat
R
-modules
is closed under kernels of epimorphisms.
Part (b) follows easily from part (a) for
n=2
.
∎

Lemma 1.3
.

A left
R
-module
F
is flat if and only if
\Ext_{R}^{1}(F,C)=0

for all cotorsion left
R
-modules
C
.

Proof.

The assertion can be deduced from
Theorem
1.4
(a)
below; see, e. g.,
[
46
, Lemma B.1.2]
.
However, in fact, Lemma
1.3
is
a much easier (and historically much earlier) result than the much more
substantial Theorem
1.4
.
See
[
72
, Lemma 3.4.1]
.
The point is that all left
R
-modules of the form

C=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
, where
N
ranges over the right

R
-modules, are cotorsion (in fact, they are pure-injective,
which is a stronger property).
Then one computes that
\Ext^{1}_{R}(F,\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))\simeq\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Tor_{1}^{R}(N,F),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
for all left
R
-modules
F
.
∎

The following theorem says that there are enough cotorsion modules,
in a strong sense.

Theorem 1.4
.

For any
R
-module
M
, there exist

(a)
a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0
with a flat
R
-module
F

and a cotorsion
R
-module
C^{\prime}
;

(b)
a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
with a cotorsion
R
-module
C

and a flat
R
-module
F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This one of the formulations of what became known as
the
Flat Cover Conjecture
, posed by Enochs in
[
21
]

and proved by Bican–El Bashir–Enochs in
[
11
]
.
In fact, there are two proofs in the paper
[
11
]
; the one
more closely related to the formulation above is based on
the preceding work of Eklof and Trlifaj
[
20
]
.

The short exact sequence in part (a) is called a
special
flat precover sequence
.
The short exact sequence in part (b) is called a
special
cotorsion preenvelope sequence
.
A further discussion can be found in
[
25
, Theorem 8.1(a)]
,

[
46
, Theorem 1.3.1]
, and
[
55
, Theorem 4.4]
.
For explicit examples of special flat precovers and special cotorsion
preenvelopes of abelian groups, see
[
49
, Section 12]
.
∎

The results of Lemmas
1.2
–
1.3
and
Theorem
1.4
can be summarized by saying
that the pair of classes (flat modules, cotorsion modules) is
a
hereditary complete cotorsion pair
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
The notion of a cotorsion pair (or a
cotorsion theory
) goes
back to Salce
[
68
]
.
See
[
25
, Chapters 5–6]
,
[
55
, Section 4]
,
or
[
46
, Appendix B]
for a discussion.

Lemma 1.5
.

Let
R
and
S
be two associative rings,
F
be an
R
-
S
-bimodule,
and
E
be a left
R
-module.
In this context:

(a)
if
F
is a flat left
R
-module and
E
is a cotorsion
left
R
-module, then
\Hom_{R}(F,E)
is a cotorsion left

S
-module;

(b)
if
F
is a flat right
S
-module and
E
is an injective
left
R
-module, then
\Hom_{R}(F,E)
is an injective left
S
-module;

(c)
if
E
is an injective left
R
-module, then

\Hom_{R}(F,E)
is a cotorsion left
S
-module.

Proof.

All the three assertions can be obtained from the homological
formula of Lemma
1.1
; this is
the approach in
[
46
, Lemma 1.3.3]
.
In all the three cases, the following more elementary argument also
works.
Let us explain part (a).
It suffices to show that the functor

G\longmapsto\Hom_{S}(G,\Hom_{R}(F,E))
takes short exact sequences of
flat left
S
-modules
G
to short exact sequences of abelian groups
(since all projective modules are flat and the syzygy modules of
flat modules are flat).
Indeed, we have
\Hom_{S}(G,\Hom_{R}(F,E))\simeq\Hom_{R}(F\otimes_{S}G,\>E)
,
the functor
F\otimes_{S}{-}
is exact on the category of flat left

S
-modules
G
, the tensor product
F\otimes_{S}G
of an
R
-flat

R
-
S
-bimodule
F
and a flat left
S
-module
G
is a flat
left
R
-module, and the functor
\Hom_{R}({-},E)
into a cotorsion
left
R
-module
E
is exact on the category flat left
R
-modules.
∎

Lemma 1.6
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of associative rings.
In this context:

(a)
if
D
is a cotorsion
S
-module, then
D
is also
cotorsion as an
R
-module;

(b)
if
K
is an injective left
S
-module and
S
is
flat as a right
R
-module, then
K
is also injective as a left

R
-module;

(c)
if
C
is a cotorsion left
R
-module and
S
is
flat as a left
R
-module, then
\Hom_{R}(S,C)
is a cotorsion
left
S
-module;

(d)
if
J
is a an injective
R
-module, then

\Hom_{R}(S,J)
is an injective
S
-module.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, Lemmas 1.3.4 and 1.3.5]
.
Both an argument based on Lemma
1.1
and
an argument similar to the one spelled out in the proof of
Lemma
1.5
above are applicable in all
the cases.
∎

1.3.
Very flat and contraadjusted modules

Let
R
be a commutative ring.
Given an element
s\in R
, we denote by
R[s^{-1}]
the ring
R

with the element
s
inverted, i. e., the localization of
R
at
the multiplicative subset
\{1,s,s^{2},s^{3},\dotsc\}
.
For an
R
-module
M
, we put
M[s^{-1}]=R[s^{-1}]\otimes_{R}M
.

An
R
-module
C
is said to be

contraadjusted

[
46
, Section 1.1]
,

[
49
, Section 2]
if
\Ext^{1}_{R}(R[s^{-1}],C)=0
for all
elements
s\in R
.
(The terminology “contraadjusted” means “adjusted to
contraherent cosheaves”.)
An
R
-module
F
is said to be

very flat

[
46
, Section 1.1]
,
[
57
]
if

\Ext^{1}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all contraadjusted
R
-modules
C
.

It is clear that the class of contraadjusted
R
-modules is closed
under extensions, direct summands, and infinite products; while
the class of very flat
R
-modules is closed under extensions,
direct summands, and infinite direct sums.
The following lemma lists slightly more nontrivial properties.

Lemma 1.7
.

(a)
Any quotient
R
-module of a contraadjusted

R
-module is contraadjusted.

(b)
The projective dimension of any very flat
R
-module
does not exceed
1
.

(c)
The class of very flat
R
-modules is closed under
kernels of epimorphisms in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, beginning of Section 1.1]
.
Part (a) follows from the fact that the projective dimension of
the
R
-module
R[s^{-1}]
never exceeds
1

[
49
, proof of
Lemma 2.1]
.
Both parts (b) and (c) follow from part (a).
∎

The following theorem says that there are enough very flat and
contraadjusted modules.

Theorem 1.8
.

For any
R
-module
M
, there exist

(a)
a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0
with a very flat
R
-module
F

and a contraadjusted
R
-module
C^{\prime}
;

(b)
a short exact sequence of
R
-modules

0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
with a contraadjusted

R
-module
C
and a very flat
R
-module
F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, Theorem 1.1.1]
or
[
55
, Theorem 4.5]
,
or a special case of
[
20
, Theorems 2 and 10]
.
In the terminology of cotorsion pairs
[
68
,
20
,
25
]
, the pair
of classes (very flat modules, contraadjusted modules) is, by
the definition, the cotorsion pair
generated by
the set of

R
-modules
R[s^{-1}]
,
s\in R
, in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
The theorem says that this cotorsion pair is
complete

(while Lemma
1.7
implies
that it is
hereditary
).
For any associative ring
R
, any cotorsion pair generated by
a set of modules in complete in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}

[
20
]
,

[
25
, Theorem 6.11]
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
1.8

provides more information than the theorem says.
Specifically, the class of very flat
R
-modules can be described
as the class of all direct summands of
transfinitely iterated
extensions
(
in the sense of the inductive limit
) of
the
R
-modules
R[s^{-1}]
,
s\in R
.
See
[
46
, Corollary 1.1.4]
,
[
55
, Theorem 4.5(b)]
,
or
[
25
, Corollary 6.14]
.

Examples 1.9
.

(1) Let
U
be an affine scheme and
V
be an affine open
subscheme in
U
.
Then the ring of functions
\mathcal{O}(V)
is a very flat module over
the ring of functions
\mathcal{O}(V)
.
This is
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.4]
.

(2) Much more generally, let
R\longrightarrow S
be a morphism of
commutative rings such that
S
is a finitely presented commutative

R
-algebra and a flat
R
-module.
Then
S
is a very flat
R
-module.
This is the assertion of the
Very Flat Conjecture
, which was
proved in
[
57
, Main Theorem 1.1]
.

See also the discussion in
[
55
, Remark 5.1]
.

Lemma 1.10
.

(a)
For any two very flat
R
-modules
F
and
G
,
the tensor product
F\otimes_{R}G
is a very flat
R
-module.

(b)
For any very flat
R
-module
F
and contraadjusted

R
-module
C
, the
R
-module
\Hom_{R}(F,C)
is contraadjusted.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.1]
.
∎

Lemma 1.11
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings.
In this context:

(a)
if
D
is a contraadjusted
S
-module, then
D
is
also contraadjusted as an
R
-module;

(b)
for any very flat
R
-module
F
, the
S
-module

S\otimes_{R}F
is very flat;

(c)
if the
R
-module
S[s^{-1}]
is very flat for
every element
s\in S
and
G
is a very flat
S
-module, then
G

is also very flat as an
R
-module;

(d)
if the
R
-module
S[s^{-1}]
is very flat for
every element
s\in S
and
C
is a contraadjusted
R
-module,
then
\Hom_{R}(S,C)
is a contraadjusted
S
-module.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.2(a–b) and Lemma 1.2.3(a–b)]
.
∎

Notice that the
R
-module
S
itself being very flat is
not

enough for the validity of
Lemma
1.11
(c–d), and the stronger
assumption that
S[s^{-1}]
is a very flat
R
-module for all
s\in S

is needed.
See
[
57
, Lemma 9.3(a) and Example 9.7]

or
[
55
, Lemma 6.1]
.
When the latter assumption is satisfied, one says that
S
is
a very flat commutative
R
-algebra
or
the commutative ring
homomorphism
R\longrightarrow S
is very flat
.

1.4.
Quasi-modules

The terminology
quasi-modules
in this section agrees
with the one in
[
56
, Section 2.1]
and does
not
agree
with the terminology in
[
52
, Section 2.3]
.
The more narrow class of quasi-modules considered
in
[
52
, Section 2.3]
is called the class of
strong
quasi-modules
in
[
56
, Section 2.2]
, and we follow the terminology
of
[
56
]
in this paper.
The point is that, out of the three definitions of a quasi-module (with
various qualifiers) discussed in
[
56
, Section 2]
, the least
restrictive one still words well for our purposes.

A more common terminology for what we,
following
[
56
, Section 2.2]
, call a strong quasi-module (and what
is called simply a “quasi-module” in
[
52
, Section 2.3]
) is
a
differential bimodule

[
7
, Section 1.1]
.
See Appendix
A
below for a further
discussion.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
B
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule.
Given an element
r\in R
, let us define the natural increasing
filtration
F^{(r)}
on
B
by the rules

•

F^{(r)}_{n}B=0
for all integers
n<0
;

•

for each integer
n\geq 0
,
F^{(r)}_{n}B
is the set of all
elements
b\in B
such that
rb-br\in F^{(r)}_{n-1}B
.

One can easily see that
F^{(r)}B
is an
R
-
R
-subbimodule in
B

and
F^{(r)}_{n-1}B\subset F^{(r)}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We say that
B
is a
quasi-module
over
R
if, for every element

r\in R
, the filtration
F^{(r)}
on
B
is exhaustive, i. e.,

B=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}F_{n}^{(r)}B
.

For a geometric interpretation of quasi-modules as bimodules supported
in the diagonal
\Spec R\subset\Spec R\times_{\Spec\mathbb{Z}}\Spec R
,
see
[
56
, Corollary 2.3]
.

Clearly, the full subcategory of quasi-modules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
is closed under subobjects, quotients, and infinite direct
sums in the abelian category of
R
-
R
-bimodules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
According to the discussion in
[
56
, Sections 1.1 and 2.1]

(see specifically
[
56
, Lemma 1.1 and Corollary 2.2]
),
the full subcategory
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
is also closed under extensions in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
In other words, one can say that
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
is a
hereditary
torsion class
(in the terminology of
[
69
, Sections VI.2–3]
),
or equivalently, a
localizing subcategory
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
Hence
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
is an abelian category (in fact, a Grothendieck
category) with an exact inclusion functor
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R

preserving infinite direct sums.

In particular,
F^{(r)}_{0}B\subset B
is the subbimodule of all
elements in
B
on which the left and right actions of
the element
r\in R
agree.
Therefore, any
R
-module
M
, viewed as an
R
-
R
-bimodule in
which the left and right actions of
R
agree, is a quasi-module
over
R
(with
F^{(r)}_{0}M=M
for all
r\in R
).
So we have
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.

Given two
R
-
R
-bimodules
A
and
B
(in particular, two
quasi-modules over
R
) we will use the notation
A\otimes_{R}B
in
the usual sense of the tensor product of the right
R
-module
structure on
A
and the left
R
-module structure on
B
.
So the right
R
-module structure on
A
and the left and

R
-module structure on
B
get eaten up in the construction of
the tensor product, which becomes an
R
-
R
-bimodule with
the left action of
R
coming from the left action of
R
in
A

and the right action of
R
coming from the right action of
R
in
B
.

Similarly, given an
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
and an
R
-module
M
,
the notation
B\otimes_{R}M
stands for the tensor product of the right

R
-module structure on
B
with the
R
-module structure of

M
, while
M\otimes_{R}B
is the tensor product of the
R
-module
structure on
M
with the left
R
-module structure on
B
.
Viewing
M
as an
R
-
R
-bimodule in which the left and right
actions of
R
agree, we obtain
R
-
R
-bimodule structures on

B\otimes_{R}M
and
M\otimes_{R}B
, as per the previous paragraph.

One can check that the full subcategory
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
is closed under
tensor products over
R
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R

[
56
, Proposition 2.4]
.
Furthermore, the unit object of the monoidal category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R

(with respect to the tensor product functor
\otimes_{R}
) is
the diagonal bimodule
R
in which the left and right actions of
R

agree, so
R\in R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
according to the discussion
above.
Thus
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
is a monoidal full subcategory in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.

Lemma 1.12
.

Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings and
B
be
a quasi-module over
S
.
Then, viewed as an
R
-
R
-bimodule,
B
is also a quasi-module
over
R
.

Proof.

Let
r\in R
be an element and
s=f(r)\in S
be the image of
r

in
S
.
Denoting by
F^{(r)}
the related increasing filtration on
B
as
an
R
-
R
-bimodule and by
G^{(s)}
the related increasing filtration
on
B
as an
S
-
S
-bimodule, one notices that the two filtrations
coincide,
G^{(s)}_{n}B=F^{(r)}_{n}B
for all
n\geq 0
.
Thus, if the filtration
G^{(s)}
is exhaustive for all
s\in f(R)\subset S
, then so is the filtration
F^{(r)}
for all
r\in R
.
∎

Lemma 1.13
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
r\in R
be an element.
Let
B
be a quasi-module over
R
.
Then

(a)
the natural maps
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\longrightarrow R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]\longleftarrow B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]
are
isomorphisms;

(b)
the
R[r^{-1}]
-
R[r^{-1}]
-bimodule
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B

is a quasi-module over
R[r^{-1}]
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to
[
52
, proof of Lemma 2.2]
.
Part (a): it suffices to show that the
R
-
R[r^{-1}]
-bimodule

B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]
, viewed as an
R
-module with respect to the left
action of
R
, belongs to the essential image of the fully
faithful functor of restriction of scalars
R[r^{-1}]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Indeed, consider the natural filtration
F^{(r)}
on the

R
-
R
-bimodule
B
as defined above in this section,
and put
G_{n}^{(r)}(B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}])=(F_{n}^{(r)}B)\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]\subset B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]
.
Then
G
is an exhaustive increasing filtration of
the
R
-
R[r^{-1}]
-bimodule
B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]
by subbimodules
such that the left and right actions of the element
r\in R

in the successive quotient bimodules

G_{n}^{(r)}(B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}])/G_{n-1}^{(r)}(B\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}])\simeq(F_{n}^{(r)}B/F_{n-1}^{(r)}B)\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]

agree.
Hence, viewed as
R
-modules with respect to the left action of
R
,
the successive quotient bimodules belong to the essential image of
the functor of restriction of scalars
R[r^{-1}]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
It remains to point out that this essential image is closed under
extensions and inductive limits in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Part (b): in view of the filtration argument in part (a) and the fact
that the class of all quasi-modules over
R[r^{-1}]
is closed under
extensions and inductive limits in
R[r^{-1}]{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R[r^{-1}]
, it
suffices to consider the case when the left and right actions of
the element
r
in the bimodule
B
coincide.
In this case, one easily checks that, for any element
s\in R
,
the rule
G_{n}^{(s)}(R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B)=R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}F_{n}^{(s)}B
defines
a filtration of the
R[r^{-1}]
-
R[r^{-1}]
-bimodule
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B

by
R[r^{-1}]
-
R[r^{-1}]
-subbimodules such that the left and right
actions of the element
s
in the successive quotient bimodules
coincide.
This proves that the natural increasing filtration
F^{(s)}
of
the
R[r^{-1}]
-
R[r^{-1}]
-bimodule
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B
is exhaustive.
The same assertion in the general case of an element
s\in R[r^{-1}]

also follows easily.
∎

Lemma 1.14
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
B
be a quasi-module over
R
.
Then

(a)
the natural maps
S\otimes_{R}B\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}S\longleftarrow B\otimes_{R}S
are isomorphisms;

(b)
the
S
-
S
-bimodule
S\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module
over
S
.

Proof.

Let
r_{1}
, …,
r_{N}\in R
be a finite family of elements such
that
\Spec S=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}\Spec R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\subset\Spec R
.
Then, for any
S
-module
M
, the Čech coresolution

(1)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muS\otimes_{R}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}M\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1},r_{\beta}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}M\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muR[r_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{N}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}M\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

is an exact sequence.

Returning to our quasi-module
B
over
R
, consider three
complexes: the Čech complex of the left
S
-module
S\otimes_{R}B

(2)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muS\otimes_{R}B\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1},r_{\beta}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muR[r_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{N}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0,

the Čech complex of the right
S
-module
B\otimes_{R}S

(3)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muB\otimes_{R}S\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}B\otimes_{R}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}B\otimes_{R}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1},r_{\beta}^{-1}]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muB\otimes_{R}R[r_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{N}^{-1}]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0,

and the Čech complex of the
S
-
S
-bimodule
S\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}S

(4)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muS\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}S\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1},r_{\beta}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1},r_{\beta}^{-1}]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muR[r_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{N}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}R[r_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{N}^{-1}]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

The complexes (
2
)
and (
3
) are exact as
particular cases of (
1
).
The complex (
4
) can be
obtained by applying the functor
{-}\otimes_{R}S
to
the complex (
2
), as one can
see from Lemma
1.13
(a).
So the complex (
4
) is
exact as well.

Now there are natural morphisms of complexes of
R
-
R
-bimodules
from both the complexes (
2
)
and (
3
) into
the complex (
4
).
Applying Lemma
1.13
(a)
again, we see that both the morphisms of complexes are isomorphisms
on all the terms except perhaps the leftmost ones.
It follows that both the morphisms of compexes are also isomorphisms
on the leftmost terms, as desired.

Part (b): according to (
2
),

S\otimes_{R}B
is an
S
-
S
-subbimodule of the
S
-
S
-bimodule

\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B
.
Each direct summand
R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module
over
R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]
by
Lemma
1.13
(b), hence
(by Lemma
1.12
) also
a quasi-module over
S
.
It follows that
S\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module over
S
.
∎

Corollary 1.15
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule,
B
be an
S
-
S
-bimodule,
and
A\longrightarrow B
be a homomorphism of
R
-
R
-bimodules.
Assume that the
R
-
R
-bimodule
A
is a quasi-module over
R
.
Then the induced morphism of
S
-
R
-bimodules
S\otimes_{R}A\longrightarrow B

is an isomorphism if and only if the induced morphism of

R
-
S
-bimodules
A\otimes_{R}S\longrightarrow B
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is our version of
[
52
, Corollary 2.3]
.
The assertion follows from
Lemma
1.14
(a).
∎

Lemma 1.16
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
be a finite collection of homomorphisms of
commutative rings such that the collection of induced maps of
the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is an affine open covering
of an affine scheme.
Let
B
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule.
Then the following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

B
is a quasi-module over
R
;

(2)

for every index
\alpha
, the right
R
-module structure on
the tensor product
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}B
comes from a (necessarily unique)

S_{\alpha}
-module structure, and the
S_{\alpha}
-
S_{\alpha}
-bimodule

S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module over
S_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

If
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
is a commutative ring homomorphism such that
the induced map of the spectra
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is an open
immersion, then
f
is a (
flat
)
ring epimorphism

in the sense of
[
69
, Chapter XI]
.
So the functor of restriction of scalars
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is
fully faithful, and the
S
-module structure of any
S
-module
is uniquely determined by its underlying
R
-module structure.
This explains the uniqueness assertion in (2).

Now the implication (1)
\Longrightarrow
(2) holds by
Lemma
1.14
.
To prove that (2)
\Longrightarrow
(1), notice that the natural
map
B\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}B
is an
R
-module
monomorphism.
By Lemma
1.12
, the condition
that
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module over
S_{\alpha}
implies that

S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}B
is a quasi-module over
R
.
It remains to point out that the (finite) direct sums of quasi-modules
are quasi-modules and any subbimodule of a quasi-module is
a quasi-module.
∎

Given an
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
and an
R
-module
E
, the notation

\Hom_{R}(B,E)
in part (b) of the following lemma stands for the abelian
group of all maps
B\longrightarrow E
that are
R
-linear with respect to
the left action of
R
in
B
and the action of
R
in
E
.
The abelian group
\Hom_{R}(B,E)
is endowed with the
R
-module
structure induced by the right action of
R
in
B
.
The notation
\Ext^{*}_{R}(B,E)
is understood similarly.

Lemma 1.17
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring, let
B
be a quasi-module over
R
,
and let
G
and
E
be
R
-modules. In this context:

(a)
if the left
R
-module
B
is very flat and
the
R
-module
G
is very flat, then the left
R
-module
B\otimes_{R}G

is very flat;

(b)
if the left
R
-module
B
is very flat and
the
R
-module
E
is contraadjusted, then the
R
-module

\Hom_{R}(B,E)
is contraadjusted.

Proof.

This is a generalization of
Lemma
1.10
.
Notice that the condition that
B
is a quasi-module cannot be dropped:
the assertions of the lemma are
not
true for an arbitrary

R
-
R
-bimodule
B
.
See
[
55
, Example 6.2]
and
Example
1.18
below.

To prove part (b), we need to show that

\Ext^{1}_{R}(R[s^{-1}],\Hom_{R}(B,E))=0
for all
s\in R
.
One approach is to compute that

\Ext^{1}_{R}(R[s^{-1}],\Hom_{R}(B,E))\simeq\Ext^{1}_{R}(B\otimes_{R}R[s^{-1}],\>E)\\
\simeq\Ext^{1}_{R}(R[s^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B,\>E)\simeq\Ext^{1}_{R}(B,\Hom_{R}(R[s^{-1}],E))=0

using Lemma
1.1
twice,
Lemma
1.13
(a),
and Lemma
1.10
(b).

Alternatively, here is a more elementary argument in
the spirit of the proof of Lemma
1.5
.
Let
0\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow P\longrightarrow R[s^{-1}]\longrightarrow 0
be a two-term
projective resolution of the
R
-module
R[s^{-1}]
, as
in
[
49
, proof of Lemma 2.1]
.
We have to show that the map
\Hom_{R}(P,\Hom_{R}(B,E))\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(Q,\Hom_{R}(B,E))
is surjective.
The latter maps is isomorphic to the map
\Hom_{R}(B\otimes_{R}P,\>E)\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(B\otimes_{R}Q,\>E)
.
Now
0\longrightarrow B\otimes_{R}Q\longrightarrow B\otimes_{R}P\longrightarrow B\otimes_{R}R[s^{-1}]\longrightarrow 0

is a two-term resolution of the
R
-module
B\otimes_{R}R[s^{-1}]
by
very flat
R
-modules
B\otimes_{R}P
and
B\otimes_{R}Q
(in the left

R
-module structures).
It remains to point out that
B\otimes_{R}R[s^{-1}]\simeq R[s^{-1}]\otimes_{R}B

is a very flat
R
-module (in the left
R
-module structure) by
Lemmas
1.13
(a)
and
1.10
(a).

To deduce part (a), we need to show that
\Ext^{1}_{R}(B\otimes_{R}G,\>E)=0

for any contraadjusted
R
-module
E
.
Once again,

\Ext^{1}_{R}(B\otimes_{R}G,\>E)\simeq\Ext^{1}_{R}(G,\Hom_{R}(B,E))=0

by Lemma
1.1
and part (b) of the present
lemma.
Alternatively, one can use
Lemmas
1.13
(a)
and
1.10
(a) together with the description of
the
R
-module
G
as a direct summand of a transfinitely iterated
extension of the
R
-modules
R[s^{-1}]
,
s\in R
(as mentioned in
Section
1.3
).
∎

Example 1.18
.

Let us present an explicit counterexample showing that
Lemma
1.17
it
not
true for
an arbitrary
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
.
As one of the proofs of Lemma
1.17
(a)
deduces it from Lemma
1.17
(b),
any refutation of part (a) of the lemma with the quasi-module
assumption on
B
dropped would also refute part (b) without
the quasi-module assumption on
B
.

According to
[
55
, Example 6.2]
(based
on
[
57
, Example 9.7]
), there exists a pair of commutative
rings
R
and
S
, an
R
-very flat
R
-
S
-bimodule
F
, and
a very flat
S
-module
G
such that the tensor product
F\otimes_{S}G

is not very flat as a left
R
-module.
Let us consider the Cartesian product ring
T=R\times S
, and view

F
as a
T
-
T
-bimodule with the left and right
T
-module
structures induced from the
R
-module and
S
-module structures
via the projection morphisms of rings
T\longrightarrow R
and
T\longrightarrow S
.
Similarly, let us consider
G
as a
T
-module.
Notice that an
R
-module (or an
S
-module) is very flat if and
only if it is a very flat as a
T
-module.
Therefore, in the situation at hand
F
is very flat as a left

T
-module and
G
is very flat as a
T
-module, but the
T
-module

F\otimes_{T}G=F\otimes_{S}G
is
not
very flat.

1.5.
Quasi-algebras

Let
R
be a commutative ring.
A
quasi-algebra

A
over
R
is an associative, unital ring
endowed with a ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow A
such that, viewed as
an
R
-
R
-bimodule with respect to the induced
R
-
R
-bimodule
structure,
A
becomes a quasi-module over
R
.
Alternatively, a quasi-algebra over
R
is the same thing as a monoid
object in the monoidal category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
defined in
Section
1.4
.
So a quasi-algebra
A
over
R
is a quasi-module endowed with

R
-
R
-bimodule morphisms of
multiplication

A\otimes_{R}A\longrightarrow A

and
unit

R\longrightarrow A
satisfying the usual associativity
and unitality axioms.

Example 1.19
.

Let
K\longrightarrow R
be a homomorphism of commutative rings and
U
be
an
R
-module.
Consider the
K
-algebra
E=\Hom_{K}(U,U)
of all
K
-linear maps

U\longrightarrow U
.
Then the action of
R
in itself provides a
K
-algebra homomorphism

R\longrightarrow E
.
In particular,
E
is an
R
-
R
-bimodule.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{R/K}(U,U)=\bigcap_{r\in R}\bigcup_{n\geq 0}F^{(r)}_{n}E\subset E

be the maximal
R
-
R
-subbimodule of
E
that is a quasi-module
over
R
.
By
[
56
, Corollary 3.10 and the subsequent paragraph]
,

\mathcal{D}_{R/K}(U,U)
is a
K
-subalgebra in
E
(obviously, containing
the image of
R
in
E
, which is even contained in the
K
-subalgebra

\Hom_{R}(U,U)=\bigcap_{r\in R}F^{(r)}_{0}E
of all
R
-linear maps

U\longrightarrow U
).
Thus
\mathcal{D}_{R/K}(U,U)
is a quasi-algebra over
R
.
We call the quasi-algebra
\mathcal{D}_{R/K}(U,U)
the
quasi-algebra
of
K
-linear
R
-differential operators
U\longrightarrow U
.
These are the
R
-differential operators as defined
in
[
56
, Section 3.3]
, i. e., in the most general sense among
the three notions of a differential operator discussed
in
[
56
, Section 3]
.

The natural
K
-subalgebras
\mathcal{D}_{R/K}^{\mathsf{st}}(U,U)\subset\mathcal{D}_{R/K}^{\mathsf{qu}}(U,U)\subset\mathcal{D}_{R/K}(U,U)
constructed
as
[
56
, the paragraphs after Corollaries 3.2 and 3.7]
,
consisting of
K
-linear
strongly
and
quite

R
-differential operators
U\longrightarrow U
, are, of course, also
quasi-algebras over
R
.
(Cf. the discussion in
Sections
A.1
–
A.2
below.)

Lemma 1.20
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
. In this context:

(a)
The tensor product
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq S\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{R}S\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
(as per Lemma
1.14
)
has a unique structure of associative ring for which the natural map

S\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
is a ring homomorphism inducing the given

S
-
S
-bimodule structure on
S\otimes_{R}A
and
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A

is a ring homomorphism.
Endowed with this ring structure,
S\otimes_{R}A
becomes a quasi-algebra
over
S
.

(b)
For any left
A
-module
M
, the left
S
-module of
tensor product
S\otimes_{R}M
admits a unique extension of its
S
-module
structure to a structure of left module over
S\otimes_{R}A
such that

M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an
A
-module morphism.

(c)
For any left
A
-module
P
, the left
S
-module of
homomorphisms
\Hom_{R}(S,P)
admits a unique extension of its

S
-module structure to a structure of left module over
S\otimes_{R}A

such that
\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow P
is an
A
-module morphism.

Proof.

Part (a): the uniqueness holds because an arbitrary
S
-
S
-bimodule
map
(S\otimes_{R}\penalty A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(A\otimes_{R}\penalty S)\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{R}S

is determined by its composition with the map
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A\longrightarrow(S\otimes_{R}\penalty A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(A\otimes_{R}\penalty S)
.
To prove existence, one observes that the functor
B\longmapsto S\otimes_{R}B\simeq S\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}S\simeq B\otimes_{R}S
is a monoidal functor
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
(as one can easily check); so it takes monoid objects to
monoid objects.

Part (b): the uniqueness holds because an arbitrary
S
-module map

(S\otimes_{R}A)\otimes_{S}(S\otimes_{R}M)\simeq S\otimes_{R}(A\otimes_{R}M)\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is
determined by its composition with the map
A\otimes_{R}M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}(A\otimes_{R}M)
.
To prove existence, one can say that, conversely, the composition

(S\otimes_{R}A)\otimes_{S}(S\otimes_{R}M)\simeq S\otimes_{R}(A\otimes_{R}M)\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
, with
the map
S\otimes_{R}(A\otimes_{R}M)\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
induced by the left action map

A\otimes_{R}M\longrightarrow M
, defines the desired module structure on
S\otimes_{R}M
.
Alternatively, to establish the existence it suffices to point out
the natural isomorphism
(S\otimes_{R}A)\otimes_{A}M\simeq S\otimes_{R}M
, which allows
to define the desired
(S\otimes_{R}\penalty A)
-module
S\otimes_{R}M
as
the extension of scalars of the
A
-module
M
with respect to the ring
homomorphism
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
.

Part (c): the uniqueness holds because an arbitrary
S
-module map

\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow\Hom_{S}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>\Hom_{R}(S,P))\simeq\Hom_{R}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>P)\simeq\Hom_{R}(S,\Hom_{R}(A,P))
is determined by its
composition with the map
\Hom_{R}(S,\Hom_{R}(A,P))\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(A,P)
.
To prove existence, one can say that, conversely, the composition

\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(S,\Hom_{R}(A,P))\simeq\Hom_{R}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>P)\simeq\Hom_{S}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>\Hom_{R}(S,P))
, with the map
\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(S,\allowbreak\Hom_{R}(A,P))
induced by the left action
map
P\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(A,P)
, defines the desired module structure
on
\Hom_{R}(S,P)
.
Alternatively, to establish the existence it suffices to point out
the natural isomorphism
\Hom_{A}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>P)\simeq\Hom_{R}(S,P)
, which
allows to define the desired
(S\otimes_{R}\penalty A)
-module
\Hom_{R}(S,P)

as the coextension of scalars of the
A
-module
P
with respect to
the ring homomorphism
A\longrightarrow A\otimes_{R}S
. ∎

Lemma 1.21
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
for any left
A
-module
M
and any right module
N

over
S\otimes_{R}A
, the map of tensor products
N\otimes_{A}M\longrightarrow N\otimes_{S\otimes_{R}A}(S\otimes_{R}M)
induced by the ring and module maps

A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an isomorphism;

(b)
for any left
A
-module
M
and any left module
Q

over
S\otimes_{R}A
, the map of Hom modules
\Hom_{S\otimes_{R}A}(S\otimes_{R}M,\>Q)\longrightarrow\Hom_{A}(M,Q)
induced by the ring and module maps
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an isomorphism;

(c)
for any left module
N
over
S\otimes_{R}A
and any left

A
-module
P
, the map of Hom modules
\Hom_{S\otimes_{R}A}(N,\Hom_{R}(S,P))\longrightarrow\Hom_{A}(N,P)
induced by the ring and module maps
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow P
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

All the assertions follow from the alternative proofs of
Lemma
1.20
(b–c).
∎

Corollary 1.22
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
the ring
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
is flat as a left and
right module over
A
;

(b)
for any flat left
A
-module
F
, the left module

S\otimes_{R}F
is flat over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(c)
for any projective left
A
-module
F
, the left module

S\otimes_{R}F
is projective over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(d)
for any cotorsion left
A
-module
C
, the left module

\Hom_{R}(S,C)
is cotorsion over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(e)
for any injective left
A
-module
J
, the left module

\Hom_{R}(S,J)
is injective over
S\otimes_{R}A
.

Proof.

Part (a) holds since
S
is a flat
R
-module.
Parts (b–c) and (e) follow either from the respective parts of
Lemma
1.21
, or directly
from the alternative proofs of
Lemma
1.20
(b–c).
In the latter approach, in part (e),
Lemma
1.6
(d) is
relevant.
In part (d), one has to use part (a), which implies that any flat
module over
S\otimes_{R}A
is also flat as an
A
-module.
One can also refer to
Lemma
1.6
(c).
∎

Given a ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow A
with a commutative ring
R
,
an
A
-module is said to be
R
-contraadjusted
if it is
contraadjusted as an
R
-module.

R
-cotorsion
A
-modules
and
R
-injective

A
-modules
(for an arbitrary homomorphism of associative rings

R\longrightarrow A
) are defined similarly.

Lemma 1.23
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, be a finite collection
of homomorphisms of commutative rings such that the collection of
induced maps of the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an affine open covering of an affine scheme.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
a left
A
-module
F
is flat if and only if the left
module
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}F
is flat over
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
for all
indices
\alpha
;

(b)
an
R
-contraadjusted left
A
-module
C
is cotorsion
over
A
if and only if the left module
\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha},C)
is
cotorsion over
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
for all indices
\alpha
;

(c)
an
R
-contraadjusted left
A
-module
J
is injective
over
A
if and only if the left module
\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha},J)
is
injective over
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
for all indices
\alpha
.

Proof.

Part (a): the “only if” assertion holds by
Corollary
1.22
(b).
To prove the “if”, consider the Čech coresolution

(5)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}F\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muS_{1}\otimes_{R}S_{2}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{N}\otimes_{R}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

(cf.
[
46
, formula (1.4) in the proof of Lemma 1.2.6(a)]
).
The complex (
5
) is
an exact sequence of left
A
-modules (see
Lemma
1.20
(b)).
For any subsequence of indices
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\dotsb<\alpha_{k}\leq N
,

k\geq 1
, the tensor product
S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}F
is a flat left module over the ring
S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}A
by
Corollary
1.22
(b).
Since
S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}A
is a flat left

A
-module (see
Corollary
1.22
(a)), it
follows that
S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}F
is a flat
left
A
-module.
Now the assertion that
F
is a flat left
A
-module is provable
by moving along the exact
sequence (
5
) from
its right end to the left one and using the fact that the class of flat

A
-modules is closed under the kernels of epimorphisms.

Part (b): the “only if” assertion holds by
Corollary
1.22
(d).
To prove the “if”, consider the Čech resolution

(6)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{R}(S_{1}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{N},\>C)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha<\beta}\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta},\>C)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha}\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha},C)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

as per
[
46
, formula (1.3) in Lemma 1.2.6(b)]
.
The complex (
6
) is
an exact sequence of left
A
-modules (see
Lemma
1.20
(c)).
For any subsequence of indices
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\dotsb<\alpha_{k}\leq N
,

k\geq 1
, the Hom module
\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}},\>C)
is a cotorsion left module over the ring

S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}A
by
Corollary
1.22
(d).
By Lemma
1.6
(a),
it follows that
\Hom_{R}(S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}},\>C)

is a cotorsion left
A
-module.
Now the assertion that
C
is a cotorsion left
A
-module is provable
by moving along the exact
sequence (
6
) from
its left end to the right one and using the fact that the class of
cotorsion
A
-modules is closed under cokernels of monomorphisms
(Lemma
1.2
(b)).

The proof of part (c) is similar to that of part (b) and also based
on the Čech
resolution (
6
).
One has to use
Corollary
1.22
(a,e)
and Lemma
1.6
(b).
∎

Question 1.24
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the induced morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion (e. g.,
S=R[r^{-1}]
, where
r\in R
is an element).

Let
M
be a left
A
-module.
Then, by Lemma
1.20
(a–b),

S\otimes_{R}A
is a quasi-algebra over
S
and
S\otimes_{R}M
is a left module
over
S\otimes_{R}A
.
By Corollary
1.22
(a–b),
if
F
is a flat left
A
-module, then
S\otimes_{R}F
is a flat left
module over
S\otimes_{R}A
, and consequently also a flat left
A
-module.

Now let
E
be an associative ring and
E\longrightarrow A
be a homomorphism
of associative rings.
Let
M
be a left
A
-module that is flat as a module over
E
.
Does it follow that the left
A
-module
S\otimes_{R}M
is flat as a module
over
E
?

Nevertheless, the following lemma is obvious.

Lemma 1.25
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
E
be an associative ring and
E\longrightarrow A
be a ring homomorphism.
Assume that the ring
A
is flat as a left
E
-module.
Then the ring
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
is also flat as a left

E
-module.

Proof.

Follows immediately from
Corollary
1.22
(a).
∎

Lemma 1.26
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, be a finite collection
of homomorphisms of commutative rings such that the collection of
induced maps of the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is an
affine open covering of an affine scheme.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
, and let
E\longrightarrow A
be a ring
homomorphism.
Then the left
E
-module
A
is flat if and only if the left

E
-module
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A\simeq A\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha}
is flat for
every
\alpha
.

Proof.

The “only if” assertion holds by
Lemma
1.25
.
To prove the “if”, consider the Čech
coresolution (
5
)
for
F=A
,

(7)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muA\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}A\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muS_{1}\otimes_{R}S_{2}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{N}\otimes_{R}A\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

The complex (
7
) is
an exact sequence of
A
-
A
-bimodules, hence also an exact
sequence of left
E
-modules.
For any subsequence of indices
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\dotsb<\alpha_{k}\leq N
,

k\geq 1
, the tensor product
S_{\alpha_{1}}\otimes_{R}\dotsb\otimes_{R}S_{\alpha_{k}}\otimes_{R}A
is a flat left
E
-module by
Lemma
1.25
.
The argument finishes similarly to the proof of
Lemma
1.23
(a).
∎

1.6.
Flaprojective and very flaprojective modules

Let
R\longrightarrow A
be a homomorphism of associative rings.
The definitions of
R
-cotorsion
A
-modules and (if
R
is
commutative)
R
-contraadjusted
A
-modules were given in
the previous Section
1.5
.

We will say that a left
A
-module
F
is
flaprojective relative
to
R
(or
A/R
-flaprojective
) if
\Ext^{1}_{A}(F,C)=0
for
all
R
-cotorsion left
A
-modules
C
.
If the ring
R
is commutative, we will say that a left
A
-module
F

is
very flaprojective relative to
R
(or
A/R
-very
flaprojective
) if
\Ext^{1}_{A}(F,C)=0
for all
R
-contraadjusted left

A
-modules
C
.
Clearly, the classes of
A/R
-flaprojective and
A/R
-very
flaprojective
A
-modules are closed under extensions, direct summands,
and infinite direct sums.

Examples 1.27
.

(1) If
A=R
and
R\longrightarrow A
is the identity map, then the class
of
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules modules coincides with the class
of flat
R
-modules.
The class of
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules coincides with
the class of very flat
R
-modules in this case.

(2) If
R=k
is a field, then the both the classes of

A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules and
A/R
-very flaprojective

A
-modules coincide with the class of projective
A
-modules.

Remark 1.28
.

Explaining the terminology “flaprojective”, one can say informally,
based on Examples
1.27
,
that the
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules are “flat along
R
” and
“projective in the direction of
A
relative to
R
”.
Similarly, the
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules are
“very flat along
R
” and “projective in the direction of
A

relative to
R
”.

The informal explanation above should be taken with caution.
For example, all projective
A
-modules are obviously

A/R
-flaprojective by the definition (and even
A/R
-very
flaprojective, if
R
is commutative).
Still, the projective
A
-module
A
need not be flat as
an
R
-module in general.

On the other hand, all cotorsion
A
-modules are also cotorsion
as
R
-modules by
Lemma
1.6
(a) (hence
also contraadjusted as
R
-modules if
R
is commutative).
Therefore, Lemma
1.3
implies
that all
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules are flat as
A
-modules.
In particular, if
R
is commutative, then all

A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules are flat as
A
-modules.
If the left
R
-module
A
is flat, it follows that all

A/R
-(very) flaprojective
A
-modules are flat as
R
-modules.

Lemma 1.29
.

(a)
One has
\Ext^{n}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all
A/R
-flaprojective

R
-modules
F
, all
R
-cotorsion
A
-modules
C
, and all
n\geq 1
.

(b)
The class of
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules is closed
under kernels of epimorphisms in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Proof.

Part (a): the point is that all injective
A
-modules are cotorsion
by definition, hence also
R
-cotorsion by
Lemma
1.6
(a).
Furthermore, the class of
R
-cotorsion
A
-modules is closed
under cokernels of monomorphisms in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
by
Lemma
1.2
(b).
With these observations in mind, the assertion of part (a) follows
by applying
[
61
, Lemma 7.1]
.
Part (b) follows immediately from part (a).
∎

Lemma 1.30
.

Assume that the ring
R
is commutative.

(a)
One has
\Ext^{n}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all
A/R
-very
flaprojective
R
-modules
F
, all
R
-contraadjusted

A
-modules
C
, and all
n\geq 1
.

(b)
The class of
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules is
closed under kernels of epimorphisms in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
Lemma
1.29
.
Lemma
1.7
(a) is relevant.
∎

Lemma 1.31
.

(a)
A left
A
-module
C
is
R
-cotorsion if and only if

\Ext_{R}^{1}(F,C)=0
for all
A/R
-flaprojective left
A
-modules
F
.

(b)
Assuming that the ring
R
is commutative, a left

A
-module
C
is
R
-contraadjusted if and only if

\Ext_{R}^{1}(F,C)=0
for all
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules
F
.

Proof.

The “only if” assertions in (a) and (b) hold by the definition.
To prove the “if” implications, one argues as follows.
For any flat left
R
-module
F
and any left
A
-module
C
,
the natural isomorphism
\Ext^{n}_{A}(A\otimes_{R}F,\>C)\simeq\Ext^{n}_{R}(F,C)

holds for all
n\geq 0
by Lemma
1.1

(see also
[
57
, Lemma 4.1(a)]
or
[
54
, Lemma 1.7(e)]
).
Therefore, for any flat left
R
-module
F
, the left
A
-module

A\otimes_{R}F
is
A/R
-flaprojective; and for any very flat
R
-module

F
, the left
A
-module
A\otimes_{R}F
is
A/R
-very flaprojective.
Now the Ext isomorphism above implies that a left
A
-module
C
is

R
-cotorsion if and only if
\Ext^{1}_{A}(A\otimes_{R}F,\>C)=0
for all
flat left
R
-modules
F
, and
C
is
R
-contraadjusted if and only
if
\Ext^{1}_{A}(A\otimes_{R}F,\>C)=0
for all very flat
R
-modules
F
.
∎

Remark 1.32
.

In the terminology of
[
68
,
20
,
25
]
, the proof of
Lemma
1.31
tells us that
the pair of classes (
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-modules,
R
-cotorsion

A
-modules) is the cotorsion pair in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
generated by the class
of all modules of the form
A\otimes_{R}F
, where
F
ranges over flat
left
R
-modules.
Similarly, the pair of classes (
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-modules,

R
-contraadjusted
A
-modules) is the cotorsion pair in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}

generated by the class of all modules of the form
A\otimes_{R}F
, where

F
ranges over very flat
R
-modules.
As both the classes of flat and very flat
R
-modules are
deconstructible (in the case of flat modules, this is
[
11
, Lemma 1
and Proposition 2]
), both the cotorsion pairs are actually
generated by suitable subsets of the mentioned two classes of modules.
The next Theorems
1.33

and
1.34
follow
immediately from these observations.
It also follows that the classes of
A/R
-(very) flaprojective

A
-modules can be described as the classes of all direct summands
of transfinitely iterated extensions (in the sense of the inductive
limit) of the
A
-modules
A\otimes_{R}F
, where the
R
-module
F
is
(very) flat
[
20
, Theorems 2 and 10]
,
[
25
, Corollary 6.14]
.
See also
[
51
, Proposition 3.3]
for a discussion much
more specific than in
[
20
,
25
]
, still considerably more general
than in the present section.

Theorem 1.33
.

For any
A
-module
M
, there exist

(a)
a short exact sequence of
A
-modules

0\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0
with an
A/R
-flaprojective

A
-module
F
and an
R
-cotorsion
A
-module
C^{\prime}
;

(b)
a short exact sequence of
A
-modules

0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
with an
R
-cotorsion

A
-module
C
and an
A/R
-flaprojective
A
-module
F^{\prime}
.

Theorem 1.34
.

Assume that the ring
R
is commutative.
Then, for any
A
-module
M
, there exist

(a)
a short exact sequence of
A
-modules

0\longrightarrow C^{\prime}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0
with an
A/R
-very
flaprojective
A
-module
F
and an
R
-contraadjusted

A
-module
C^{\prime}
;

(b)
a short exact sequence of
A
-modules

0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow F^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
with an
R
-contraadjusted

A
-module
C
and an
A/R
-very flaprojective
A
-module
F^{\prime}
.

Proofs of
Theorems
1.33

and
1.34
.

In view of the discussion in
Remark
1.32
,
these are two special cases of
[
20
, Theorems 2 and 10]

or
[
25
, Theorem 6.11]
.
∎

Lemma 1.35
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
and
B=S\otimes_{R}A=A\otimes_{R}S
be
the related quasi-algebra over
S
, as per
Lemma
1.20
(a).
In this context:

(a)
any
B/S
-flaprojective
B
-module is also

A/R
-flaprojective as an
A
-module;

(b)
if
F
is an
A/R
-flaprojective left
A
-module,
then
S\otimes_{R}F
is a
B/S
-flaprojective left
B
-module.

Proof.

Part (a): given an
R
-cotorsion left
A
-module
C
, the related
left
B
-module
\Hom_{A}(B,C)=\Hom_{R}(S,C)
is
S
-cotorsion by
Lemma
1.6
(c).
Furthermore, one has
\Ext^{n}_{A}(B,C)=\Ext^{n}_{A}(A\otimes_{R}S,\>C)\simeq\Ext^{n}_{R}(S,C)=0
for all
n\geq 1
by Lemma
1.1

(see also
[
57
, Lemma 4.1(a)]
).
Consequently, the natural isomorphism
\Ext^{n}_{A}(G,C)\simeq\Ext^{n}_{B}(G,\Hom_{A}(B,C))
holds for all left
A
-modules
G
and all

n\geq 0
by Lemma
1.1
(see
also
[
54
, Lemma 1.7(e)]
).
Now it is clear that
G
is
A/R
-flaprojective whenever it is

B/S
-flaprojective.

Part (b): recall the isomorphism
S\otimes_{R}F\simeq B\otimes_{A}F
from
the alternative proof of
Lemma
1.20
(b).
Now for any left
B
-module
D
, one has
\Ext^{n}_{B}(B\otimes_{A}F,\>D)\simeq\Ext_{A}^{n}(F,D)
for all
n\geq 1
by Lemma
1.1

(see also
[
57
, Lemma 4.1(a)]
).
It remains to point out that any
S
-cotorsion left
B
-module
D

is also
R
-cotorsion as an
A
-module by
Lemma
1.6
(a).
∎

Lemma 1.36
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a morphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R

is an open immersion.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
, and
B=S\otimes_{R}A=A\otimes_{R}S
be
the related quasi-algebra over
S
as per
Lemma
1.20
(a).
In this context:

(a)
any
B/S
-very flaprojective
B
-module is also

A/R
-very flaprojective as an
A
-module;

(b)
if
F
is an
A/R
-very flaprojective left
A
-module,
then
S\otimes_{R}F
is a very
B/S
-flaprojective left
B
-module.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
Lemma
1.35
.
One needs to use Lemma
1.11
(d)
with Example
1.9
(1) in part (a) and
Lemma
1.11
(a) in part (b).
∎

Lemma 1.37
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
be a finite collection of homomorphisms of
commutative rings such that the collection of induced maps of
the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is an affine open covering
of an affine scheme.
Let
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
a left
A
-module
F
is
A/R
-flaprojective if and only
if the left module
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}F
over the ring
B_{\alpha}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
is
B_{\alpha}/S_{\alpha}
-flaprojective for all
indices
\alpha
;

(b)
a left
A
-module
F
is
A/R
-very flaprojective if and
only if the left module
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}F
over the ring
B_{\alpha}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
is
B_{\alpha}/S_{\alpha}
-very flaprojective for all
indices
\alpha
.

Proof.

The argument from the proof of
Lemma
1.23
(a), based on
the Čech
coresolution (
5
),
is applicable.
In part (a), one needs to use
Lemmas
1.35
(a–b)
and
1.29
(b).
For part (b), use
Lemmas
1.36
(a–b)
and
1.30
(b).
∎

1.7.
Robustly flaprojective bimodules

This section is a bit sketchy.
It sheds some light on the exposition below in this paper, but
using its results can be avoided (as we will point out).

Let
R\longrightarrow A
be a homomorphism of associative rings and
K
be
an associative ring.
We will say that an
A
-
K
-bimodule
F
is
robustly
flaprojective relative to
R
(or
(A/R,K)
-robustly
flaprojective
) if the left
A
-module
F\otimes_{K}H
is

A/R
-flaprojective for every flat left
K
-module
H
.
The class of
(A/R,K)
-robustly flaprojective
A
-
K
-bimodules
is closed under transfinitely iterated extensions and kernels of
epimorphisms (since so is the class of
A/R
-flaprojective
left
A
-modules).

Example 1.38
.

For any
R
-
K
-bimodule
U
that is flat as a left
R
-module,
the
A
-
K
-bimodule
A\otimes_{R}U
is
(A/R,K)
-robustly
flaprojective.
Indeed, one has
(A\otimes_{R}\penalty U)\otimes_{K}H\simeq A\otimes_{R}(U\otimes_{K}H)
,
and
U\otimes_{K}H
is a flat left
R
-module (cf. Remark
1.32
).

Lemma 1.39
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
A
and
K
be quasi-algebras over
R
, and
B=S\otimes_{R}A
and

L=S\otimes_{R}K
be the related quasi-algebras over
S
.
In this context:

(a)
Let
G
be a
(B/S,L)
-robustly flaprojective

B
-
L
-bimodule.
Then
G
is also
(A/R,K)
-robustly flaprojective as
an
A
-
K
-bimodule.

(b)
Let
F
be an
A
-
K
-bimodule whose underlying

R
-
R
-bimodule is a quasi-module over
R
.
Assume that the
A
-
K
-bimodule
F
is
(A/R,K)
-robustly
flaprojective.
Then the
B
-
L
-bimodule
S\otimes_{R}F\simeq S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{R}S\simeq F\otimes_{R}S
is
(B/S,L)
-robustly flaprojective.

Proof.

Part (a): given a flat left
K
-module
H
, the left
L
-module

S\otimes_{R}H\simeq L\otimes_{K}H
is flat by
Corollary
1.22
(b).
Now the left
B
-module
G\otimes_{K}H\simeq G\otimes_{L}(L\otimes_{K}H)
is

B/S
-flaprojective by assumption.
By Lemma
1.35
(a),
it follows that
G\otimes_{K}H
is also
A/R
-flaprojective as
a left
A
-module.

Part (b): any flat left
L
-module
H
is also flat as a left

K
-module by
Corollary
1.22
(a).
Now we have
(S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{R}S)\otimes_{L}H\simeq(S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{K}L)\otimes_{L}H\simeq S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{K}H
.
The left
A
-module
F\otimes_{K}H
is
A/R
-flaprojective by assumption.
By Lemma
1.35
(b),
it follows that
S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{K}H
is
B/S
-flaprojective as
a left
B
-module.
∎

Lemma 1.40
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
be a finite collection of homomorphisms of
commutative rings such that the collection of induced maps of
the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is an affine open covering
of an affine scheme.
Let
A
and
K
be quasi-algebras over
R
, and let
F
be
an
A
-
K
-bimodule whose underlying
R
-
R
-bimodule is
a quasi-module over
R
.
Let
B_{\alpha}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A
and
L_{\alpha}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}K
be
the related rings.
Then the
A
-
K
-bimodule
F
is
(A/R,K)
-robustly flaprojective
if and only if the
B_{\alpha}
-
L_{\alpha}
-bimodule
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}F

is
(B_{\alpha}/S_{\alpha},L_{\alpha})
-robustly flaprojective for
all indices
\alpha
.

Proof.

This is similar to
Lemmas
1.23
(a)
and
1.37
, and based on
Lemma
1.39
(a-b).
The fact that the class of
(A/R,K)
-robustly flaprojective modules
is closed under kernels of epimorphisms has to be used.
∎

Let us emphasize that part (a) of the next lemma assumes, somewhat
counterintuitively, that
H
is a
very
flat
R
-module.
See Section
A.4
for an additional
discussion.
Part (b) of the same lemma explains why did not define “robustly
very flaprojective bimodules”.
Abusing the terminology, one can say that
all very flaprojective
bimodules are robustly very flaprojective
(in the setting we are
interested in).

Lemma 1.41
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring,
A
be a quasi-algebra over
R
, and

F
be an
A
-
R
-bimodule whose underlying
R
-
R
-bimodule is
a quasi-module over
R
.
In this context:

(a)
if the left
A
-module
F
is
A/R
-flaprojective and

H
is a very flat
R
-module, then the left
A
-module
F\otimes_{R}H

is
A/R
-flaprojective;

(b)
if the left
A
-module
F
is
A/R
-very flaprojective
and
H
is a very flat
R
-module, then the left
A
-module

F\otimes_{R}H
is
A/R
-very flaprojective.

Proof.

In part (a), one observes that the class of
A/R
-flaprojective
left
A
-modules is closed under transfinitely iterated extensions
(by Remark
1.32
)
and direct summands.
Therefore, it suffices to consider the case of
H=R[r^{-1}]
, where

r\in R
is an element.
In this case, one has
F\otimes_{R}R[r^{-1}]\simeq R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}F
by
Lemma
1.13
(a), and
the left
A
-module
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}F
is
A/R
-flaprojective by
Lemma
1.35
(a–b).
The proof of part (b) is similar and based on
Lemma
1.36
(a–b).
∎

2.
Contraherent Cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-Modules

There are no new results is this section, which is mostly
an extraction from
[
46
, Chapters 1–3]
.
The preprint
[
55
]
can be used as a supplementary reference.

2.1.
Cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

In this section we mostly follow
[
46
, Section 2.1]
.

Let
X
be a topological space.
Recall that a
presheaf of abelian groups

\mathcal{M}
on
X
is
a contravariant functor from the category of open subsets in
X

(with identity inclusions as morphisms) to the category of
abelian groups
\mathsf{Ab}
.
So, to every open subset
U\subset X
, a presheaf
\mathcal{M}
assigns
an abelian group
\mathcal{M}(U)
of
sections of
\mathcal{M}
over
U
;
and to every pair of open subsets
V\subset U\subset X
, it assigns
the
restriction map

\mathcal{M}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(V)
, which must be
an abelian group homomorphism.

A presheaf of abelian groups
\mathcal{M}
on
X
is called a
sheaf
if
it satisfies the following
sheaf axiom
.
For every open covering
U=\bigcup_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}
of an open
subset
U\subset X
, the short sequence of abelian groups

(8)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{M}(U)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{\alpha}\mathcal{M}(V_{\alpha})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{\alpha,\beta}\mathcal{M}(V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta})

must be left exact.

Dual-analogously, a
copresheaf of abelian groups

\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is
a covariant functor from the category of open subsets in
X
to
\mathsf{Ab}
.
To every open subset
U\subset X
, a copresheaf
\mathfrak{P}
assigns
an abelian group
\mathfrak{P}[U]
of
cosections of
\mathfrak{P}
over
U
;
and to every pair of open subsets
V\subset U\subset X
, it assigns
the
corestriction map

\mathfrak{P}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]
, which must be
an abelian group homomorphism.

A copresheaf of abelian groups
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is called a
cosheaf

if it satisfies the following
cosheaf axiom
.
For every open covering
U=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of an open subset

U\subset X
, the short sequence of abelian groups

(9)

0\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\mathfrak{P}[U]\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\coprod\nolimits_{\alpha}\mathfrak{P}[V_{\alpha}]\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\coprod\nolimits_{\alpha,\beta}\mathfrak{P}[V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}]

must be right exact.

Now let
(X,\mathcal{O})
be a ringed space.
So
\mathcal{O}
is a sheaf of (associative and unital, not necessarily
commutative) rings on
X
.
In the rest of this Section
2.1
, all

modules
will be presumed to be left modules.

A
presheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

\mathcal{M}
on
X
is a presheaf of
abelian groups together with an additional structure of
an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module on every group of sections
\mathcal{M}(U)
such that
the restriction maps
\mathcal{M}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(V)
are
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module morphisms.
Here the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module structure on
\mathcal{M}(V)
is defined using
the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(V)
in the sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}
.

A presheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X
is called a
sheaf of

\mathcal{O}
-modules
if its underlying presheaf of abelian groups is
a sheaf of abelian groups.
So the sequence (
8
) is a left exact sequence of

\mathcal{O}(U)
-modules in this case.
We will denote the category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X

(with the obvious morphisms) by
(X,\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
.

Dual-analogously, a
copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}
