-modules

\mathfrak{P}
on
X

is a copresheaf of abelian groups together with an additional structure
of an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module on the group of cosections
\mathfrak{P}[U]
over every
open subset
U\subset X
such that, for every pair of open subsets

V\subset U\subset X
, the corestriction map
\mathfrak{P}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]
is
an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module morphism.
Here the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{P}[V]
is defined in terms of
the
\mathcal{O}(V)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{P}[V]
and the ring homomorphism

\mathcal{O}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(V)
of restriction of sections in the sheaf of
rings
\mathcal{O}
.

A copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X
is called a
cosheaf of

\mathcal{O}
-modules
if its underlying copresheaf of abelian groups is
a cosheaf of abelian groups.
So the sequence (
9
) is a right exact sequence of

\mathcal{O}(U)
-modules in this case.
We will denote the category of cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X

(with the obvious morphisms) by
(X,\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Now let
\mathbf{B}
be a base of open subsets in
X
.
The aim of the subsequent paragraphs is to generalize the discussion
above from the case of the base of
all
open subsets in
X
to
the case of arbitrary topology base
\mathbf{B}
.

We will view
\mathbf{B}
as a category with identity inclusion maps as
morphisms.
Then a
presheaf of abelian groups

\mathcal{N}
on
\mathbf{B}
is simply defined
as a functor
\mathbf{B}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}
.
Once again, to every open subset
U\in\mathbf{B}
, a presheaf
\mathcal{N}
assigns
an abelian group
\mathcal{N}(U)
of
sections of
\mathcal{N}
over
U
; and to
every pair of open subsets
V\subset U
,
U
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
, it assigns
the
restriction map

\mathcal{N}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}(V)
.

A presheaf of abelian groups
\mathcal{N}
on
\mathbf{B}
is called a
sheaf
if
it satisfies the following version of the sheaf axiom.
For every open covering
U=\bigcup_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}
of an open subset

U\in\mathbf{B}
by open subsets
V_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{B}
, and every family of open
coverings
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}=\bigcup_{\gamma}W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}

of all the intersections
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
by open subsets

W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\in\mathbf{B}
, the short sequence of abelian groups

(10)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{N}(U)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}(V_{\alpha})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\mathcal{N}(W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})

must be left exact.

Dual-analogously, a
copresheaf of abelian groups

\mathfrak{Q}
on
\mathbf{B}

is a functor
\mathbf{B}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}
.
To every open subset
U\in\mathbf{B}
, a copresheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
assigns an abelian
group
\mathfrak{Q}[U]
of
cosections of
\mathfrak{Q}
over
U
; and to every
pair of open subsets
V\subset U
,
U
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
, it assigns
the
corestriction map

\mathfrak{Q}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}[U]
.

A copresheaf of abelian groups
\mathfrak{Q}
on
\mathbf{B}
is called a
cosheaf

if it satisfies the following version of the cosheaf axiom.
For every open covering
U=\bigcup_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}
of an open subset

U\in\mathbf{B}
by open subsets
V_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{B}
, and every family of open
coverings
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}=\bigcup_{\gamma}W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}

of all the intersections
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
by open subsets

W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\in\mathbf{B}
, the short sequence of abelian groups

(11)

0\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\mathfrak{Q}[U]\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\coprod\nolimits_{\alpha}\mathfrak{Q}[V_{\alpha}]\mskip 1.5mu\leftarrow\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\mskip 1.5mu\coprod\nolimits_{\alpha,\beta}\mathfrak{Q}[W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}]

must be right exact.

As above, let
(X,\mathcal{O})
be a ringed space.
A
presheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

\mathcal{N}
on
\mathbf{B}
is a presheaf of
abelian groups on
\mathbf{B}
such that the abelian group
\mathcal{N}(U)
is endowed
with an additional structure of an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module for every
U\in\mathbf{B}

and the restriction map
\mathcal{N}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}(V)
is an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module
morphism for all
V\subset U
,
U
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
.
A presheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
is called a
sheaf of

\mathcal{O}
-modules
on
\mathbf{B}
if its underlying presheaf of abelian groups
is a sheaf of abelian groups on
\mathbf{B}
.
We will denote the category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}

(with the obvious morphisms) by
(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
.

Dual-analogously, a
copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

\mathfrak{Q}
on
\mathbf{B}

is a copresheaf of abelian groups on
\mathbf{B}
such that the abelian group

\mathfrak{Q}[U]
is endowed with an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module structure for every

U\in\mathbf{B}
and the corestriction map
\mathfrak{Q}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}[U]
is
an
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module morphism for all
V\subset U
,
U
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
.
Here, as above, the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{Q}[V]
is defined
in terms of the
\mathcal{O}(V)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{Q}[V]
and
the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(V)
.
A copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
is called a
cosheaf of

\mathcal{O}
-modules
on
\mathbf{B}
if its underlying copresheaf of abelian groups
is a cosheaf of abelian groups on
\mathbf{B}
.
We will denote the category of cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}

(with the obvious morphisms) by
(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{O})
be a ringed space and
\mathbf{B}
be a topology base in
X
.
In this setting:

(a)
Any sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
can be extended to
a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X
(i. e., on all open subsets of
X
)
in a unique way.
The functor of restriction from all open subsets of
X
to the open
subsets belonging to
\mathbf{B}
is an equivalence of categories

(X,\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}\simeq(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}.

(b)
Any cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
can be extended to
a cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
X
(i. e., on all open subsets of
X
)
in a unique way.
The functor of restriction from all open subsets of
X
to the open
subsets belonging to
\mathbf{B}
is an equivalence of categories

(X,\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\simeq(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}.

Proof.

Part (a) is essentially
[
27
, Section 0.3.2]
; see
also
[
32
, Lemma Tag 009U]
.
Part (b) is
[
46
, Theorem 2.1.2]
.
∎

Given an open subset
Y\subset X
, denote by
\mathcal{O}|_{Y}
the restriction
of the sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}
on
X
to the open subset
Y
.
Then, for any sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules
\mathcal{M}
on
X
, the sheaf of

\mathcal{O}|_{Y}
-modules
\mathcal{M}|_{Y}
on
Y
is defined by the rule
\mathcal{M}|_{Y}(U)=\mathcal{M}(U)

for all open subsets
U\subset Y
.
For any cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
, the cosheaf of

\mathcal{O}|_{Y}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}
on
Y
is defined by the rule
\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}[U]=\mathfrak{P}[U]

for all open subsets
U\subset Y
.

2.2.
Locally contraherent cosheaves

This section is largely based on
[
46
, Sections 2.2 and 3.1]
.

Let
X
be a scheme with the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and a fixed open
covering
\mathbf{W}
.
We will say that an open subscheme
U\subset X
is
subordinate
to
\mathbf{W}
if there exists an open subscheme
W\in\mathbf{W}
such that

U\subset W
.
An open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
is said to be

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
if every open subscheme
U_{\alpha}

belonging to this covering is subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

Denote by
\mathbf{B}
the base of open subsets in
X
consisting of all
the affine open subschemes
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a presheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.
One says that the presheaf
\mathcal{M}
is
quasi-coherent
if
the following
quasi-coherence axiom
holds:

•

For every pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
-module map

\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{M}(V)

corresponding by adjunction to the restriction map
\mathcal{M}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}(V)

is an isomorphism.

Lemma 2.2
.

The quasi-coherence axiom implies the sheaf axiom: Any quasi-coherent
presheaf on
\mathbf{B}
is a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the case of an affine scheme
X=U
with
the open covering
\mathbf{W}=\{U\}
.
Then the point is that the rule
V\longmapsto\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)
defines the restriction to
\mathbf{B}
of
the quasi-coherent sheaf on
\Spec U
corresponding to
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathcal{M}(U)
.
Alternatively, one can observe that, in the context of the definition
of a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
, it suffices to check the sheaf
axiom for one’s favorite choice of the coverings
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}=\bigcup_{\gamma}W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
of the intersections

V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
, rather than for all such coverings (this is
a part of the formulation of
[
46
, Proposition 2.1.3]
).
In the situation at hand, we have
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}\in\mathbf{B}
, since
the intersections of affine open subschemes in an affine scheme
U

are affine.
So let
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
be its own covering.
Furthermore, as affine schemes are quasi-compact, it suffices to
consider
finite
affine open coverings of
U

only
[
46
, Remark 2.1.4]
.
This reduces the question to an exercise in commutative algebra, which
we leave to the reader (cf.
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.6]
).
∎

We denote the full subcategory of quasi-coherent (pre)sheaves on
\mathbf{B}

by
\mathbf{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\subset(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
.
The full subcategory of quasi-coherent sheaves in
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
is
denoted by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\subset(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
.

Corollary 2.3
.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{B}
be
the topology base of
X
formed by all the affine open subschemes
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
Then the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
is
equivalent to the category
\mathbf{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent presheaves
on
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

This result is essentially due to Enochs and
Estrada
[
23
, Section 2]
.
By Lemma
2.2
, any quasi-coherent
presheaf
\mathcal{M}
on
\mathbf{B}
is a sheaf on
\mathbf{B}
; so
Theorem
2.1
(a) tells us
that
\mathcal{M}
can be uniquely extended to a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
on
X
.
It remains to explain that the class of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
one obtains in this way coincides with the class of all quasi-coherent
sheaves on
X
.
In other words, a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
X
is quasi-coherent
if and only if it satisfies the quasi-coherence axiom above for all
pairs of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
any fixed open covering
\mathbf{W}
of the scheme
X
.
This is clear from the standard local definition of a quasi-coherent
sheaf on a ringed space
[
32
, Section Tag 01BD]
together with
the classical description of quasi-coherent sheaves on affine schemes
in terms of modules over the ring of global
functions
[
32
, Section Tag 01I6]
.
As a bonus, these arguments prove that the quasi-coherence condition is
local in the sense that the category
\mathbf{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent
presheaves on
\mathbf{B}
does not depend on the choice of an open covering

\mathbf{W}
of a scheme
X
.
∎

Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.
One says that the copresheaf
\mathfrak{P}
is
contraherent
(on
\mathbf{B}
) if
the following two axioms hold for every pair of affine open subschemes

V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
:

(i)

The
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
-module map

\mathfrak{P}[V]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])

corresponding by adjunction to the corestriction map
\mathfrak{P}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

One has

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])=0.

The condition (i) is called the
contraherence axiom
.
The condition (ii) is called the
contraadjustedness axiom
.

Concerning condition (ii), notice first of all that the projective
dimension of the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
never exceeds
1

[
55
, Remark 5.1]
.
Moreover, the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
is
very flat

(in the sense of Section
1.3
above);
see Example
1.9
(1).
On the other hand, all principal affine open subschemes in
U
occur
as the schemes
V
in the context of condition (ii).
So, setting
R=\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
, for any element
s\in R
one has

R[s^{-1}]=\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
for a certain affine open subscheme
V\subset U
.

It follows that, as
U
is fixed and
V
varies, condition (ii) is
equivalent to the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}[U]
being

contraadjusted
in the sense of
Section
1.3
.
Hence the terminology “contraadjustedness axiom”.

Lemma 2.4
.

The contraherence and contraadjustedness axioms imply the cosheaf
axiom: Any contraherent copresheaf on
\mathbf{B}
is a cosheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

One observes that, in the context of the definition of a cosheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
, it suffices to check the cosheaf axiom
for one’s favorite choice of the coverings
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}=\bigcup_{\gamma}W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
of the intersections

V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
, rather than for all such coverings.
This is a part of the formulation of
[
46
, Theorem 2.1.2]
.
As mentioned in the proof of Lemma
2.2
,
in the situation at hand we have
V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}\in\mathbf{B}
; so let

V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}
be its own covering.
Furthermore, it suffices to consider
finite
affine open
coverings
U=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}

only
[
46
, Remark 2.1.4]
.
This reduces the question to a commutative algebra
lemma
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.6(b)]
.
∎

A cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is said to be

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
if the restriction of
\mathfrak{P}
to
\mathbf{B}

is a contraherent co(pre)sheaf on
\mathbf{B}
.
In other words, a cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}
is called a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaf on
X
if the contraherence axiom (i) and the contraadjustedness
axiom (ii) hold for
\mathfrak{P}
for all pairs of affine open subschemes

V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
We denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\subset(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.
The full subcategory of contraherent co(pre)sheaves on
\mathbf{B}
is
denoted by
\mathbf{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\subset(\mathbf{B},\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Corollary 2.5
.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{B}
be
the topology base of
X
formed by all the affine open subschemes
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
Then the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
X
is equivalent to the category
\mathbf{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
of
contraherent copresheaves on
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

This is
[
46
, Theorem 2.2.1]
(in the case of the trivial open
covering
\mathbf{W}=\{X\}
) or
[
46
, Theorem 3.1.1]
(in the general
case of an arbitrary open covering
\mathbf{W}
).
The assertion follows from
Lemma
2.4

and Theorem
2.1
(b).
∎

A cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is said to be
(
globally
)
contraherent
if it is locally contraherent
for the trivial covering
\{X\}
of the scheme
X
.
In other words, a cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}
is called contraherent if
the contraherence axiom (i) and the contraadjustedness axiom (ii)
hold for
\mathfrak{P}
for all pairs of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X
.
We denote the full subcategory of contraherent cosheaves on
X
by

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\subset(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

A cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is said to be
locally
contraherent
if there
exists
an open covering
\mathbf{W}
such
that
\mathfrak{P}
is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent.
The full subcategory of locally contraherent cosheaves is denoted
by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\subset(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.
By the definition, we have inclusions of full subcategories

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}\subset(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}

for any open covering
\mathbf{W}
of a scheme
X
.

Unlike the quasi-coherence property of a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules,
the contraherence property of a cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules is

not
local: a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X

need not
be (globally) contraherent.
In fact, the contraadjustedness axiom (ii) is local, but
the contraherence axiom (i) is not.
A counterexample can be found in
[
46
, Example 3.2.1]
.

The
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves in the sense of
the definition above are sometimes called
locally contraadjusted
,
to distinguish them from the following two narrower classes of

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves.
A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is said to be

locally cotorsion
if the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}[U]
is
cotorsion (in the sense of Section
1.2
)
for every affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{J}
on
X
is said to be

locally injective
if the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{J}[U]
is
injective for every affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate
to
\mathbf{W}
.
(Similarly one can speak about
locally cotorsion
and

locally injective
contraherent copresheaves of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.)

The local cotorsion and local injectivity properties of locally
contraherent cosheaves
are
local (just as the terminology
suggests).
This means that, given an affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of the scheme
X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, a
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is locally cotorsion if and only if
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U_{\alpha})
-module
\mathfrak{P}[U_{\alpha}]
is cotorsion for
every
\alpha
(see
[
46
, Lemma 1.3.6(a) and Section 3.1]
).
Similarly, a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{J}
on
X
is
locally injective if and only if the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U_{\alpha})
-module

\mathfrak{J}[U_{\alpha}])
is injective for every
\alpha

(see
[
46
, Lemma 1.3.6(b) and Section 3.1]
).

We denote the full subcategories of locally cotorsion and locally
injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves by

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}.

Specializing to the case of the trivial open covering
\mathbf{W}
, we
obtain the full subcategories of locally cotorsion and locally injective
(globally) contraherent cosheaves

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}.

Finally, we put
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}

and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
; so

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}.

It follows from the previous paragraph that one has

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}=X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\cap X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

and

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}=X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\cap X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}.

2.3.
Exact categories of locally contraherent cosheaves

In this section we mostly follow
[
46
, Sections 3.1–3.2]
.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
A short sequence of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves

0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{R}\longrightarrow 0
on
X
is said to be

exact
if, for every affine open subscheme
U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the short sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-modules

0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{R}[U]\longrightarrow 0
is exact.
This rule defines an exact category structure (in the sense of
Quillen
[
12
]
) on the additive category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
of

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X

[
46
, Section 3.1]
.

Specializing to the case of the trivial open covering
\{X\}
of
the scheme
X
, we obtain the natural exact category structure on
the additive category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
of (globally) contraherent cosheaves
on
X
.
Passing to the inductive limit over refinements of coverings
\mathbf{W}
,
we obtain a natural exact category structure on the additive category

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}
of locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
The latter sentence means that a short sequence in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}
is
called
exact
if it is exact in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
for
some

open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
The full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
are closed under
extensions in the exact category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

[
46
, Corollary 3.2.4]
.

The property of a short sequence of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
X
to be exact is local.
This means that, given an affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of the scheme
X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, a short sequence
of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{R}\longrightarrow 0
on
X
is exact if and only if the short sequence of

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U_{\alpha})
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U_{\alpha}]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}[U_{\alpha}]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{R}[U_{\alpha}]\longrightarrow 0
is exact for every
\alpha

[
46
, Lemma 1.4.1(a) or 3.1.2(a)]
.
It follows that a short sequence in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
is exact if and only
if it is exact in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

[
46
, Section 3.2]
.

A morphism in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
or in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
is an admissible epimorphism
if and only if it is an admissible epimorphism in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

[
46
, Lemma 1.4.1(b)]
.
In other words, the full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
are
closed under kernels of admissible epimorphisms in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

[
46
, Section 3.2]
.
For admissible monomorphisms, the similar assertions are
not

true
[
46
, Example 3.2.1]
.

A short sequence of locally cotorsion (resp., locally injective)

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
is said to be

exact
if it is exact in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
This rule defines exact category structures on the additive categories

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
of locally cotorsion and
locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
The full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
are
closed under extensions and cokernels of admissible monomorphisms
in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
(by Lemma
1.2
(b)),
but not under the kernels of admissible epimorphisms.

Specializing to the case of the trivial open covering
\{X\}
of
the scheme
X
, we obtain natural exact category structures on
the additive categories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
of locally
cotorsion and locally injective (globally) contraherent cosheaves
on
X
.
Passing to the inductive limits over refinements of coverings
\mathbf{W}
,
we obtain natural exact category structures on the additive categories

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
of locally cotorsion and locally
injective locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
.

The exact categories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
have exact functors
of infinite products.
The functor of cosections
{-}[U]\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}

preserves infinite products for every quasi-compact quasi-separated open
subscheme
U\subset X

[
46
, Remark 2.1.4 and Section 3.1]
.
The full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
are
closed under infinite products in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
(in particular,
the full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
are closed
under infinite products in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
).

For any affine scheme
U=\Spec R
, the functor of global cosections

{-}[U]
provides an equivalence between the exact category
U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
of
contraherent cosheaves on
U
and the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}
of
contraadjusted
R
-modules,

U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\simeq R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}.

Here the exact category structure on the category of contraadjusted

R
-modules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}
is inherited from the abelian exact
structure on the ambient abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Lemma
1.11
(d) together with
Example
1.9
(1) is relevant to this category
equivalence.
See
[
55
, Section 5.6]
for a discussion.

Similarly, the same functor of global cosections
{-}[U]
provides
an equivalence between the exact category
U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
of locally
cotorsion contraherent cosheaves on
U
and the exact category

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
of cotorsion
R
-modules,

U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\simeq R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}.

Here the exact category structure on the category of cotorsion

R
-modules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
is inherited from the abelian exact structure
on the ambient abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Lemma
1.6
(c)
is relevant to this category equivalence.

Finally, on an affine scheme
U=\Spec R
, the exact structure on
the category of locally injective contraherent cosheaves
U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}

is split (i. e., all short exact sequences are split).
The very same functor of global cosections
{-}[U]
provides
an equivalence between the additive category
U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
and
the additive category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of injective
R
-modules,

U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\simeq R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}.

Lemma
1.6
(d)
is relevant to this category equivalence.

2.4.
Direct images

In this section and the next one we mostly follow
[
46
, Sections 2.3
and 3.3]
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
be a morphism of ringed spaces.
Given a copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules
\mathfrak{Q}
on
Y
, the
direct
image

f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}
of the copresheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
is a copresheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
X
defined by the rule

(f_{!}\mathfrak{Q})[U]=\mathfrak{Q}[f^{-1}(U)]

for every open subset
U\subset X
.
Here
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y
is the preimage of
U
in
Y
(which is
an open subset in
Y
).
As a part of the datum of a morphism of ringed spaces
f
, we have
a ring homomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(f^{-1}(U))
, which allows
to define an
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module structure on
the
\mathcal{O}_{U}(f^{-1}(U))
-module
\mathfrak{Q}[f^{-1}(U)]
.
The corestriction maps in the copresheaf
f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}
are defined as
the respective corestriction maps in the copresheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
.

For any cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules
\mathfrak{Q}
on
Y
, the copresheaf
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}
on
X
is a cosheaf (since the preimage
of any open covering of
U
is an open covering of
f^{-1}(U)
).
We have constructed an additive functor of direct image

f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Let
j\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
be an identity open inclusion of
ringed spaces.
So
Y
is an open subset in
X
and the sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}_{Y}=\mathcal{O}_{X}|_{Y}

is the restriction of the sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}
to
Y\subset X
.
Then the functor
j_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
is
left adjoint to the restriction functor
\mathfrak{P}\longmapsto\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}\colon(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
defined at the end of
Section
2.1
.
In other words, for any cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}\in(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
and

\mathfrak{Q}\in(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
there is a natural adjunction isomorphism
of abelian groups
[
46
, formula (2.7) in Section 2.3]

(12)

\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(j_{!}\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P}|_{Y})

Here we denote by
\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
and
\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}
the groups of
morphisms in the categories of cosheaves of modules
(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}

and
(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Now let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a morphism of schemes.
The morphism
f
is called
affine
if, for every affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
, the preimage
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y
is an affine
open subscheme in
Y
.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

of the scheme
X

[
26
, Définition 1.2.1 and Corollaire 1.3.2]
,

[
32
, Lemma Tag 01S8]
.

For any affine morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct image
functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes contraherent
cosheaves on
Y
to contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
In particular, given a contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
on
Y
,
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
(f_{!}\mathfrak{Q})[U]=\mathfrak{Q}[f^{-1}(V)]
is contraadjusted
for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
by
Lemma
1.11
(a).
So we have the direct image functor between the categories of
contraherent cosheaves
[
46
, Section 2.3]

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}.

This assertion does
not
hold without the assumption that
the morphism
f
is affine (
not
even for open immersions
f

of smooth algebraic varieties over a field
[
46
, Remark 2.3.1
and Example 2.3.2]
).

Furthermore, for any affine morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
,
the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}

takes locally cotorsion contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to locally
cotorsion contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
In particular, given a locally cotorsion contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{Q}

on
Y
, the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
(f_{!}\mathfrak{Q})[U]=\mathfrak{Q}[f^{-1}(V)]
is cotorsion
for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
by
Lemma
1.6
(a).
So we have the direct image functor between the categories of
locally cotorsion contraherent cosheaves
[
46
, Section 2.3]

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}.

See the beginning of the next
Section
2.5

for a brief discussion of
flat
morphisms of schemes.
For any flat affine morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct
image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes locally
injective contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to locally injective
contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
In particular, given a locally injective contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{I}

on
Y
, the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
(f_{!}\mathfrak{I})[U]=\mathfrak{I}[f^{-1}(V)]
is injective
for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
by
Lemma
1.6
(b).
So we have the direct image functor between the categories of
locally injective contraherent cosheaves
[
46
, Section 2.3]

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}.

More generally, let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of the scheme
X
and

\mathbf{T}
be an open covering of the scheme
Y
.
A morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is said to be

(
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine
if, for every affine open subscheme

U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the preimage
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y

an affine open subscheme subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
.
So all
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine morphisms of schemes are affine.

For any
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
,
the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}

takes
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
The resulting functor between the exact categories of locally
contraherent cosheaves

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}

is exact and preserves infinite products
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.

Similarly, for any
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine morphism of schemes

f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes locally cotorsion
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
Y
to locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
X
.
The resulting functor between the exact categories of locally cotorsion
locally contraherent cosheaves

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}

is exact and preserves infinite products
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.

Finally, for any flat
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine morphism of schemes

f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes locally injective
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
Y
to locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves on
X
.
The resulting functor between the exact categories of locally injective
locally contraherent cosheaves

f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}

is exact and preserves infinite products
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.

2.5.
Inverse images

We refer to
[
46
, Section 0.10 of the Introduction]

and
[
55
, Section 5.6]
for a introductory discussion of partially
defined functors between exact categories, which sheds some light
on the material of this section.

A morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is said to be
flat
if,
for every pair of affine open subschemes
U\subset X
and
V\subset Y

such that
f(V)\subset U
, the commmutative ring homomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
makes
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
a flat
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

of the scheme
X
and affine open subschemes
V
belonging to any
chosen affine open covering
f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\beta}V_{\beta}
of
the open subscheme
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y

[
32
, Lemma Tag 01U5]
.

A morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is said to be
very flat

if, for every pair of affine open subschemes
U\subset X
and

V\subset Y
such that
f(V)\subset U
, the commmutative ring
homomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
makes
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
a very
flat
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module (in the sense of
Section
1.3
).
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

of the scheme
X

[
46
, Corollary 1.2.5(b), Lemma 1.2.6(a),
and Section 1.10]
.

Equivalently, a morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is very flat if and
only if, for every pair of affine open subschemes
U\subset X
and

V\subset Y
such that
f(V)\subset U
, the homomorphism of commutative
rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
is very flat, or in other words,

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
is a very flat commutative algebra over
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)

(in the sense of the definition at the end of
Section
1.3
).
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

of the scheme
X
and affine open subschemes
V
belonging to any
chosen affine open covering
f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\beta}V_{\beta}
of
the open subscheme
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y

[
46
, Lemma 1.8.6 and
Section 1.10]
.

A morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is said to be
coaffine
if
for every affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
there exists an affine
open subscheme
U\subset X
such that
f(V)\subset U
.
Interesting examples of
noncoaffine
morphisms of algebraic
varieties (in particular, with an affine variety
Y
) are provided by
the
Jouanolou trick
and its generalization by
Thomason
[
71
, Proposition 4.3 or 4.4]
.
Any morphism into an affine scheme
X
is coaffine.
Any open immersion of schemes is coaffine.

Now let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of the scheme
X
and
\mathbf{T}
be
an open covering of the scheme
Y
.
A morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is said to be

(
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-coaffine
if, for every affine open subscheme

V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
, there exists an affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
such that
f(V)\subset U
.

Given an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of the scheme
X
and a morphism of
schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, one can consider the open covering
\mathbf{T}
of
the scheme
Y
formed by the preimages of all affine open subschemes

U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
Then the morphism
f
is (
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-coaffine.
Moreover, the morphism
f
is (
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine whenever
it is affine.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
Y
be a scheme
with an open covering
\mathbf{T}
.
Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-coaffine morphism of schemes.

Assume that the morphism
f
is very flat, and let
\mathfrak{P}
be
a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
.
Then the
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
Y
is
defined by the rule

(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V]=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])

for any affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
and
any chosen affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}

such that
f(V)\subset U
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])
is
contraadjusted by Lemma
1.11
(d).

We refer to
[
46
, Sections 2.3 and 3.3]
for the details of
this construction, including an explanation of why the
\mathbf{T}
-locally
contraherent cosheaf
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
Y
is well-defined.
Proving this becomes a little bit more involved when the scheme
X
is

not
assumed to be semi-separated; see
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.
The resulting inverse image functor between the exact categories of
locally contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}

is exact and preserves infinite products.

Assume that the morphism
f
is flat, and let
\mathfrak{P}
be
a locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
.
Then the locally cotorsion
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}

on
Y
is defined by the same rule

(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V]=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])

for any affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
and
any chosen affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}

such that
f(V)\subset U
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])
is
cotorsion by Lemma
1.6
(c).
The resulting inverse image functor between the exact categories of
locally cotorsion locally contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathsf{lct}}

is exact and preserves infinite products
[
46
, Sections 2.3
and 3.3]
.

Finally, let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be an arbitrary
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-coaffine
morphism of schemes, and let
\mathfrak{J}
be a locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
.
Then the locally injective
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
f^{!}\mathfrak{J}

on
Y
is defined by the same rule

(f^{!}\mathfrak{J})[V]=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{J}[U])

for any affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
and
any chosen affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}

such that
f(V)\subset U
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\mathfrak{J}[U])
is
injective by Lemma
1.6
(d).
The resulting inverse image functor between the exact categories of
locally injective locally contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathsf{lct}}

is exact and preserves infinite products
[
46
, Sections 2.3
and 3.3]
.

Passing to the inductive limit of categories over the refinements
of the coverings, we obtain an exact functor of inverse image of
locally contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}

for any very flat morphism of schemes
f
, an exact functor of
inverse image of locally cotorsion locally contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}

for any flat morphism of schemes
f
, and an exact functor of
inverse image of locally injective contraherent cosheaves

f^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muY{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathsf{lin}}

for an arbitrary morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a morphism of schemes, and let
\mathfrak{P}
be a locally
contraherent cosheaf on
X
such that the locally contraherent cosheaf
of inverse image
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
Y
is well-defined as per one of
the constructions above.
Let
\mathfrak{Q}
be a cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules on
Y
.
Then there is a natural (adjunction) isomorphism of abelian
groups
[
46
, formulas (3.4) and (3.5) in Section 3.3]

(13)

\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(f_{!}\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathfrak{Q},f^{!}\mathfrak{P}).

Given a scheme
X
with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and an open subscheme

Y\subset X
, denote by
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
the open covering of
Y
consisting
of all the open subschemes
Y\cap W
with
W\in\mathbf{W}
.
Then the open immersion
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is a very flat

(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W}|_{Y})
-coaffine morphism.
In this case, the functor of inverse image
j^{!}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\longrightarrow Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}|_{Y}}
defined above in this section agrees with the functor
of restriction to an open subscheme
\mathfrak{P}\longmapsto\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}
from
the end of Section
2.1
for

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
.

We refer to Section
3.6
below
for a further discussion with some details spelled out.

3.
Contraherent Cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-Modules

This section offers several points of view on locally contraherent
cosheaves of modules over quasi-coherent quasi-algebras
\mathcal{A}
over
schemes
X
, and spells out the basics of the theory, including
various exact categories of locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules,
direct and inverse image functors, etc.
This is new material, not yet presented elsewhere, even though parts
of the expositions are parallel to the theory of locally contraherent
cosheaves (of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules) elaborated upon in
[
46
]
and
discussed in Section
2

above.

3.1.
Quasi-coherent quasi-algebras

This section is largely based on
[
52
, second half of
Section 2.3]
, with a generalization worked out
in
[
56
, Section 2.1]
and
Sections
1.4
–
1.5
above.

Let
X
be a topological space endowed with a sheaf of commutative
rings
\mathcal{O}
.
We are interested in sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-
\mathcal{O}
-bimodules on
X
.
For the definition of a
quasi-module
over a commutative ring,
see Section
1.4
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-
\mathcal{O}
-bimodules on
X
, and let

U\subset X
be an open subset with a finite open covering

U=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}
.
Suppose that, for every index
\alpha
,
the
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
-
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})

is a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
.
Then the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}(U)
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}(U)
is a quasi-module
over
\mathcal{O}(U)
.

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of
Lemma
1.16
(2)
\Rightarrow
(1).
By the sheaf axiom, the natural map
\mathcal{B}(U)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})
is injective.
It is also a homomorphism of
\mathcal{O}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}(U)
-bimodules.
By Lemma
1.12
, the assumption
that the
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
-
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})

is a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}(V_{\alpha})
implies that, viewed as
a
\mathcal{O}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}(U)
-bimodule,
\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})
is a quasi-module
over
\mathcal{O}(U)
.
Finally, the direct sums of quasi-modules are quasi-modules and
any subbimodule of a quasi-module is a quasi-module, as mentioned in
the beginning of Section
1.4
.
∎

Assume that quasi-compact open subsets in the topological space
X

form a base of the topology.
Then we will say that a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-
\mathcal{O}
-bimodules
\mathcal{B}
on
X

is a
sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}
if, for every
quasi-compact open subset
U\subset X
, the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}(U)
-bimodule

\mathcal{B}(U)
is a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}(U)
.
By Lemma
3.1
, it suffices to
check this condition for open subsets
U
belonging to any chosen
base of neighborhoods of zero
\mathbf{B}
in
X
consisting of quasi-compact
open subsets.

A
sheaf of quasi-algebras

\mathcal{A}
over the sheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}

on
X
is defined as a sheaf of associative rings on
X
endowed with
a morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
such that, viewed with
the induced structure of a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-
\mathcal{O}
-bimodules, the sheaf

\mathcal{A}
is a sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}
on
X
.

Now let
X
be a scheme.
As a particular case of the discussion above, we obtain the definitions
of a
sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and a
sheaf of
quasi-algebras over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
on
X
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{B}
be a sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Then the sheaf
\mathcal{B}
, viewed as a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with
respect to the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, is quasi-coherent if
and only if it is quasi-coherent as a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with
respect to the right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Proof.

This assertion is based on
Corollary
1.15
.
The argument from
[
52
, proof of Proposition 2.4]
applies.
∎

A
quasi-coherent sheaf of quasi-modules on
X
(or just
a
quasi-coherent quasi-module over
X
for brevity) is defined
as a sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
satisfying the equivalent
conditions of Lemma
3.2
.

Morphisms of quasi-coherent quasi-modules
are just the usual
morphisms of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules.
We denote the category of quasi-coherent quasi-modules on
X
by

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.
One can easily check that
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
is a Grothendieck abelian category.
The two forgetful functors
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}\rightrightarrows X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
(assigning
to a quasi-coherent quasi-module its underlying quasi-coherent sheaves
of with the left and right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structures) are exact and
preserve infinite direct sums.

Any quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
can be viewed as a quasi-coherent
quasi-module over
X
in which the left and right actions of
\mathcal{O}_{X}

agree.
The resulting inclusion functor
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
is exact and
fully faithful, and preserves infinite direct sums.

For an affine scheme
U=\Spec R
, the category of quasi-coherent
quasi-modules over
U
is naturally equivalent to the category
of quasi-modules over
R
, that is
U{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}\simeq R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QMod}}}
.
Lemma
1.14
(b) is relevant here.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a quasi-compact quasi-separated morphism of
schemes and
\mathcal{B}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-module on
Y
.
Then the direct image
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-module on
X
.

Proof.

Recall that, by the definition, for any morphism of schemes (or ringed
spaces), the functor of direct image of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

f_{*}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
agrees with the functor of
direct image of sheaves of abelian groups under the map of topological
spaces
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
(see the beginning of
Section
2.4
).
Accordingly, for any sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-bimodules
\mathcal{B}
on
Y
,
the direct image
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is naturally a sheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules on
X
.
Furthermore, for any quasi-compact quasi-separated morphism of
schemes
f
, the functor of direct image
f_{*}
preserves quasi-coherence
of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules
[
27
, Proposition I.6.7.1]
,

[
32
, Lemma Tag 01LC]
.

It remains to show that, for every affine open subscheme
U\subset X
,
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-bimodule
(f_{*}\mathcal{B})(U)=\mathcal{B}(f^{-1}(U))
is
a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
.
This is another application of the argument from the proofs of
Lemmas
1.16
(2)
\Rightarrow
(1)
and
3.1
.
Since the morphism
f
is quasi-compact by assumption, the open
subset
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y
is quasi-compact.
So
f^{-1}(U)
is a finite union of affine open subschemes
V_{\alpha}

in
Y
; that is
f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}
.
Now the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})
is
a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
by
Lemma
1.12
;
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-bimodule
\mathcal{B}(f^{-1}(U))
is a subbimodule
of
\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}\mathcal{B}(V_{\alpha})
, and any subbimodule
of a quasi-module is a quasi-module. ∎

Let
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be an open immersion of schemes and
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-module on
X
.
Viewing
\mathcal{A}
as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
in the left or right

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, one can consider the inverse image
j^{*}\mathcal{A}
.
Both the inverse images so obtained are, of course, the same things
as the restriction
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
of the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules

\mathcal{A}
to the open subscheme
Y\subset X
.
So we write
j^{*}\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
.
The sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-bimodules
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
is a quasi-coherent
quasi-module on
Y
.

Recall from Section
1.4
that, given
a commutative ring
R
and two
R
-
R
-bimodules
A
and
B
,
the notation
A\otimes_{R}B
stands for the tensor product of the right

R
-module structure on
A
and the left
R
-module structure on
B
,
as usual.
Given an
R
-module
M
, the notation
B\otimes_{R}M
and
M\otimes_{R}B
is
understood similarly.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
X
be a scheme.

(a)
Given two quasi-coherent quasi-modules
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}

over
X
, their tensor product
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B}
, defined by the rule

(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B})(U)=\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U),

for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
, is again a quasi-coherent
quasi-module over
X
.

(b)
Given a quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{B}
and
a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}
on
X
, the two tensor products

\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B}
, defined by
the rules

(\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M})(U)=\mathcal{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)\text{ and }(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B})(U)=\mathcal{M}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U),

for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
, are quasi-coherent sheaves
on
X
.

Proof.

This is our version of
[
52
, Lemma 2.5]
.
In part (a), the point is that
\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U)
is
a quasi-module over
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
by
[
56
, Proposition 2.4]
, and for
every pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X
one has

\mathcal{A}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)}\mathcal{B}(V)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{A}(U))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U))\\
\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U)).

Part (b) can be viewed as a particular case of part (a).
∎

The abelian category of quasi-coherent quasi-modules
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
is
an (associative, noncommutative) monoidal category with respect to
the tensor product operation
\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
.
The structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
, viewed as an object of
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
via
the fully faithful inclusion functor
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
, is
the unit object of this monoidal category.
Viewing
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
as a (commutative) monoidal category with respect
to the usual tensor product functor
\otimes_{X}
makes the inclusion
functor
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
monoidal.
Furthermore, the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is both a left and a right module
category over the monoidal category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
with respect to
the tensor product functors
\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
defined above.

An (associative, unital)
quasi-coherent quasi-algebra

\mathcal{A}

over
X
is defined as a monoid object in the monoidal category

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.
So a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
is a quasi-coherent
quasi-module endowed with quasi-coherent quasi-module morphisms of

multiplication

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
and
unit

\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
satisfying the usual associativity and unitality
axioms.
Alternatively, a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over
X
is
the same thing as a sheaf of quasi-algebras over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
whose
underlying sheaf of quasi-modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
is quasi-coherent.

For an affine scheme
U=\Spec R
, the category of quasi-coherent
quasi-algebras over
U
is naturally equivalent to the category
of quasi-algebras over
R
(in the sense of
Section
1.5
).
Lemma
1.20
(a) is relevant here.

Remarks 3.5
.

(1) Let
K\longrightarrow R
be a homomorphism of commutative rings, and
let
B
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule over
K
(i. e., the left and right
actions of
K
in
B
agree; see
Section
A.1
).
Consider the ring
T=R\otimes_{K}R
.
Then
[
56
, Corollary 2.3]
says that
B
is a quasi-module over
R

if and only if, viewed as a
T
-module,
B
is supported
set-theoretically in the image of the diagonal closed immersion of
schemes
\Spec R\longrightarrow\Spec R\times_{\Spec K}\Spec R=\Spec T
.

(2) Consider a morphism of schemes
f\colon X\longrightarrow S
.
Let us say that a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules
\mathcal{B}
on
X

is a
module over
S
if, for every pair of
affine open subschemes
U\subset S
and
V\subset X
such that

f(V)\subset U
, the left and right actions of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
in

\mathcal{B}(V)
restrict to one and the same action of
\mathcal{O}_{S}(U)
in
\mathcal{B}(V)
,
i. e., the left and right actions of
\mathcal{O}_{S}(U)
in
\mathcal{B}(V)
agree.

(3) Assume that the morphism
f
is separated, i. e., the diagonal
morphism
X\longrightarrow X\times_{S}X
is a closed immersion.
Globalizing the assertion of (1) from affine to arbitrary schemes, one
can say that the abelian category of quasi-coherent quasi-modules over

X
that are modules over
S
is equivalent to the abelian category of
quasi-coherent sheaves on
W=X\times_{S}X
supported set-theoretically
in the image
Z\subset W
of the diagonal closed immersion

X\longrightarrow X\times_{S}X
.

Example 3.6
.

Let
X\longrightarrow S
be a smooth morphism of schemes (e. g., in the sense
of
[
32
, Section Tag 01V4]
).
Assume for simplicity that we are in characteristic
0
, that is,

S
is a scheme over
\Spec\mathbb{Q}
.
Then the sheaf of rings of relative (fiberwise) differential operators

\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.

3.2.
\Cohom
from quasi-coherent quasi-modules to
contraherent cosheaves

The material of this section is a (partial) generalization
of
[
46
, Sections 2.4 and 3.6]
.
We refer to
[
46
, Section 0.10 of the Introduction]

and
[
55
, Sections 5.5–5.6]
for a relevant discussion of
partially defined functors between exact categories.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
Denote by
\mathbf{B}
the base of open subsets in
X
consisting of all
the affine open subschemes
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-module and
\mathfrak{P}
be
a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
.
The copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
on
\mathbf{B}

is defined by the rule

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})[U]=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U])

for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

Here, as usual, the
\Hom
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-modules is taken with
respect to the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module structure on
\mathcal{B}(U)
(and
the only given
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{P}[U]
).
The abelian group
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U])
is endowed with
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module structure induced by the right

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module structure on
\mathcal{B}(U)
(as per the convention
in Section
1.4
).

The
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathcal{B}(U)
depends contravariantly on

U\in\mathbf{B}
, while the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}[U]
depends covariantly
on
U\in\mathbf{B}
.
Hence the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U])
depends
covariantly on
U\in\mathbf{B}
, so
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
is indeed
a copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

(a)
If a quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{B}
on
X
is very
flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to the left

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, and
\mathfrak{P}
is a
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent cosheaf on
X
, then
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
is
a contraherent copresheaf on
\mathbf{B}
.

(b)
If a quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{B}
on
X
is
flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to the left

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, and
\mathfrak{P}
is a locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
, then
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
is
a locally cotorsion contraherent copresheaf on
\mathbf{B}
.

(c)
If a quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{B}
on
X
is
flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to the right

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, and
\mathfrak{J}
is a locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
, then
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})

is a locally injective contraherent copresheaf on
\mathbf{B}
.

(d)
For any quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{B}
on
X
, if

\mathfrak{J}
is a locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
on
X
, then
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})
is a locally cotorsion contraherent
copresheaf on
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof.

The copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
on
\mathbf{B}

satisfies the contraherence axiom (i) from
Section
2.2
for all
quasi-coherent quasi-modules
\mathcal{B}
and all
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
.
Indeed, we have

\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})[U])\\
=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U]))\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\>\mathfrak{P}[U])\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\>\mathfrak{P}[U])\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\>\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U]))\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[V])\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[V])

for every pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U
,
U
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
,
by Lemma
1.14
(a).

It remains to point out that the contraadjustedness axiom (ii) holds
in part (a) by Lemma
1.17
(b).
In the assumptions of part (b), the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})[U]
is cotorsion by
Lemma
1.5
(a).
In the assumptions of part (c), the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})[U]
is injective by
Lemma
1.5
(b).
In the assumptions of part (d), the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})[U]
is cotorsion by
Lemma
1.5
(c).
∎

Using
Corollary
2.5
,
in each of the cases (a–d) of Lemma
3.7

we extend the contraherent copresheaf
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
or

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})
on
\mathbf{B}
to a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
on (all open subsets of) the whole scheme
X
.
The resulting
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
is also
denoted by
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
or
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})
, respectively.
To summarize:

•

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent if
\mathcal{B}
is
very flat on the left and
\mathfrak{P}
is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent;

•

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{P})
is locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent if
\mathcal{B}
is flat on the left and
\mathfrak{P}
is locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent;

•

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})
is locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent if
\mathcal{B}
is flat on the right and
\mathfrak{J}
is locally
injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent;

•

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{J})
is locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent if
\mathfrak{J}
is locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent.

3.3.
Locally contraherent cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with
a morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
.
Then a
quasi-coherent sheaf of left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
on
X
(or
a
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
for brevity) can be simply
defined as a sheaf of left
\mathcal{A}
-modules whose underlying sheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules is quasi-coherent.

Quasi-coherent right
\mathcal{A}
-modules
are defined similarly.
The category of quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
and
the category of quasi-coherent right
\mathcal{A}
-modules
{\operatorname{\mathsf{Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{A}
are
Grothendieck abelian categories.
The forgetful functor
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is exact and preserves
infinite direct sums.

Let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
.
Then a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf of
(
left
)

\mathcal{A}
-modules
on
X
(or a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
for brevity) can be defined as a cosheaf of

\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
(in the sense of
Section
2.1
) whose underlying
cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent.
A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module is said to be

X
-locally cotorsion
(respectively,
X
-locally
injective
) if its underlying
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules is locally cotorsion (resp., locally injective).

We will denote the category of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left

\mathcal{A}
-modules by
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
, the category of
X
-locally
cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules by

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and the category of
X
-locally
cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules by

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.

We also put
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.
The objects of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
are called
contraherent cosheaves of

\mathcal{A}
-modules
(or
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
for brevity),
the objects of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
are called
X
-locally
cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
, and similarly for the objects
of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.
Finally, we put
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.
The objects of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}
are called
locally contraherent
cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
, and similarly for the objects of

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
and
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.

A short sequence of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves of

\mathcal{A}
-modules is said be
exact
in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
if its
underlying short sequence of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules is exact as a short sequence in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
The similar rules define short exact sequence in all the other
categories of (locally) contraherent cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
mentioned in the previous two paragraphs.
Endowed with these classes of short exact sequences, all of these
categories become exact categories.

The exact categories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
have exact functors of infinite products.
The forgetful functors from these exact categories to the respective
exact categories of locally contraherent cosheaves (of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules)
on
X
are exact and preserve infinite products.

The forgetful functors
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
, and

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
also (preserve and)
reflect admissible monomorphisms and admissible epimorphisms.
So a morphism in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
is an admissible monomorphism
(respectively, an admissible epimorphisms) if and only if it is
an admissible monomorphism (resp., admissible epimorphism) in

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
; and the same applies to the two other forgetful
functors above.

Now let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
As explained in Section
3.1
,
the category of quasi-coherent quasi-modules
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
is a monoidal
category, the category of quasi-coherent sheaves
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is a (left
and right) module category over
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
, and
\mathcal{A}
is a monoid
object in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.
In this context, quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules can be equivalently
defined as left module objects in the left module category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

over the monoid object
\mathcal{A}
in the monoidal category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.
So a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
is the same thing as
a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
endowed with a
left action

morphism of quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}

satisfying the usual associativity and unitality axioms together
with the multiplication and unit morphisms of
\mathcal{A}
.

Given a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}
on
X
, the tensor product

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}
has a natural structure of a quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module.
The functor
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}\,\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is left
adjoint to the forgetful functor
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

For a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over an affine scheme

U=\Spec R
, the abelian category of quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
is naturally equivalent to the abelian category of left modules over
the ring
A=\mathcal{A}(U)
, that is
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Lemma
1.20
(b) is relevant here.

Denote by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
the full subcategory of
all quasi-coherent quasi-modules that are very flat as quasi-coherent
sheaves on
X
with respect to the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Similarly, denote by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
the full
subcategory of all quasi-coherent quasi-modules that are flat as
quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
with respect to the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
structure, and by
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
the full subcategory
of all quasi-coherent quasi-modules that are flat as quasi-coherent
sheaves on
X
with respect to the right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Clearly, the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
belongs
to all the three full subcategories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}
,
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}
,
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}
.

It is clear from the definitions that the
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}
and

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}
are monoidal full subcategories in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.
By Lemma
1.17
(a),
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}

is also a monoidal full subcategory in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
.

The functor

\Cohom_{X}({-},{-})\colon(X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}

from Lemma
3.7
(a) and the subsequent
discussion in Section
3.2
defines
a structure of right module category over
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}
on
the opposite category to the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
of
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
Similarly, the functor

\Cohom_{X}({-},{-})\colon(X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}

from Lemma
3.7
(b) and the subsequent
discussion in Section
3.2
defines
a structure of right module category over
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}
on the opposite
category to the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
of locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
.
Finally, the functor

\Cohom_{X}({-},{-})\colon(X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muX{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}

from Lemma
3.7
(c) and the subsequent
discussion in Section
3.2
defines
a structure of right module category over
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}
on the opposite
category to the category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
of locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
.

These assertions are based on the observations that

•

for all
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}
and
\mathfrak{P}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
,
a natural associativity isomorphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P}))\simeq\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B},\>\mathfrak{P})
holds in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
;

•

for all
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}
and
\mathfrak{P}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
,
a natural associativity isomorphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P}))\simeq\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B},\>\mathfrak{P})
holds in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
;

•

for all
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}
and
\mathfrak{J}\in X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
,
a natural associativity isomorphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{B},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{J}))\simeq\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{B},\>\mathfrak{J})
holds in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
.

The other relevant observation is that of a natural isomorphism

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathfrak{P})\simeq\mathfrak{P}
for any
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
that is very
flat with respect to its left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Then
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
can be
equivalently defined as right module objects in the right module
category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
over the monoid object
\mathcal{A}
in the monoidal
category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lvfl}}
.
So a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
is the same
thing as a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
endowed with
a
left action
morphism of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves

\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})
satisfying natural associativity and
unitality axioms together with the multiplication and unit morphisms
of
\mathcal{A}
.
