Specifically, the two compositions

\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})\rightrightarrows\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{A},\>\mathfrak{P})\simeq\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P}))

of the left action morphism
\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})
with
the morphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})\allowbreak\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{A},\>\mathfrak{P})
induced by the multiplication morphism

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
and with the morphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P}))
induced by the left action
morphism
\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})
must be equal to each other
(associativity).
The composition

\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}

of the left action morphism
\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})
with
the morphism
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})\allowbreak\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}
induced by the unit
morphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
must be equal to the identity endomorphism
of
\mathfrak{P}
(unitality).

Similarly, let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
that
is flat with respect to its left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Then
X
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left

\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
can be equivalently defined as right module
objects in the right module category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
over the monoid
object
\mathcal{A}
in the monoidal category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{lfl}}
.
So an
X
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
is the same thing as a locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
endowed with a
left
action
morphism of locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{P})
satisfying the associativity
and unitality axioms above.

Finally, let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
that
is flat with respect to its right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Then
X
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left

\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
can be equivalently defined as right module
objects in the right module category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lin}}
over the monoid
object
\mathcal{A}
in the monoidal category
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}_{\mathsf{rfl}}
.
So an
X
-locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{J}
is the same thing as a locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
X
endowed with a
left
action
morphism of locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaves
\mathfrak{J}\longrightarrow\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{J})
satisfying the same
associativity and unitality axioms.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
For any
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
on
X
such that
a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{Q})
is
well-defined as per one of the conditions in
Section
3.2
, the
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent cosheaf
\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{Q})
on
X
has a natural left

\mathcal{A}
-module structure.
Under these assumptions, for any
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
there is a natural adjunction isomorphism of
abelian groups

(14)

\Hom^{X}(\mathfrak{P},\mathfrak{Q})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathfrak{P},\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{Q})).

Here we denote by
\Hom^{X}
the groups of morphisms in the category
of locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
, and by
\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}

the groups of morphisms in the category of locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.

Let
U=\Spec R
be an affine scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent
quasi-algebra over
U
with the ring of global sections
A=\mathcal{A}(U)
;
so
A
is a quasi-algebra over
R
.
Then the exact category of contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{U\}}
is naturally equivalent to the exact category

A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}
of
R
-contraadjusted left
A
-modules (see
Section
1.5
for the terminology).
Here the exact category structure on the full subcategory

A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\subset A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is inherited from the abelian
exact structure of the ambient abelian category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Similarly, the exact category of
U
-locally cotorsion contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{U\}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
is
naturally equivalent to the exact category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
of

R
-cotorsion left
A
-modules.
Once again, the exact category structure on the full subcategory

A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is inherited from the abelian
exact structure of the ambient abelian category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Finally, the exact category of
U
-locally injective contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{U\}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
is
naturally equivalent to the exact category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-inj}}}}
of

R
-injective left
A
-modules.
As above, the exact category structure on the full subcategory

A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-inj}}}}\subset A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is inherited from the abelian
exact structure of the ambient abelian category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Lemma
1.20
(c) is relevant to
these assertions.

3.4.
\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion and
\mathcal{A}
-locally injective
cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
over
X
.
A quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is said to be

flat
(or
\mathcal{A}
-flat
) if, for every affine open subscheme

U\subset X
, the left
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{F}(U)
is flat.
By Lemma
1.23
(a), it suffices
to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U
belonging to
any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of
the scheme
X
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to
its left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, then any
\mathcal{A}
-flat left

\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
is also flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

The full subcategory
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}
of
\mathcal{A}
-flat
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules is closed under kernels of
epimorphisms, extensions, and infinite direct sums in the abelian
category of quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
So the full subcategory
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
inherits
an exact category structure from the abelian exact structure of

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
.
A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
is said to be

\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion
if, for every affine open subscheme

U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the left
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}[U]
is
cotorsion.
By Lemma
1.23
(b), it suffices
to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U
belonging to
any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of
the scheme
X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
By Lemma
1.6
(a), any

\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
on
X
is
X
-locally cotorsion.

The full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
of
\mathcal{A}
-locally
cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is closed under
cokernels of admissible monomorphisms, extensions, and infinite products
in the exact category of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
(or in the exact category of
X
-locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
).
See, in particular, Lemma
1.2
(b).
So the full subcategory
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
inherits an exact category structure from
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.

A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{J}
on
X
is said to be

\mathcal{A}
-locally injective
if, for every affine open subscheme

U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the left
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{J}[U]
is
injective.
By Lemma
1.23
(c), it suffices
to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U
belonging to
any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of
the scheme
X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to
its right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure then,
by Lemma
1.6
(b), any

\mathcal{A}
-locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
on
X
is
X
-locally injective.

The full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
of
\mathcal{A}
-locally
injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is closed under
cokernels of admissible monomorphisms, extensions, and infinite products
in the exact category of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
So the full subcategory
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
inherits an exact category structure from
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.

As usual, we put
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}

and
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
, as well as

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
and

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.

Let
U=\Spec R
and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
U

with the ring of global sections
A=\mathcal{A}(U)
.
Then the exact category of
\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{U\}}
is naturally
equivalent to the exact category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
of cotorsion left

A
-modules.
Here the exact category structure on the full subcategory

A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}\subset A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is inherited from the abelian
exact structure of the ambient abelian category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

The exact category of
\mathcal{A}
-locally injective contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{U\}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
over
an affine scheme
U
has a split exact structure (all short exact
sequences are split).
The additive category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
is naturally equivalent
to the additive category
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}
of injective left
A
-modules.

3.5.
Direct images

Let us introduce one extra piece of terminology:
A
quasi-ringed scheme

(X,\mathcal{A})
is a pair consisting of a scheme

X
and a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over
X
.
Given a quasi-ringed scheme
(Y,\mathcal{B})
and a morphism of schemes

f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct image
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is, generally speaking,
just a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with a morphism of sheaves
of rings
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
.
A
morphism of quasi-ringed schemes

f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})

is a pair consisting of a morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
and
a morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
on
X
making
the square diagram of morphisms of sheaves of rings on
X

(15)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.63512pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.63512pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.63512pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.63512pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{f_{*}\mathcal{B}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-10.68996pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.71153pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-7.44444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.71153pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.46153pt\raise-7.44444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

commutative.

When the morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is quasi-compact and
quasi-separated, the direct image
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-coherent
quasi-module on
X
by
Lemma
3.3
.
On the other hand,
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with
a morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
(constructed
as the composition
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
).
One can easily check that the two resulting structures of a sheaf
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules on
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
agree; so
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Thus, in the context of a morphism of quasi-ringed schemes

f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
, the morphism of sheaves of rings

\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
is a morphism of quasi-coherent quasi-algebras
over
X
whenever
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated.

More generally, let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a morphism of schemes,
\mathcal{A}
be
a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with a morphism of sheaves of rings

\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
, and
\mathcal{B}
be a sheaf of rings on
Y
endowed with
a morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}
.
Assume that we are given a morphism of sheaves of rings

\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
on
X
making the square
diagram (
15
) commutative.

Assume that the morphism
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is quasi-compact and
quasi-separated.
Let
\mathcal{N}
be a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{B}
-module on
Y
.
Then the direct image
f_{*}\mathcal{N}
has a natural structure of a sheaf
of
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
whose underlying sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
is quasi-coherent.
Using the morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
, one defines
the induced structure of a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module on
f_{*}\mathcal{N}
.
So we have the direct image functor

(16)

f_{*}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}.

Let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
and
\mathbf{T}
be an open covering
of
Y
such that the morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is

(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine (in the sense of
Section
2.4
).
Let
\mathfrak{Q}
be a
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
Y
.
Then the direct image
f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}
is a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
cosheaf on
X
with a natural structure of a locally contraherent
cosheaf of
f_{*}\mathcal{B}
-modules.
Using the morphism of sheaves of rings
\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{B}
, one defines
the induced structure of a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
on
f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}
.
So we obtain the direct image functor

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}},

which restricts to a direct image functor between the categories
of
Y
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-modules
and
X
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}.

Assuming additionally that
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is a flat morphism of
schemes, the two direct image functors
f_{!}
above restrict to a direct
image functor between the categories of
Y
-locally injective

\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-modules and
X
-locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules,

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}.

In particular, let
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be an open immersion of schemes,
and let
\mathcal{A}
be a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with a morphism of
sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
.
Put
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
; then there are natural morphisms of sheaves of
rings
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}
on
Y
and
\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow j_{*}\mathcal{B}
on
X

making the square diagram (
15
)
commutative (for
f=j
).
Given an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of
X
, consider the open covering

\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
of
Y
as at the end of
Section
2.5
;
then
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is a
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W}|_{Y})
-coaffine morphism of schemes.
If the open immersion morphism
j
is affine, then it is also
a
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W}|_{Y})
-affine morphism of schemes.

In this context, for any quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X
,
the restriction
j^{*}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}|_{Y}
of
M
to
Y
is naturally
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{B}
-module on
Y
; and for any
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
, the restriction

j^{!}\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}
of
\mathfrak{P}
to
Y
is naturally a
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
-locally
contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module on
Y
.
Assuming that the open immersion morphism
j
is affine,
as a special case of the adjunction
isomorphism (
12
),
we obtain an adjunction isomorphism

(17)

\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}(j_{!}\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P}|_{Y})

for any
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module
\mathfrak{Q}
on
Y
and
any
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
.
Here we denote by
\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}
the groups of morphisms in
the categories of locally contraherent cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules and

\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
and
Y
.

In this paper, we are mostly interested in the direct images
of quasi-coherent and locally contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-modules in
the context of the previous two paragraphs (i. e., for an affine
open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
and the sheaf of rings

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
).
Moreover, we will almost always assume that
\mathcal{A}
is a quasi-coherent
quasi-algebra over
X
(hence
\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
over
Y
).

Now let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
be a morphism of quasi-ringed
schemes (so
\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and

\mathcal{A}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
Y
).
Let us say that
f
is
right flat
(as a morphism of
quasi-ringed schemes) if, for every pair of affine open subschemes

U\subset X
and
V\subset Y
such that
f(V)\subset U
, the ring
homomorphism
\mathcal{A}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}(V)
makes
\mathcal{B}(V)
a flat right

\mathcal{A}(U)
-module.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

of the scheme
X
and affine open subschemes
V
belonging to any
chosen affine open covering
f^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\beta}V_{\beta}
of
the open subscheme
f^{-1}(U)\subset Y
(by
Lemmas
1.23
(a)
and
1.26
).

Let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
be a morphism of quasi-ringed schemes,
and let
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{T}
be open coverings of
X
and
Y
such that
the morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-affine.
Then the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
takes
\mathcal{B}
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{T}
-locally
contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to
\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
(by
Lemma
1.6
(a)).
So we have the direct image functor

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}.

Assume that the morphism of quasi-ringed schemes

f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
is right flat.
Then the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}

takes
\mathcal{B}
-locally injective
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
on
Y
to
\mathcal{A}
-locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
on
X
(by Lemma
1.6
(b)).
So we obtain the direct image functor

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}.

3.6.
Inverse images

The definition of a right flat morphism of quasi-ringed schemes

f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
was given in the previous
Section
3.5
.
The definition of a
left flat
morphism of quasi-ringed schemes
is similar.

Given a morphism of quasi-ringed schemes
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})

consider the topology base
\mathbf{B}
of
Y
consisting of all the affine
open subschemes
V\subset Y
for which there exists an affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
such that
f(V)\subset U
.
For any quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X
, one defines
the presheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
on
\mathbf{B}
by the rule

(f^{*}\mathcal{M})(V)=\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)

for any affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
,
V\in\mathbf{B}
and any chosen
affine open subscheme
U\subset X
such that
f(V)\subset U
.

The tricky part of this definition is to check that
the
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module
\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)
does not depend on
the choice of an affine open subscheme
U\subset X
.
For the beginning, assume that the scheme
X
is
semi-separated
,
i. e., finite intersections of affine open subschemes in
X

are affine.
Then, for any two affine open subschemes
U^{\prime}\subset X
and

U^{\prime\prime}\subset X
such that
f(V)\subset U^{\prime}
and
f(V)\subset U^{\prime\prime}
,
one has
f(V)\subset U^{\prime}\cap U^{\prime\prime}
, where
U^{\prime}\cap U^{\prime\prime}
is a smaller
affine open subscheme in
X
.
It remains to compute that, given a pair of affine open subschemes

U\subset U^{\prime}\subset X
such that
f(V)\subset U
, one has

\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)\simeq\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime}))\simeq\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime})

because

\mathcal{M}(U)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime})\\
\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime}))\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{M}(U^{\prime})\simeq\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{M}(U).

To check the quasi-coherence axiom from
Section
2.2
for
the presheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
on
\mathbf{B}
, one computes that,
for any affine open subschemes
V\subset V^{\prime}\subset Y
and any affine
open subscheme
U\subset X
such that
f(V^{\prime})\subset U
,

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}(f^{*}\mathcal{M})(V^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}(\mathcal{B}(V^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U))\\
\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}\mathcal{B}(V^{\prime}))\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)\simeq\mathcal{B}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{A}(U)}\mathcal{M}(U)=(f^{*}\mathcal{M})(V).

Now, by Theorem
2.1
(a)
and Lemma
2.2
, the quasi-coherent
presheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
on
\mathbf{B}
extends uniquely to
a quasi-coherent sheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
, which we also denote
by
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
.

It remains to explain what to do if the scheme
X
is not
semi-separated.
In this case, one just covers
X
by semi-separated (or affine)
open subschemes
X_{\alpha}
, puts
Y_{\alpha}=f^{-1}(X_{\alpha})
,
and restricts the quasi-coherent quasi-algebras
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
to

X_{\alpha}
and
Y_{\alpha}
, respectively.
Furthermore, one also restricts the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}

to the open subschemes
X_{\alpha}\subset X
, obtaining quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{X_{\alpha}}
-modules
\mathcal{M}|_{X_{\alpha}}
.
Then one takes the inverse images of the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{X_{\alpha}}
-modules
\mathcal{M}|_{X_{\alpha}}
along the (obvious)
morphisms of quasi-ringed schemes
f_{\alpha}\colon(Y_{\alpha},\mathcal{B}|_{Y_{\alpha}})\longrightarrow(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{A}|_{X_{\alpha}})
.
Finally, one observes that the resulting quasi-coherent

\mathcal{B}|_{Y_{\alpha}}
-modules
f_{\alpha}^{*}(\mathcal{M}|_{X_{\alpha}})
on
Y_{\alpha}

agree on the intersections
Y_{\alpha}\cap Y_{\beta}
(e. g., since the open
subschemes
X_{\alpha}\cap X_{\beta}\subset X
are also semi-separated); so
(e. g., by Theorem
2.1
(a))
they can be glued together to obtain the desired quasi-coherent

\mathcal{B}
-module
f^{*}\mathcal{M}
on
Y
.
(Cf. the discussion of the non-semi-separatedness issue
in
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.)

We have constructed the inverse image functor

(18)

f^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}.

The functor
f^{*}
is right exact and preserves infinite direct sums.
When the morphism of quasi-ringed schemes
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})

is right flat, the functor
f^{*}
is exact.
When the morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is quasi-compact and
quasi-separated, the functor
f^{*}
is left adjoint to the direct
image functor
f_{*}
(
16
).

Having spelled out the quasi-coherent case in terms instructive for
our purposes, let us pass to the locally contraherent context, which is
only slightly more complicated.
Let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
be a left flat morphism of quasi-ringed
schemes.
Let
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{T}
be open coverings of
X
and
Y
such that
the morphism of schemes
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T})
-coaffine.
Consider the topology base
\mathbf{B}
of
Y
consisting of all the affine
open subschemes
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
.

Given an
\mathcal{A}
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
on
X
, we define the copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules

f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
\mathbf{B}
by the rule

(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V]=\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])

for any affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
and
any chosen affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}

such that
f(V)\subset U
.
Notice that the
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])
is
cotorsion by Lemma
1.6
(c).

Once again, we assume that the scheme
X
is semi-separated for
the beginning, and check that the
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])
does not depend on the choice of
an affine open subscheme
U\subset X
.
Indeed, given a pair of affine open subschemes
U\subset U^{\prime}\subset X

such that
f(V)\subset U
, one has

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}(\mathcal{A}(U),P[U^{\prime}]))\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U^{\prime}])

because

\mathfrak{P}[U]\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U^{\prime})}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U^{\prime}])\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}(\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U^{\prime})}\mathcal{O}_{X}(U),\>\mathfrak{P}[U^{\prime}])\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U^{\prime})}(\mathcal{A}(U),\mathfrak{P}[U^{\prime}]).

Let us check that the contraherence axiom (i) from
Section
2.2
for
the copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
\mathbf{B}
.
Indeed, for any affine open subschemes
V\subset V^{\prime}\subset Y
and any
affine open subscheme
U\subset X
such that
f(V^{\prime})\subset U
, we have

\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V^{\prime}])=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{P}[U]))\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V^{\prime})}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V),\>\mathfrak{P}[U])\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{P}[U])=(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V].

Furthermore, for any
V\in\mathbf{B}
, the module
(f^{!}\mathfrak{P})[V]
is cotorsion
over
\mathcal{B}(V)
(as mentioned above), hence also cotorsion over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(V)

(by Lemma
1.6
(a)); so
the contraadjustedness axiom (ii) holds (for the underlying copresheaf
of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules of the copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
)
as well.
Now, by Theorem
2.1
(b)
and Lemma
2.4
,
the contraherent copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
\mathbf{B}
extends
uniquely to a
\mathcal{B}
-locally cotorsion
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent
cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
, which we also denote by
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
.

The case of a non-semi-separated scheme
X
is dealt with similarly
to the way it is treated in the discussion above in the context of
quasi-coherent sheaves; see also
[
46
, Section 3.3]
.

We have constructed the inverse image functor

(19)

f^{!}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}.

The functor
f^{!}
is exact (as a functor of exact categories) and
preserves infinite products.

Now let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
be an arbitrary morphism of
quasi-ringed schemes.
Then, given an
\mathcal{A}
-locally injective
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{J}
on
X
, we define the copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules

f^{!}\mathfrak{J}
on
\mathbf{B}
by the same rule

(f^{!}\mathfrak{J})[V]=\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{J}[U])

for
V\in\mathbf{B}
and
U
as above.
The
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(V),\mathfrak{J}[U])
is injective by
by Lemma
1.6
(d),
hence cotorsion by the definition; hence it is also cotorsion
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
-module by
Lemma
1.6
(a).

So the contraadjustedness axiom (ii) from
Section
2.2
holds for
(the underlying copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules of) the copresheaf
of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{!}\mathfrak{J}
on
\mathbf{B}
.
The rest of the arguments, proving that the
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module

(f^{!}\mathfrak{J})[V]
is well-defined and the contraherence axiom (i)
is satisfied for
f^{!}\mathfrak{J}
, is the same as above.
Hence the contraherent copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f^{!}\mathfrak{J}
on
\mathbf{B}

extends uniquely to a
\mathcal{B}
-locally injective
\mathbf{T}
-locally
contraherent cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
, which we also denote
by
f^{!}\mathfrak{J}
.

We have constructed the inverse image functor

(20)

f^{!}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}.

The functor
f^{!}
is exact and preserves infinite products.

Let
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
be a morphism of quasi-ringed schemes,
and let
\mathfrak{P}
be locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
such that
the locally contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
on
Y
is well-defined
as per one of the two constructions above.
Let
\mathfrak{Q}
be a cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
.
Then there is a natural (adjunction) isomorphism of abelian groups

(21)

\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}(f_{!}\mathfrak{Q},\mathfrak{P})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{Q},f^{!}\mathfrak{P}).

Here we denote by
\Hom^{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}
the groups of morphisms
in the categories of cosheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
and cosheaves
of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
.

Indeed, let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
such that the cosheaf

\mathfrak{P}
is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent, and let
\mathbf{T}
be an open covering
of
Y
such that the morphism
f
is
(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{T}
)-coaffine (so
the cosheaf
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
is
\mathbf{T}
-locally contraherent).
Then both the abelian groups
in (
21
) are naturally
isomorphic to the group of all compatible systems of
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
maps
\mathfrak{Q}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]
defined for affine open subschemes
U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
and
V\subset Y
subordinate to
\mathbf{T}
such that

f(V)\subset U
.
Here the
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module structure on
\mathfrak{Q}[V]
is obtained from
the
\mathcal{B}(V)
-module structure by restriction of scalars via
the ring homomorphism
\mathcal{A}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}(V)
.

Given a quasi-ringed scheme
(X,\mathcal{A})
and an open subscheme

Y\subset X
with the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
,
put
\mathcal{B}=j^{*}\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
.
Then
(Y,\mathcal{B})
is a quasi-ringed scheme and
j\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})

is naturally a left and right flat morphism of quasi-ringed schemes.
Given an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of
X
, consider the open covering

\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
of
Y
as at the end of
Section
2.5
and in
Section
3.5
.
In this context, the inverse image
functor
j^{*}
(
18
) defined in
this section agrees with the restriction functor
\mathcal{M}\longmapsto\mathcal{M}|_{Y}

discussed in Section
3.5
.
The inverse image functors
j^{!}

(
19
)
and (
20
) defined in this section
agree with the restriction functor
\mathfrak{P}\longmapsto\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}
from
Section
3.5
wherever
the former are defined.

In this paper, we are mostly interested in the inverse images of
quasi-coherent and locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules in the context
of the previous paragraph (i. e., for an open immersion morphism

j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
and the quasi-coherent quasi-algebra

\mathcal{B}=j^{*}\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
).
We will use the convenient notation
j^{*}
and
j^{!}
for the functors
of restriction of quasi-coherent and locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules to the open subscheme
Y\subset X
(even though
the more restrictive assumptions on a locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
required for the general definition of the inverse
image
f^{!}\mathfrak{P}
above in this section may be not satisfied).
So we just put
j^{*}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}|_{Y}
and
j^{!}\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}
, by abuse of notation.

Let us empasize, however, that, generally speaking, for a morphism
of quasi-ringed schemes
f\colon(Y,\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A})
, the inverse
image functor (
18
) from this section
does
not
agree with the functor of inverse image of
quasi-coherent sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules
f^{*}\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
Similarly, the inverse image
functors (
19
)
and (
20
) from this section
do
not
agree with the functors of inverse image of
locally contraherent cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules from
Section
2.5
.
On ther other hand, the direct image functors
f_{*}
and
f_{!}

from Section
3.5
, by
the definition,
do
agree with the direct image functors
from Section
2.4
.

3.7.
Čech (co)resolutions

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme and

X=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}U_{\alpha}
be a finite affine open covering
of
X
.
For every subsequence of indices
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\dotsb<\alpha_{k}\leq N
,
denote by
j_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}\colon\bigcap_{s=1}^{k}U_{\alpha_{s}}\longrightarrow X
the open immersion morphism.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a sheaf of rings on
X
endowed with a morphism of
sheaves of rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
.
Then the Čech coresolution

(22)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{M}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}j_{\alpha}{}_{*}j_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{M}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}j_{\alpha,\beta}{}_{*}j_{\alpha,\beta}^{*}\mathcal{M}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muj_{1,\dotsc,N}{}_{*}j_{1,\dotsc,N}^{*}\mathcal{M}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

is a finite exact sequence of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
Here, as suggested in the previous
Section
3.6
, we just use
the symbol
j^{*}
as a simplified notation for the functor

\mathcal{M}\longmapsto\mathcal{M}|_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of restriction of
quasi-coherent sheaves of
\mathcal{A}
-modules to an open subscheme

Y\subset X
with the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.

Now let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
, and let

X=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}U_{\alpha}
be a finite affine open covering
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
Let
\mathfrak{P}
be a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
.
Then the Čech resolution
[
46
, formula (3.6) in Section 3.3]

(23)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muj_{1,\dotsc,N}{}_{!}j_{1,\dotsc,N}^{!}\mathfrak{P}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}j_{\alpha,\beta}{}_{!}j_{\alpha,\beta}^{!}\mathfrak{P}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}j_{\alpha}{}_{!}j_{\alpha}^{!}\mathfrak{P}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathfrak{P}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

is a finite exact sequence of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
In fact, (
23
) is a finite
resolution of
\mathfrak{P}
by (globally) contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
Once again, we use the symbol
j^{!}
as simplified notation for
the functor
\mathfrak{P}\longmapsto\mathfrak{P}|_{Y}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}|_{Y}}
of restriction of locally contraherent cosheaves
of
\mathcal{A}
-modules to an open subscheme
Y\subset X
with the open
immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.

So (
23
) is a finite exact
sequence in the exact category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
Whenever the
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathfrak{P}
belongs
to one of the exact subcategories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
,

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, or

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
, the
Čech resolution (
23
)
is a finite exact sequence in the respective exact category.

3.8.
Projection formulas

Let
X
be a scheme and
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme such that
the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is affine.
Then, for any quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{A}
on
X
and any
quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{N}
on
Y
there is a natural isomorphism

(24)

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}j_{*}\mathcal{N}\simeq j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{N})

of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra on
X
,
then (
24
) is an isomorphism
of quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.

Let
\mathbf{W}
be an open covering of
X
and
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
be the restriction
of
\mathbf{W}
to
Y
, as in
Sections
2.5

and
3.5
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-module on
X
that is very flat
as a quasi-coherent sheaf in the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure,
and let
\mathfrak{Q}
be a
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
-locally contraherent cosheaf on
Y
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

(25)

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},j_{!}\mathfrak{Q})\simeq j_{!}\Cohom_{Y}(j^{*}\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{Q})

of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X

(cf.
[
46
, formula (3.21) in Section 3.8]
).

Indeed, for any affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to

\mathbf{W}
, one has

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},f_{!}\mathfrak{Q})[U]=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{A}(U),\mathfrak{Q}[Y\cap U])\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y\cap U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y\cap U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{A}(U),\>\mathfrak{Q}[Y\cap U])\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y\cap U)}(\mathcal{A}(Y\cap U),\mathfrak{Q}[Y\cap U])=(j_{!}\Cohom_{Y}(j^{*}\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{Q}))[U].

Similarly, a natural
isomorphism (
25
) of
locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X
holds
for any quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{A}
on
X
that is flat as
a quasi-coherent sheaf in the left
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure and any
locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
-contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
on
Y
.
Finally, for any quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{A}
on
X
and any
locally injective
\mathbf{W}|_{Y}
-locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{I}
on
Y
,
there is a natural isomorphism

(26)

\Cohom_{X}(\mathcal{A},j_{!}\mathfrak{I})\simeq j_{!}\Cohom_{Y}(j^{*}\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{I})

of locally cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X

(cf.
[
46
, formula (3.22) in Section 3.8]
).

If
\mathcal{A}
is a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra on
X
,
then (
25
)
and (
26
) are
isomorphisms of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.

4.
Some Antilocal Classes

The concept of
antilocality
was defined in
the paper
[
54
]
in the context of commutative algebra, i. e.,
affine schemes.
For quasi-compact semi-separated schemes, it was worked out
in
[
46
, Sections B.3–B.6]
.
The construction on which this concept is based goes back
to
[
17
, proof of Lemma A.1]
.

The results of
Sections
4.1
–
4.3
, concerning quasi-coherent
sheaves, provide three different
\mathcal{A}
-module versions/generalizations
of most results of
[
46
, Section 4.1]
.
The results of
Sections
4.5
–
4.7
, concerning locally
contraherent cosheaves, provide three different
\mathcal{A}
-module
versions/generalizations of most results of
[
46
, Section 4.3]
.

4.1.
Antilocality of
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules

Let
X
be a scheme.
We denote by
\Ext_{X}^{*}({-},{-})
the Ext groups computed in
the abelian category of quasi-coherent sheaves
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

A quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is said to be

very flat

[
46
, Section 1.10]
if the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module

\mathcal{F}(U)
is very flat for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering of the scheme
X

[
46
, Lemma 1.2.6(a) and Section 1.10]
.

The class of very flat quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
is closed under
extensions, kernels of epimorphisms, direct summands, and infinite
direct sums (see Section
1.3
and
Lemma
1.7
(c)).
The class of very flat quasi-coherent sheaves on schemes is preserved
by the inverse images with respect to all morphisms of schemes
(by Lemma
1.11
(b)) and the direct
images with respect to very flat affine morphisms of schemes
(in the sense of Section
2.5
;
by Lemma
1.11
(c)).

A quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{C}
on
X
is said to be

contraadjusted

[
46
, Section 2.5]
if
\Ext_{X}^{1}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0

for all very flat quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
X
.
The class of contraadjusted quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
is closed
under extensions, direct summands, and infinite
products
[
15
, Corollary 8.3]
,
[
16
, Corollary A.2]
.
On a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme
X
, it is also closed
under cokernels of monomorphisms
[
46
, Corollary 4.1.2(c)]
.

The class of contraadjusted quasi-coherent sheaves on schemes is
preserved by the direct images with respect to quasi-compact
quasi-separated morphisms of schemes
[
46
, Section 2.5]
,

[
54
, Lemma 1.7(c)]
.
For more substantial results about very flat and contraadjusted
quasi-coherent sheaves on quasi-compact semi-separated schemes,
see
[
46
, Corollary 4.1.4]
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X

(in the sense of the definition in
Section
3.1
).
We will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X

(in the sense of Section
3.3
)
is
X
-contraadjusted
if the underlying quasi-coherent sheaf
(of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules) of
\mathcal{C}
is contraadjusted.

Furthermore, we will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}

on
X
is
very flaprojective
if the
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{F}(U)

is
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-very flaprojective (in the sense of
Section
1.6
) for all affine open
subschemes
U\subset X
.
By Lemma
1.37
(b),
it suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering of the scheme
X
.
The class of very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is
closed under extensions, kernels of epimorphisms, direct summands,
and infinite direct sums in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

(see Section
1.6

and Lemma
1.30
(b)).

Now let
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme with the open immersion
morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.
Then, by the definition, the inverse image functor
\mathcal{F}\longmapsto\mathcal{F}|_{Y}=j^{*}\mathcal{F}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
to very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
.
Assuming that the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is affine,
the direct image functor
j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes
very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
to very
flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
(by
Lemma
1.36
(a)).

Given a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over a scheme
X
, we
denote by
\Ext_{\mathcal{A}}^{*}({-},{-})
the Ext groups computed in the abelian
category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
of quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
X
be a semi-separated scheme,
U\subset X
be an affine
open subscheme with the open immersion morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
,
and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be a very flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-module
on
U
and
\mathcal{C}
be an
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent left

\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
.
Then one has

(a)

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{C})=0
;

(b)
assuming that
X
is also quasi-compact,

\Ext^{n}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Part (a): the equivalence of categories
\mathcal{A}|_{U}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{A}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}

identifies very flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-modules
on
U
with
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}(U)
-very flaprojective left
\mathcal{A}(U)
-modules.
By Remark
1.32
,
the
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}(U)
-very flaprojective
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{G}(U)

is a direct summand of a transfinitely iterated extension of

\mathcal{A}(U)
-modules of the form
\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}F
, where
F
ranges
over very flat
\mathcal{O}(U)
-modules.
The open immersion morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
is affine, so the direct
image functor
j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
preserves extensions
and inductive limits, hence also transfinitely iterated extensions.
In view of the Eklof lemma
[
20
, Lemma 1]
,
[
61
, Lemma 7.5]
,
it suffices to consider the case when
\mathcal{G}(U)=\mathcal{A}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}F
.

Denoting by
\mathcal{F}
the very flat quasi-coherent sheaf on
U

corresponding to the
\mathcal{O}(U)
-module
F
, we have

\mathcal{G}\simeq\mathcal{A}|_{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathcal{F}
.
By the projection
formula (
24
), we have

j_{*}(\mathcal{G})=j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}j_{*}\mathcal{F}
.
Notice that
j_{*}\mathcal{F}
is a very flat quasi-coherent sheaf on
X

(as
j
is a very flat affine morphism).

It remains to point out that the functor
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}\,\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, which is well-defined by
Lemma
3.4
(b),
is left adjoint to the forgetful functor
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

(as mentioned in Section
3.3
).
By
[
54
, Lemma 1.7(e)]
, we have an
\Ext^{1}
adjunction
isomorphism
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{H},\>\mathcal{C})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{C})
for any flat quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{H}
and any
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
.
Taking
\mathcal{H}=j_{*}\mathcal{F}
, we arrive to the desired
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}
vanishing
conclusion.

Part (b): the category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is a Grothendieck abelian category,
so it has enough injective objects.
The
\Ext^{1}
adjunction isomorphism mentioned in the previous paragraph
implies that all injective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules are

X
-contraadjusted (see
Section
4.3
below for
a stronger assertion).
The class of all
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is
closed under cokernels of monomorphisms in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, since the class
of all contraadjusted quasi-coherent sheaves is closed under cokernels
of monomorphisms in
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

[
46
, Corollary 4.1.2(c)]
.
Therefore, part (b) follows from part (a) by
[
61
, Lemma 7.1]
.
∎

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then, for any very flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}

and any
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}

on
X
, one has
\Ext^{n}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

The argument is based on the Čech coresolution and
Lemma
4.1
(b).
Let
X=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}U_{\alpha}
be a finite affine open covering
of the scheme
X
.
Then the Čech
coresolution (
22
)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{F}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}j_{\alpha}{}_{*}j_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{F}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}j_{\alpha,\beta}{}_{*}j_{\alpha,\beta}^{*}\mathcal{F}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muj_{1,\dotsc,N}{}_{*}j_{1,\dotsc,N}^{*}\mathcal{F}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

is a finite coresolution of
\mathcal{F}
by the direct images of
very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-modules from
affine open subschemes
U=U_{\alpha_{1}}\cap\dotsb\cap U_{\alpha_{k}}\subset X
in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
By Lemma
4.1
(b),
we have
\Ext^{n}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
n\geq 1
, where

j\colon U\longrightarrow X
is the open immersion morphism.
The assertion of the corollary follows.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Then there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{F}
is a very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a finitely iterated extension of the direct images of

U_{\alpha}
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules
in the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

This is a version of
[
17
, proof of Lemma A.1]
,

[
54
, Proposition 4.3]
,
[
46
, Lemma 4.1.1 or
Proposition B.4.4]
, etc.
The same construction, proceeding (roughly speaking) by induction on
the number of affine open subschemes in the finite affine open covering

X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
of the scheme
X
, is applicable in
the situation at hand as well.
The local nature of the notion of a very flaprojective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-module plays a key role.
It is also important that the class of very flaprojective
quasi-coherent modules is preserved by the direct images with respect
to affine open immersions of schemes, as well as extensions and
kernels of epimorphisms.
Of course, one also needs to use
Theorem
1.34
(a).
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Denote by
j_{\alpha}\colon U_{\alpha}\longrightarrow X
the open immersion morphisms.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then any quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X
is a submodule of
a finite direct sum
\bigoplus_{\alpha}j_{\alpha}{}_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
for
some
U_{\alpha}
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
on
U_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

For every index
\alpha
, consider the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-module
j_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{M}
on
U_{\alpha}
, and pick
a
U_{\alpha}
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-module

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
together with an injective morphism of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules
j_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}

on
U_{\alpha}
.
For example, it suffices to take
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
to be an injective
object of the module category
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{A}(U_{\alpha}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, and recall that all injective

\mathcal{A}(U_{\alpha})
-modules are contraadjusted over
\mathcal{O}(U_{\alpha})
(cf. the proof of Lemma
1.29
(a)).
By adjunction, we obtain a morphism of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha}j_{\alpha}{}_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
, which is injective
because it is injective in restriction to every
U_{\alpha}
.
∎

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
In this context:

(a)
there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{F}
is a very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is an
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module;

(b)
there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{C}
is an
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module;

(c)
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
is

X
-contraadjusted if and only if it is a direct summand of a finitely
iterated extension of the direct images of
U_{\alpha}
-contraadjusted
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules in the abelian category

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

Part (a) follows immediately from
Lemma
4.3
(since the class
of contraadjusted quasi-coherent sheaves is preserved by extensions and
direct images with respect to quasi-compact quasi-separated morphisms).
Part (b) follows from part (a) and
Lemma
4.4
by virtue
of a classical argument known as the
Salce lemma

[
68
]
,

[
20
, proof of Theorem 10]
,
[
46
, Lemma B.1.1(b) or proof of
Lemma 4.1.3]
.

To prove part (c), one needs to observe that the proof of part (b)
actually provides a more precise result: in part (b), one can choose
the short exact sequence so that
\mathcal{C}
is a a finitely iterated extension
of the direct images of
U_{\alpha}
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules.
Now let
\mathcal{D}
be an
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
on
X
.
Choose a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
,
where
\mathcal{C}
is a finitely iterated extension of the direct images from

U_{\alpha}
as above, while
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Then
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{D})=0
by
Corollary
4.2
, hence
\mathcal{D}
is a direct
summand of
\mathcal{C}
, as desired.
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme, and let
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
In this context:

(a)
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}
is very
flaprojective if and only if
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})\allowbreak=0
for all

X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{C}
;

(b)
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is

X
-contraadjusted if and only if
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})\allowbreak=0

for all very flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

The “only if” assertions are provided by
Corollary
4.2
.
The “if” implications follow from the same corollary and
Theorem
4.5
(a–b)
by the argument similar to the one in the proof of
Theorem
4.5
(c);
see
[
46
, Lemma B.1.2]
.
The point is that the classes of very flaprojective and

X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules are closed under
direct summands in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

The assertions of
Theorem
4.5
(a–b)
and Corollaries
4.2

and
4.6

can be summarized by saying that the pair of classes (very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules,
X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules) is a hereditary complete cotorsion pair in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
On the other hand,
Theorem
4.5
(c) is
an extra piece of information which one obtains from the proof of
Theorem
4.5
(a–b)
presented above.

The assertion of
Theorem
4.5
(c)
can be expressed by saying that the class of
X
-contraadjusted
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on a quasi-compact semi-separated
scheme
X
is
antilocal
in the sense of
[
54
, Section 4]
.
This is the main result of this
Section
4.1
for our purposes
in this paper.
The whole machinery of very flaprojective modules was developed in
Section
1.6
with the sole aim to prove
this result (which does not mention very flaprojective modules).

4.2.
Antilocality of
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

This section is a slight variation upon the previous one.
All the proofs are similar, and therefore omitted or replaced by
references.

Let
X
be a scheme.
A quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is said to be
flat
if
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\mathcal{F}(U)
is flat for all affine open subschemes

U\subset X
.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering of the scheme
X

(see
[
46
, Lemma 1.8.3]
for a more general relative version
of this assertion).

The class of flat quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
is closed under
extensions, kernels of epimorphisms, direct summands, and infinite
direct sums.
The class of flat quasi-coherent sheaves on schemes is preserved
by the inverse images with respect to all morphisms of schemes
and the direct images with respect to flat affine morphisms of schemes.

A quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{C}
on
X
is said to be

cotorsion

[
23
]
if
\Ext_{X}^{1}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all flat
quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
X
.
The class of cotorsion quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
is closed
under extensions, direct summands, and infinite
products
[
15
, Corollary 8.3]
,
[
16
, Corollary A.2]
.
On a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme
X
, it is also closed
under cokernels of monomorphisms
[
46
, Corollary 4.1.9(c)]
.

The class of cotorsion quasi-coherent sheaves on schemes is preserved
by the direct images with respect to quasi-compact quasi-separated
morphisms of schemes
[
46
, Section 2.5]
,

[
54
, Lemma 1.7(c)]
.
For more substantial results about flat and cotorsion quasi-coherent
sheaves on quasi-compact semi-separated schemes,
see
[
23
, Corollary 4.2]
,
[
41
, Section 2.4]
,

[
17
, Lemma A.1]
, or
[
46
, Corollary 4.1.11]
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
We will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
is

X
-cotorsion
if the underlying quasi-coherent sheaf
(of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules) of
\mathcal{C}
is cotorsion.

Furthermore, we will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}

on
X
is
flaprojective
if the
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{F}(U)

is
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-flaprojective (in the sense of
Section
1.6
) for all affine open
subschemes
U\subset X
.
By Lemma
1.37
(a),
it suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering of the scheme
X
.
The class of flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is
closed under extensions, kernels of epimorphisms, direct summands,
and infinite direct sums in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

(see Section
1.6

and Lemma
1.29
(b)).

Now let
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme with the open immersion
morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.
Then, by the definition, the inverse image functor
\mathcal{F}\longmapsto\mathcal{F}|_{Y}=j^{*}\mathcal{F}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
to flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
.
Assuming that the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is affine,
the direct image functor
j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes
flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
to flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
(by
Lemma
1.35
(a)).

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
X
be a semi-separated scheme,
U\subset X
be an affine
open subscheme with the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
,
and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathcal{G}
be a flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-module on
U

and
\mathcal{C}
be an
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
.
Then one has

(a)

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{C})=0
;

(b)
assuming that
X
is also quasi-compact,

\Ext^{n}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Similar to
Lemma
4.1
.
In part (b), one observes that all injective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules are actually
X
-cotorsion (on any scheme
X
;
see Section
4.3
below for
a more general assertion).
Then one also has to use
[
46
, Lemma 4.1.9(c)]
.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then, for any flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}

and any
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
,
one has
\Ext^{n}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Similar to Corollary
4.2
, and based
on Lemma
4.8
(b).
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Then there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{F}
is a flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a finitely iterated extension of the direct images of

U_{\alpha}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules
in the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
4.3

and using Theorem
1.33
(a).
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Denote by
j_{\alpha}\colon U_{\alpha}\longrightarrow X
the open immersion morphisms.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then any quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X
is a submodule of
a finite direct sum
\bigoplus_{\alpha}j_{\alpha}{}_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
for
some
U_{\alpha}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
on
U_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

This is a slightly stronger version of
Lemma
4.4
,
provable in the same way.
∎

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
In this context:

(a)
there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{F}
is a flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is an
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module;

(b)
there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{C}
is an
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module;

(c)
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
is
X
-cotorsion
if and only if it is a direct summand of a finitely iterated extension
of the direct images of
U_{\alpha}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules in the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

Similar to
Theorem
4.5
,
and based on Corollary
4.9
and
Lemmas
4.10
–
4.11
.
∎

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme, and let
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
In this context:

(a)
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}
is flaprojective
if and only if
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
X
-cotorsion
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{C}
;

(b)
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is

X
-cotorsion if and only if
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
flaprojective quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

Similar to
Corollary
4.6
.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

Similarly to Remark
4.7
,
the assertions of
Theorem
4.12
(a–b)
and Corollaries
4.9

and
4.13
can be summarized
by saying that the pair of classes (flaprojective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules,
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules) is
a hereditary complete cotorsion pair in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
On the other hand,
Theorem
4.12
(c) is
an extra piece of information which one obtains from our proof of
Theorem
4.12
(a–b).

The assertion of
Theorem
4.12
(c)
can be expressed by saying that the class of
X
-cotorsion
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on a quasi-compact semi-separated
scheme
X
is
antilocal
in the sense of
[
54
, Section 4]
.
This is the main result of this
Section
4.2
for our purposes
in this paper.
The whole machinery of flaprojective modules was developed in
Section
1.6
with the sole aim to prove
this result (which does not mention flaprojective modules).

4.3.
Antilocality of
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
over
X
.
Recall that, according to
Section
3.4
,
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module is said to be
flat

(or
\mathcal{A}
-flat
) if the
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{F}(U)
is flat
for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
.
It suffices to check this condition for affine open subschemes
U

belonging to any chosen affine open covering of the scheme
X

(see Lemma
1.23
(a)).

The class of flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is closed under
extensions, kernels of epimorphisms, direct summands, and infinite
direct sums in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
Given an open subscheme
Y\subset X
with the open immersion morphism

j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the inverse image functor
j^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules to flat
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules.
If the open immersion morphism
j
is affine, then the direct image
functor
j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes flat quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules to flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules (as one can see
from Corollary
1.22
(a)).

A quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is said to be

\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion
(or just
cotorsion
) if

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all flat quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules

\mathcal{F}
on
X
.
The class of
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is closed
under extensions, direct summands, and direct products in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

[
15
, Corollary 8.3]
,
[
16
, Corollary A.2]
.
For a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}
over an affine scheme
U
,
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{B}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
U
is
\mathcal{B}
-cotorsion if
and only if the
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module
\mathcal{C}(U)
is cotorsion.

Given an open subscheme
Y\subset X
with a quasi-compact open
immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
, the direct image functor

j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-cotorsion
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules to
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules.
Indeed, the direct image functor
j_{*}
is right adjoint to the inverse
image functor
j^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, which is exact.
Therefore, for any quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{M}
on
X
and
any quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-module
\mathcal{N}
on
Y
there is
a natural injective map of abelian groups
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{M},j_{*}\mathcal{N})\longrightarrow\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}|_{Y}}(j^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})

[
54
, Lemma 1.7(b)]
.
So
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}|_{Y}}(j^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})=0
implies
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{M},j_{*}\mathcal{N})=0
.

All
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules are
X
-cotorsion.
Indeed, the functor
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}\,\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

takes flat quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
to flat quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules.
The functor
\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}
is also left adjoint to the exact
forgetful functor
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
Therefore, for any flat quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{H}
and any
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
there is a natural
isomorphism of abelian groups
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{H},\>\mathcal{C})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{C})

[
54
, Lemma 1.7(e)]
, as mentioned in
the proof of
Lemma
4.1
.
So
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{H},\>\mathcal{C})=0
implies

\Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{C})=0
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with a finite
affine open covering
X=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
and
\mathcal{M}
be
a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Then there exists a short exact sequence of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules
0\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow 0
on
X

such that
\mathcal{F}
is a flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is
a finitely iterated extension of the direct images of

\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
-modules
in the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

Similar to the proofs of
Lemmas
4.3

and
4.10
.
One needs to use various properties of flat quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules listed above, such as the local nature of the definition,
closedness under extensions and kernels of epimorphisms, and
preservation by the direct images with respect to affine open
immersions.
One also has to use Theorem
1.4
(a)
for the rings
R=\mathcal{A}(U_{\alpha})
.
∎

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
X
be quasi-compact semi-separated scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
In this context:

(a)
A quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
is

\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion if and only if the functor
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}({-},\mathcal{C})
takes
takes short exact sequences of flat quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
to short exact sequences of abelian groups.

(b)
A quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
is

\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion if and only if
\Ext_{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all
all flat quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{F}
on
X
.

(c)
The class of
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
is closed under cokernels of monomorphisms in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Proof.

This is similar to
[
46
, Corollaries 4.1.2 and 4.1.9]

and based on the fact that every quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module is
a quotient of a flat one (which is a weak version of
Lemma
4.15
) together with
the fact that the class of flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is
closed under kernels of epimorphisms.
∎
