(a),
the presheaf
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}M
extends uniquely to a quasi-coherent sheaf
of
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
, which we will denote also by
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}M
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Then the Hom group
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})
has a natural left
R
-module
structure, and the adjunction isomorphism

\Hom_{R}(M,\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C}))\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M},\>\mathcal{C})

holds for any left
R
-module
M
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module with a right action of
a ring
R
by left
\mathcal{A}
-module endomorphisms,
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module, and
M
be a left
R
-module.
Let
n\geq 0
be an integer such that
\Ext^{i}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for all

0<i\leq n
.
Assume that
\Tor_{i}^{R}(\mathcal{F}(U),M)=0
for all affine open subschemes

U\subset X
and all
0<i\leq n
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of abelian groups

\Ext^{n}_{R}(M,\Hom_{X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C}))\simeq\Ext_{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}M,\>\mathcal{C}).

Proof.

This is a quasi-coherent sheaf version of
Lemma
1.1
and a slight generalization
of
[
46
, Lemma 2.5.1]
, provable in the same way.
∎

Assume that the scheme
X
is quasi-separated.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule and
\mathcal{C}
be
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
.
The copresheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
on
\mathbf{B}
is
defined by the rule

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]=\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})

for all affine open subschemes
U\subset X
with the open immersion
morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
.
Notice that
j^{*}\mathcal{E}
is a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-module
with a right action of the ring
R=\mathcal{B}(U)
, hence
j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}
is
a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module with a right action of
the same ring.
This right action of the ring
\mathcal{B}(U)
on the quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module
j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{C}
induces a left action of
\mathcal{B}(U)
on
the group
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
, making
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]

a left
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module.

Furthermore, let
V\subset U\subset X
be a pair of affine open
subschemes with open immersion morphisms
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
,
h\colon V\longrightarrow U
, and
k=jh\colon V\longrightarrow X
.
Then there is a natural adjunction morphism

j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muj_{*}h_{*}h^{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\simeq k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E}.

This morphism of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E}
induces a map of abelian groups

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
, providing
the corestriction map
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[V]\longrightarrow\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]

in the copresheaf
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
on
\mathbf{B}
.

The contraherence axiom (i) from
Section
2.2
always holds
for (the underlying copresheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules of) the copresheaf
of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
on
\mathbf{B}
.
Indeed, one computes

\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U])=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}))\\
\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\>\mathcal{C})\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})=\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[V].

Here we are using the natural isomorphism of quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules

(27)

(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\simeq j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V))\simeq j_{*}h_{*}h^{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\simeq k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E}

on
X
.
The isomorphism
(j_{*}\mathcal{G})\otimes_{R}H\simeq j_{*}(\mathcal{G}\otimes_{R}H)
holds
for any quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{G}
on
U
with a right action
of a ring
R
and any flat left
R
-module
H
, and only requires
the morphism
j
to be quasi-compact and quasi-separated
(see
[
46
, Section 2.5]
); when the morphism
j
is affine,
this isomorphism holds for an arbitrary left
R
-module
H
.
On the other hand, the isomorphism
j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\simeq h_{*}h^{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}
holds due to quasi-coherence of the sheaf

\mathcal{E}
with respect to its right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

The lemma below provides sufficient conditions for
the contraadjustedness axiom (ii) from
Section
2.2
and its stronger
versions to be satisfied for the copresheaf
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
.
See
[
46
, Section 0.10 of the Introduction]

and
[
55
, Sections 5.6 and 8.7]
for a relevant discussion of
partially defined functors between exact categories.

We will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule is

\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective
it is is very flaprojective (in
the sense of Section
4.1
)
as a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module.
The terminology
\mathcal{A}
-flaprojective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
and
\mathcal{A}
-flat quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
has a similar meaning (with the references to
the definitions in Sections
4.2

and
4.3
).
In particular, any quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over
X
is
naturally an
\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective (hence also
\mathcal{A}
-flaprojective
and
\mathcal{A}
-flat) quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule
(cf. Remark
1.28
).

Now let
\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodule.
We will say that
\mathcal{E}
is
robustly flaprojective
if, for every
affine open subscheme
U\subset X
, the
A
-
R
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}(U)

is
(A/R,R)
-robustly flaprojective in the sense of the definition
in Section
1.7
.
Here we use the notation
A=\mathcal{A}(U)
and
R=\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
.
In view of Lemma
1.40

(for
K=R
), it suffices to check this condition for affine open
subschemes
U\subset X
belonging to any chosen affine open covering
of the scheme
X
.
For example, for any quasi-coherent quasi-module
\mathcal{F}
on
X
that
is flat as a quasi-coherent sheaf with respect to its left

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure, the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodule

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{F}
is robustly flaprojective.
We will say that a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is

\mathcal{A}
-robustly flaprojective
if
\mathcal{E}
is robustly flaprojective
as a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodule.

When no confusion is likely to arise between the left and right module
structures, we will also speak of
\mathcal{B}
-flat
quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules, meaning that the underlying quasi-coherent
right
\mathcal{B}
-module is flat.
Finally, we will say that a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{J}

is
injective
if it is injective as an object of the Grothendieck
abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
The definitions of
X
-contraadjusted
,
X
-cotorsion
,
and
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules can be also
found in Sections
4.1
–
4.3
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
a scheme
X
, let
\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule,
and let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{J}
be a quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules.
Let
U\subset X
be an affine open subscheme.
In this context:

(a)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-compact and semi-separated,
the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-very
flaprojective, and the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is

X
-contraadjusted, then the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]
is contraadjusted;

(b)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-compact and semi-separated,
the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-robustly
flaprojective, and the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is

X
-cotorsion, then the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]

is cotorsion;

(c)
if the scheme
X
is semi-separated, the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-flat, and the quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion, then the
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[U]
is cotorsion;

(d)
if the scheme
X
is semi-separated, the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{B}
-flat and the quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{J}
is injective, then the
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})[U]
is injective;

(e)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-separated and the quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{J}
is injective, then the
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})[U]
is cotorsion.

Proof.

Notice first of all that flatness of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
and
\mathcal{B}
-modules is preserved by the restrictions to open subschemes
and the direct images from affine immersions of open subschemes.
The same applies to flaprojectivity and very flaprojectivity
(see Sections
4.1
–
4.3
).
To make the open immersion morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
affine for
an affine open subscheme
U\subset X
, the semi-separatedness assumption
is imposed in parts (a–d).

In part (a), we let
G
be a very flat
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module and compute

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}(G,\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}))\\
\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}G,\>\mathcal{C})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}G),\>\mathcal{C})=0.

Here the first isomorphism holds by
Lemma
5.16
(for
n=1

and
R=\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
), the second isomorphism is true because
j
is
an affine morphism (or because
G
is a flat
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module),
and the final vanishing holds since
\mathcal{E}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}G
is
an
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-very flaprojective
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
(by Lemma
1.41
(b)).
It is helpful to notice that the class of very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is preserved by the direct images from
affine open immersion morphisms (by
Lemma
1.36
(a), as
mentioned in Section
4.1
),
and one has
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for any very flaprojective
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}
and any
X
-contraadjusted
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
on
X
by
Corollary
4.2
.

Alternatively, instead of referring to
Lemma
1.41
(b), one can say
that it suffices to consider the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-modules
G=\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)

for affine open subschemes
V\subset U
.
Then
\mathcal{E}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\simeq\mathcal{E}(V)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{E}(U)
is an
\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-very flaprojective

\mathcal{A}(U)
-module by
Lemma
1.35
(a).

The same computation works in part (b) for a flat

\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module
G
.
In this context, instead of referring to
Lemma
1.41
, one says
that the
\mathcal{A}(U)
-module
\mathcal{E}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}G
is

\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-flaprojective since the

\mathcal{A}(U)
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}(U)
is

(\mathcal{A}(U)/\mathcal{O}_{X}(U),\mathcal{O}_{X}(U))
-robustly flaprojective by assumption.
The class of flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules is preserved
by the direct images from affine open immersion morphisms
(by Lemma
1.35
(a), as
mentioned in Section
4.2
), and
one has
\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})=0
for any flaprojective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}
and any
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module

\mathcal{C}
on
X
by Corollary
4.9
.

In part (c), we let
G
be a flat left
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module and compute

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{B}(U)}(G,\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}))\\
\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}G,\>\mathcal{C})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}G),\>\mathcal{C})=0.

Here the first isomorphism holds by
Lemma
5.16
(for
n=1

and
R=\mathcal{B}(U)
), the second isomorphism is true because
j
is
an affine morphism (or because
G
is a flat
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module),
and the final vanishing holds since
\mathcal{E}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}G
is a flat

\mathcal{A}(U)
-module.
Notice that the class of flat quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}(U)
-modules is
preserved by the direct images from affine open immersion morphisms
(as mentioned in Section
4.3
).

In part (d), we let
M
be an arbitrary left
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module
and compute

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{B}(U)}(M,\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J}))\\
\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}M,\>\mathcal{J})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}M),\>\mathcal{J})=0.

Here the first isomorphism holds by
Lemma
5.16
(for
n=1

and
R=\mathcal{B}(U)
) and the second isomorphism is true because
j
is
an affine morphism.
The condition of
\mathcal{B}
-flatness of the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is needed in order to satisfy
the Tor vanishing assumption in
Lemma
5.16
.

In part (e), we let
G
be a flat left
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module and compute

\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{B}(U)}(G,\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J}))\\
\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}G,\>\mathcal{J})\simeq\Ext^{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}G),\>\mathcal{J})=0.

Here the first isomorphism holds by
Lemma
5.16
(for
n=1

and
R=\mathcal{B}(U)
) and the second isomorphism is true because
G
is
a flat
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module.
The condition of flatness of the
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module
G
is also needed
in order to satisfy the Tor vanishing assumption in
Lemma
5.16
.
∎

Under any one of the assumptions (a–e) of
Lemma
5.17
, we have proved, in
particular, that the copresheaf
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
or

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
on
\mathbf{B}
, viewed as a copresheaf of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, satisfies the contraadjustedness
axiom (ii) from Section
2.2
.
Recall that any cotorsion
\mathcal{B}(U)
-module is also cotorsion
(hence contraadjusted) as a
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module by
Lemma
1.6
(a).
The contraherence axiom (i) was established above.
By Lemma
2.4

and Theorem
2.1
(b),
the copresheaf
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
or
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
on
\mathbf{B}

extends uniquely to a contraherent cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
,
which we will denote also by
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
or

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
.

To summarize:

•

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
is a contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module whenever
\mathcal{E}

is
\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective,
\mathcal{C}
is
X
-contraadjusted, and
X
is
quasi-compact and semi-separated;

•

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
is an
X
-locally cotorsion contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module whenever
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-robustly flaprojective,
\mathcal{C}

is
X
-cotorsion, and
X
is quasi-compact and semi-separated;

•

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
is a
\mathcal{B}
-locally cotorsion contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module whenever
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-flat,
\mathcal{C}
is
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion,
and
X
is semi-separated;

•

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
is a
\mathcal{B}
-locally injective contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module whenever
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{B}
-flat,
\mathcal{J}
is injective,
and
X
is semi-separated;

•

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
is a
\mathcal{B}
-locally cotorsion contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module whenever
\mathcal{J}
is injective and
X
is quasi-separated.

Here the definitions of a contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module and
an
X
-locally cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module were given in
Section
3.3
, while the definitions
of a
\mathcal{B}
-locally cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module and
a
\mathcal{B}
-locally injective contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module can be found
in Section
3.4
.

Denote by
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
the Grothendieck abelian category of
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules on
X
.
Denote further by
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-vflp}}}}
the full subcategory
of
\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules,
by
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-rflp}}}}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
-robustly
flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules,
by
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
-flat
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules, and by

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{B}
-flat
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
.
All the four full subcategories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-vflp}}}}
,

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-rflp}}}}
,
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}
, and

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
are closed under
extensions and kernels of epimorphisms in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
, so they
inherit exact category structures from the abelian exact structure
of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
.

For any abelian category
\mathsf{A}
, we denote by
\mathsf{A}^{\mathsf{inj}}\subset\mathsf{A}

the full subcategory of injective objects in
\mathsf{A}
.
The additive category
\mathsf{A}^{\mathsf{inj}}
is endowed with the split exact
category structure.

It is clear from the constructions and arguments above that, in
the context of each of the parts (a–d) of
Lemma
5.17
, the related functor

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}
is exact as a functor of two arguments between
the respective exact categories of quasi-coherent (bi)modules and
contraherent modules over quasi-algebras over
X
.
So, under the respective assumptions on the scheme
X
, we have
exact functors of two arguments, all of them denoted by

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}({-},{-})
:

(28)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-vflp}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}};

(29)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-rflp}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}};

(30)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}};

(31)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}.

5.6.
Cosections of the
\fHom
cosheaf

The aim of this section is prove the following version
of
[
46
, Lemma 2.5.2]
.
For affine open subschemes
Y\subset X
, the assertions of the lemma
hold by the definition of
\fHom
; the point of the lemma is
that there is often a similar isomorphism for
nonaffine
open
subschemes
Y\subset X
.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme in
X
with
the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
X
,
let
\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule, and let
\mathcal{C}

and
\mathcal{J}
be quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
In this context:

(a)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-compact and semi-separated,
the open immersion morphism
j
is affine, the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective, and
the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{C}
is
X
-contraadjusted,
then there is a natural isomorphism of left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[Y]\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
;

(b)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-compact and semi-separated,
the open immersion morphism
j
is affine, the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-robustly flaprojective, and
the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module is
X
-cotorsion, then
there is a natural isomorphism of left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[Y]\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
;

(c)
if the scheme
X
is semi-separated, the scheme
Y

is quasi-compact, the open immersion morphism
j
is affine,
the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
is
\mathcal{A}
-flat, and
the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module is
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion, then
there is a natural isomorphism of left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[Y]\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
;

(d)
if the scheme
X
is quasi-separated, the scheme
Y

is quasi-compact, and the quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{J}
is
injective, then there is a natural isomorphism of left

\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})[Y]\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
.

Proof.

First of all, the
\Hom
group
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
or

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
is a left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-module because
the ring
\mathcal{B}(Y)
acts on the right on the quasi-coherent left

\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-module
j^{*}\mathcal{E}
on
Y
, hence also on the quasi-coherent
left
\mathcal{A}
-module
j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}
on
X
.
The group of cosections
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[Y]
or

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})[Y]
is a left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-module since

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
or
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
is a contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module, hence, in particular, a cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
on
X
(according to the previous
Section
5.5
).

The argument is similar to the proof of
[
46
, Lemma 2.5.2]
.
In all cases (a–d), the scheme
X
is, at least, quasi-separated,
and the open subscheme
Y
is, at least, quasi-compact.
Let
Y=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}
be a finite affine open
covering of
Y
.
Denote by
k_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{i}}
the open immersions

V_{\alpha_{1}}\cap\dotsb\cap V_{\alpha_{i}}\longrightarrow Y
.
For any quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{G}
on
Y
, the Čech
coresolution (
22
)

(32)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{G}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}k_{\alpha}{}_{*}k_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}k_{\alpha,\beta}{}_{*}k_{\alpha,\beta}^{*}\mathcal{G}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muk_{1,\dotsc,N}{}_{*}k_{1,\dotsc,N}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

is a finite exact sequence of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
.
Put
\mathcal{G}=j^{*}\mathcal{E}
.

When the open immersion morphism
j\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is affine
(cases (a–c)), the functor
j_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is exact.
Denote by
h_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{i}}=jk_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{i}}

the open immersions
V_{\alpha_{1}}\cap\dotsb\cap V_{\alpha_{i}}\longrightarrow X
.
Applying the functor
j_{*}
to the exact
sequence (
32
), we obtain
a finite exact sequence of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules with a right
action of the ring
\mathcal{B}(Y)

(33)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muj_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}h_{\alpha}{}_{*}h_{\alpha}^{*}\mathcal{E}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}h_{\alpha,\beta}{}_{*}h_{\alpha,\beta}^{*}\mathcal{E}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muh_{1,\dotsc,N}{}_{*}h_{1,\dotsc,N}^{*}\mathcal{E}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

In the case (a), the terms of
the sequence (
33
) are
very flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
by
Lemma
1.36
(a),
as per the discussion in
Section
4.1
.
Using Lemma
1.30
(b)
and arguing by induction moving from the rightmost end of the exact
sequence ot the leftmost end, one shows that the
\mathcal{A}
-modules of
cocycles in (
33
) are also
very flaprojective.
In view of Corollary
4.2
, the functor

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}({-},\mathcal{C})
transforms (
33
)
into an exact sequence of left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules.

In the case (b), the terms of
the sequence (
33
) are
flaprojective quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
by
Lemma
1.35
(a),
as per the discussion in
Section
4.2
.
Using Lemma
1.29
(b)
and Corollary
4.9
, one similarly shows
that the functor
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}({-},\mathcal{C})

takes (
33
)
to an exact sequence of left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules.

In the case (c), one arrives to the very same conclusion using
Corollary
1.22
(a),
the discussion in Section
4.3
,
and the definition of an
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module.
Indeed, the terms of
the sequence (
33
) are flat
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
in this case.

In all the cases (a–c), we have obtained a finite exact sequence of
left
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-modules ending in the terms

(34)

\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})[V_{\alpha}]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

Thus we have computed the
\mathcal{B}(Y)
-module
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
in
a way that agrees with the description of the module of cosections of
the cosheaf
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
over the open subset
Y\subset X

provided by the cosheaf axom (
9
).

The proof of part (d) is very similar to
[
46
, proof of
Lemma 2.5.2(c)]
.
We refrain from reiterating the details.
∎

5.7.
Contratensor product over a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra

This section is a partial generalization of
[
46
, Section 2.6]
.

Let
X
be a quasi-separated scheme, and let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two
quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
X
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule on
X
(as defined
in Section
5.5
, and let
\mathfrak{P}

be a cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
.
The quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{P}
on
X
, called
the
contratensor product
of
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathfrak{P}
over
\mathcal{A}
,
is constructed as the following (nonfiltered) inductive limit in
the abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

The diagram is indexed by the poset of all affine open subschemes

U\subset X
.
To every such affine open subscheme
U
with the open immersion
morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
, the diagram assigns the quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-module

(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{P}[U].

Notice that the ring
\mathcal{B}(U)
acts naturally on the right on
the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{U}
-module
j^{*}\mathcal{E}
on
U
, hence also
on the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}
on
X
.
So the tensor product, as defined in
Section
5.5
, makes sense.

To a pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X
with open
immersion morphisms
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
and
k\colon V\longrightarrow X
, the diagram
assigns the morphism of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

(35)

(k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{P}[V]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{P}[U]

constructed as follows.
By (
27
), we have

k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E}\simeq(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
, hence

(36)

(k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{P}[V]\simeq((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V))\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{P}[V]\\
\simeq((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}(\mathcal{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V))\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{P}[V]\\
\simeq((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathcal{B}(V))\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{P}[V]\simeq(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{P}[V].

The desired morphism (
35
)
is now induced by the corestriction morphism of
\mathcal{B}(U)
-modules

\mathfrak{P}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}[U]
.

So we put

\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{P}=\varinjlim\nolimits_{U}((j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{P}[U]).

Now let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
such that
the contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-module
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})
is well-defined
as per one of the sufficient conditions in
Section
5.5

(see Lemma
5.17
).
Then there is a natural (adjunction) isomorphism of abelian groups

(37)

\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{P},\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C}))\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{P},\>\mathcal{C}).

Indeed, both the abelian groups in (
37
)
are naturally isomorphic to the group of all compatible collections
of morphisms of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules

(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{P}[U]\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{C}

on
X
, or equivalently, all the compatible collections of morphisms
of
\mathcal{B}(U)
-modules

\mathfrak{P}[U]\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{C})

defined for all the affine open subschemes
U\subset X
with open
immersion morphisms
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
.
Notice that affine open subschemes form a topology base
\mathbf{B}
on
X
,
so a morphism of cosheaves of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
is uniquely
determined by an arbitrary morphism of the related copresheaves of

\mathcal{B}
-modules on
\mathbf{B}
(by
Theorem
2.1
(b)).

For a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}
over a quasi-compact
semi-separated scheme
X
, the notion of an antilocal contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-module on
X
was defined in
Sections
4.4
–
4.5
.
The notation
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for the full exact subcategory of antilocal
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules in the exact category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
was introduced in
Section
4.7
.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme, and let
\mathcal{A}
and

\mathcal{B}
be quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
X
.
Then the functor of contratensor product
\odot_{\mathcal{B}}
is exact
on the Cartesian product of the full subcategories of
\mathcal{B}
-flat
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodules
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}
and antilocal contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-modules

\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\subset(X,\mathcal{B}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.
So the functor

(38)

\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}\times\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

is exact as a functor of two arguments acting between exact/abelian
categories.

Proof.

The Grothendieck abelian category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
has enough injective
objects.
Hence it suffices to show that, for any fixed injective quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{J}\in\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}
, the functor
(\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{P})\longmapsto\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{P},\>\mathcal{J})
is exact as a functor of two arguments

(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times(\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}})^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}
.
According to the adjunction
formula (
37
) and taking into account
Lemma
5.17
(d), we have

\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{P},\>\mathcal{J})\simeq\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}(\mathfrak{P},\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})),

where
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})
is a
\mathcal{B}
-locally injective contraherent

\mathcal{B}
-module.
It remains to point out that the functor
\mathcal{E}\longmapsto\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{J})\colon(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}})^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
is exact as mentioned
in (
31
), while the functor

\Hom^{\mathcal{B}}\colon(\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}

is exact (for any open covering
\mathbf{W}
of the scheme
X
) by
Corollary
4.23
(3).
∎

5.8.
Projection formulas

This section is a very partial generalization of the results
of
[
46
, Section 3.8]
concerning the contraherent
\fHom

and contratensor product functors.
We restrict ourselves to
open immersions

f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme such that
the open immersion morphism
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is quasi-compact.
Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
X
, let

\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule on
X
, and let
\mathcal{D}

be a quasi-coherent left
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module on
Y
.

Assume that one of the five sufficient conditions of
Section
5.5

(see Lemma
5.17
)
for the existence of a contraherent cosheaf
\fHom
is satisfied
simultaneously for
\mathcal{E}
on
X
, for
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
, and for
the scheme
X
itself.
In this case, the same conditions are also satisfied for
the quasi-coherent
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-module
f^{*}\mathcal{E}
on
Y
,
for the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
f_{*}\mathcal{D}
on
X
, and for
the scheme
Y
.
In particular:

•

If the quasi-coherent
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
is

Y
-contraadjusted, then the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
f_{*}\mathcal{D}

on
X
is
X
-contraadjusted, as mentioned
in Section
4.1

and explained in
[
46
, Section 2.5]
.

•

If the quasi-coherent
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
is

Y
-cotorsion, then the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
f_{*}\mathcal{D}

on
X
is
X
-cotorsion, as mentioned
in Section
4.2

and explained in
[
46
, Section 2.5]
.

•

If the quasi-coherent
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
is

f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion, then the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
f_{*}\mathcal{D}

on
X
is
\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion, as explained
in Section
4.3
.

•

If the quasi-coherent
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{D}
on
Y
is
injective, then the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-module
f_{*}\mathcal{D}
on
X

is injective, since the functor
f_{*}\colon(f^{*}\mathcal{A}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

is right adjoint to an exact functor
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow(f^{*}\mathcal{A}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

Under the assumptions above, there is a natural isomorphism of
cosheaves of
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X

(39)

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},f_{*}\mathcal{D})\simeq f_{!}\fHom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D}),

where the cosheaf of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},f_{*}\mathcal{D})
on
X

is contraherent by Section
5.5

(hence it follows from (
39
) that the cosheaf
of
\mathcal{B}
-modules
f_{!}\fHom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})
on
X

is contraherent, too).
The formula (
39
) is our version
of
[
46
, formulas (3.23) and (3.24)]
.

Indeed, let
U\subset X
be an affine open subscheme.
Put
V=Y\cap U
, and notice that the scheme
V
is quasi-compact
(since the morphism
f
is quasi-compact by assumption).
Furthermore, the open immersion morphism
V\longrightarrow Y
is affine
whenever the scheme
X
is semi-separated (since the open immersion
morphism
U\longrightarrow X
is affine in this case).
Denote the open immersion morphisms by
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
,
j^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow Y
, and
f^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow U
.
Then we have natural isomorphisms of
\mathcal{B}(U)
-modules

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{E},f_{*}\mathcal{D})[U]=\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},f_{*}\mathcal{D})\simeq\Hom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\\
\simeq\Hom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(j^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}{}^{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\simeq\Hom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\\
\simeq\fHom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})[V]=(f_{!}\fHom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D}))[U],

as desired.
Here the base change isomorphism
f^{*}j_{*}\mathcal{M}\simeq j^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime}{}^{*}\mathcal{M}
holds
for all quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathcal{M}
on
U
, since
j
is a quasi-compact
and quasi-separated morphism of schemes, while
f
is a flat morphism
of schemes.
The isomorphism
\Hom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\simeq\fHom_{f^{*}\mathcal{A}}(f^{*}\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})[V]
holds by
Lemma
5.18
.

Now assume that the scheme
X
is quasi-separated and the open
immersion morphism
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is affine,
Let
\mathfrak{Q}
be a cosheaf of
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
on
X

(40)

\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}\simeq f_{*}(f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{Q}).

The formula (
40
) is our version
of
[
46
, formula (3.25)]
.

Indeed, in the notation above, and denoting also the open immersion

V\longrightarrow X
by
k=jf^{\prime}=fj^{\prime}
, we have natural isomorphisms of
quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X

(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}((f_{!}\mathfrak{Q})[U])=(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\simeq(k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\\
\simeq(f_{*}j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\simeq f_{*}((j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V])

where the second isomorphism is (
36
),
and the last isomorphism holds since we are assuming that the morphism
of schemes
f
is affine.
It remains to point out that the direct image functor

f_{*}\colon(f^{*}\mathcal{A}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
for an affine morphism
f

preserves all inductive limits.

The following lemma, which is our version of
[
46
, construction in
Section 3.8 and Lemma 8.4.1]
, extends the natural
isomorphism (
40
) to certain

not necessarily affine
open immersion morphisms
f
.
However, part (b) requires more restrictive assumptions on
the cosheaf
\mathfrak{Q}
.

We refer to Sections
4.4
–
4.5
for the definition of
an
antilocal
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-module on a quasi-compact
semi-separated scheme
X
.
The notation
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for the exact category of antilocal
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
was introduced in
Section
4.7
, and
the notation
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for the exact category of antilocal
contraherent cosheaves on
X
was also mentioned there.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
X
a scheme and
Y\subset X
be an open subscheme with the open
immersion morphism
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be quasi-coherent quasi-algebras over
X
, let

\mathcal{E}
be a quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule on
X
, and let

\mathfrak{Q}
be a cosheaf of
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
.
In this context:

(a)
If the scheme
X
is quasi-separated and the open immersion
morphism
f
is quasi-compact, then there is a natural morphism
of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X

(41)

\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muf_{*}(f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{Q}).

(b)
If the schemes
X
and
Y
are quasi-compact and
semi-separated, the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
on
X

is
\mathcal{B}
-flat, and
\mathfrak{Q}
is an antilocal contraherent
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-module
on
Y
, then
the morphism (
41
)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Part (a): the construction is similar to the one
in
[
46
, Section 3.8]
, but a bit simpler, essentially because
the morphism of quasi-ringed schemes
f\colon(Y,f^{*}\mathcal{B})\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{B})
is left flat in our case.
Let
U\subset X
and
V\subset Y
be affine open subschemes such
that
V\subset U
.
As above, we denote the open immersion morphisms by
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
,

j^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow Y
, and
f^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow U
.
The desired morphism is produced as the composition of natural
morphism and isomorphisms

\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}f_{!}\mathfrak{Q}=\varinjlim\nolimits_{U\subset X}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{Q}[Y\cap U]\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{U\subset X}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(U)}(\varinjlim\nolimits_{V\subset Y\cap U}\mathfrak{Q}[V])\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{U\subset X}\varinjlim\nolimits_{V\subset Y\cap U}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{(U,V):V\subset Y\cap U}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(U)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{(U,V):V\subset Y\cap U}f_{*}j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V]\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muf_{*}(\varinjlim\nolimits_{(U,V):V\subset Y\cap U}j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V])\\
\simeq f_{*}(\varinjlim\nolimits_{V\subset Y}j^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}(V)}\mathfrak{Q}[V])=f_{*}(f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{Q}).

Here the isomorphism in the third line holds by
formula (
36
).

Part (b): the argument is similar
to
[
46
, proof of Lemma 8.4.1]
.
The idea is to show that both the left-hand side and the right-hand
side of (
41
) are exact
as functors
f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
(or equivalently,

f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
) of the argument

\mathfrak{Q}\in f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for any fixed
\mathcal{B}
-flat quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule
\mathcal{E}
on
X
.

The direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}

takes antilocal contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to antilocal contraherent
cosheaves on
X
, and is exact as a functor
f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
, by
[
46
, Corollary 4.6.3(a)]
.
It follows that the direct image functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,f^{*}\mathcal{B}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{B}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes antilocal contraherent
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-modules on
Y
to
antilocal contraherent
\mathcal{B}
-modules on
X
, and is exact as
a functor
f_{!}\colon f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
.
On the other hand, the functor
\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}{-}\,\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is exact by Lemma
5.19
.
This proves that the left-hand side
of (
41
) is exact
as a functor of
\mathfrak{Q}\in f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
.

The proof of exactness of the right-hand side
of (
41
) is based on
the notion of a
dilute
quasi-coherent sheaf on a quasi-compact
semi-separated scheme, which was suggested in
[
41
, Section 4.1]

and elaborated upon in
[
46
, Section 4.2]
.
The functor
f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}{-}\,\colon f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is exact by Lemma
5.19
.
As the functor
f_{*}
of direct image of quasi-coherent sheaves with
respect to a morphism
f
of quasi-compact semi-separated schemes is
exact on the full exact subcategory of dilute quasi-coherent
sheaves
[
46
, Corollary 4.2.7]
, it suffices to show that
the quasi-coherent left
f^{*}\mathcal{A}
-module
f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{Q}
is
dilute as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
Y
for all
\mathfrak{Q}\in f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
.
The class of dilute quasi-coherent sheaves is closed under
extensions in
Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

[
46
, Section 4.2]
, so in view of
Theorem
4.27
(c) it
suffices to consider the case of a contraherent
f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-module
\mathfrak{Q}

of the form
\mathfrak{Q}=j^{\prime}_{!}\mathfrak{R}
, where
j^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow Y
in the open immersion
of an affine open subscheme
V\subset Y
and
\mathfrak{R}
is a contraherent

j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{B}
-module on
V
.
By (
40
), we have

f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}j^{\prime}_{!}\mathfrak{R}\simeq j^{\prime}_{*}(j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{R})
, and it remains to mention
that the direct images of quasi-coherent sheaves from affine open
subschemes are dilute on quasi-compact semi-separated
schemes
[
46
, Lemma 4.2.1 or Corollary 4.2.7]
.
Notice that all quasi-coherent sheaves on affine schemes
are dilute
[
46
, Section 4.2]
.

Now that both sides
of (
41
) are known to be
exact as functors of
\mathfrak{Q}\in f^{*}\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
,
Theorem
4.27
(c)
implies that, in order to show
that (
41
) is
an isomorphism, it suffices to consider the case of
\mathfrak{Q}=j^{\prime}_{!}\mathfrak{R}

as above.
In this case, the assertion follows from
the formula (
40
) applied to two
affine open immersion morphisms
j^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow Y
and
fj^{\prime}\colon V\longrightarrow X
.
One needs to use commutativity of the triangular diagram

\mathcal{E}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}f_{!}j^{\prime}_{!}\mathfrak{R}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muf_{*}(f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{f^{*}\mathcal{B}}j^{\prime}_{!}\mathfrak{R})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muf_{*}j^{\prime}_{*}(j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{E}\odot_{j^{\prime}{}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{B}}\mathfrak{R})

of natural morphisms of quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
.
The rightmost morphism and the composition are isomorphisms
by (
40
), hence the leftmost morphism
is an isomorphism, too, as desired.
∎

5.9.
Compatibility with forgetting the action of
\mathcal{A}

Let
X
be a scheme.
Then the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
of
X
is an obvious example
of a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
The structure sheaf
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
can be also viewed as a quasi-coherent

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodule.

One can specialize the discussion
in Sections
5.5
–
5.7
to the case of

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{O}_{X}=\mathcal{B}
and a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
X
(viewed as
a quasi-coherent quasi-module on
X
via the natural inclusion functor

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--QQcoh}}}
, as per the discussion in
Section
3.1
).
This leads to the definitions of the partially defined functor

\fHom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}({-},{-})
and the functor
{-}\odot_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}
, acting
between the categories of quasi-coherent sheaves and contraherent
cosheaves (or more generally, cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules) on
X
.
These functors agree with the functors
\fHom_{X}
and
\odot_{X}

defined in
[
46
, Sections 2.5–2.6]
.
So we will write
\fHom_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}=\fHom_{X}
and
\odot_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}=\odot_{X}
.

Now let
\mathcal{A}
be an arbitrary quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then the quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule
\mathcal{A}
is

\mathcal{A}
-very flaprojective,
\mathcal{A}
-robustly flaprojective, and

\mathcal{A}
-flat on both sides, as mentioned in
Section
5.5
.

The notation
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}
for exact subcategories in the abelian
category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
can be found in
Section
5.3
.
The similar notation
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

was mentioned in the proofs of
Lemma
5.7
(b)
and Theorem
5.9
;
see
[
46
, Section 4.1]
for a detailed discussion.

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
Then there is a commutative diagram of exact functors between
additive/exact categories

(47)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 25.68195pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.89723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 29.80336pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.68195pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 79.68195pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 107.26115pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 49.68195pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 83.19588pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

On all the diagrams
(
42
–
47
)
,
the vertical arrows denote the forgetful functors.

Proof.

The leftmost vertical (forgetful) functors on all the diagrams are
the respective restrictions of the exact forgetful functor

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
The leftmost vertical functors in (a) and (b) are well-defined by
the definitions of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}
and
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
.
The leftmost vertical functors in (c) and (d) are well-defined
according to the argument in
Section
4.3
.
The leftmost vertical functors in (e) and (f) are well-defined since,
under the assumptions of (e–f), the forgetful functor

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is right adjoint to an exact functor

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}\,\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
; so this forgetful
functor takes injective objects to injective objects.

The rightmost vertical (forgetful) functor in part (a) is provided
by the definition of the exact category
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
.
All the other rightmost vertical functors are the respective
restrictions of the one in part (a).
The rightmost vertical functor in part (b) is well-defined by
the definition of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
.
The rightmost vertical functors in parts (c–d) are well-defined by
Lemma
1.6
(a), as
mentioned in Section
3.4
.
The rightmost vertical functors in parts (e–f) are well-defined by
Lemma
1.6
(b), as also
mentioned in Section
3.4
.

All the lower horizontal functors are provided by the construction
and discussion of
[
46
, Section 2.5]
.
All the upper horizontal functors are provided by the construction
of Section
5.5
and the respective
parts of Lemma
5.17
.

Let us explain why the diagrams are commutative.
In all the cases, this follows from the constructions of the functors

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\fHom_{X}
in view of the natural isomorphism of
quasi-coherent left
\mathcal{A}
-modules

(48)

j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{A}\simeq j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{U}}\mathcal{O}_{U})\simeq\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}

for any affine open subscheme
U\subset X
with the open immersion
morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
and the fact that the functor

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{-}\,\colon X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is left adjoint to
the forgetful functor
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
In all the cases (a–e),
(
48
) is a special case of
the projection formula (
24
).
In the case (f), where the open immersion morphism
j
need not be
affine, the isomorphism (
48
)
holds due to the assumption that
\mathcal{A}
is flat as a quasi-coherent
sheaf in the right
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Finally, let us point out an alternative argument proving that
the functor
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})
takes
X
-cotorsion quasi-coherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules to
X
-locally cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules
in part (b) without a reference to
Lemma
5.17
(b) and the complicated
notion of a robustly flaprojective quasi-coherent bimodule.
Instead of the reference to
Lemma
5.17
(b), in the proof of
part (b) one could just refer to the commutative
diagram (
42
) in part (a)
and the fact that the functor
\fHom_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},{-})
takes cotorsion
quasi-coherent sheaves to locally cotorsion contraherent
cosheaves
[
46
, Section 2.5]
.
This is based on Lemma
5.17
(a),
but avoids using Lemma
5.17
(b).
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
over
X
.
Assume that either

(a)
the scheme
X
is semi-separated, or

(b)
the scheme
X
is quasi-separated and
\mathcal{A}
is flat
as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
with respect to the left

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Then there is a commutative diagram of additive functors

(49)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern where the vertical arrows denote the forgetful functors.

Proof.

The constructions of the vertical (forgetful) functors are obvious.
The lower horizontal functor was constructed
in
[
46
, Section 2.6]
.
The upper horizontal functor is provided by the construction
of Section
5.7
.

Let us explain why the diagram is commutative.
In both cases (a) and (b), this follows from the constructions of
the functors
\odot_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\odot_{X}
in view of the natural isomorphism

(50)

j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{A}\simeq j_{*}(j^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{A}(U))\simeq(j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)}\mathcal{A}(U)

of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
with a right action of
the ring
\mathcal{A}(U)
.
Here
U\subset X
is an affine open subscheme with the open
immersion morphism
j\colon U\longrightarrow X
.
In case (a), the isomorphism holds because
j
is an affine morphism;
in case (b), because
\mathcal{A}(U)
is a flat left
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
-module.

One also needs to use the obvious isomorphism of tensor products

(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}S)\otimes_{S}P\simeq\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}P

for any quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on a scheme
X
with a right action
of a ring
R
on
\mathcal{F}
, any associative ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow S
,
and any
S
-module
P
.
Finally, one needs to use the fact that the forgetful functor

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
preserves all inductive limits.
∎

5.10.
Naïve co-contra correspondence

We refer to the introduction to the paper
[
48
]
for
a general discussion of the co-contra correspondence phenomenon.
This section is an
\mathcal{A}
-module generalization
of
[
46
, Section 4.8]
.
We start with underived versions of the naïve co-contra
correspondence for
\mathcal{A}
-modules before proceeding to deduce
the derived versions.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme.
The notation
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for exact categories of antilocal contraherent

\mathcal{A}
-modules was introduced in
Section
4.7
,
and the similar notation
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
for the exact category of
antilocal contraherent cosheaves was also mentioned there.
We refer to
Sections
2.2
–
2.3
for the notation

X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\subset X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
, and put
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathsf{lct}}_{\mathsf{al}}=X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\cap X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
for any open covering
\mathbf{W}

of the scheme
X
.
See
[
46
, Section 4.3]
for a detailed discussion.

restrict to mutually inverse equivalences between the exact
categories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
and
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, forming
a commutative square diagram with the similar mutually inverse
equivalences
\mathcal{O}_{X}\odot_{X}{-}
and
\fHom_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},{-})
between
the exact categories
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
and
X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathsf{lct}}
,

(53)

\begin{gathered}\qquad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 30.18753pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.18753pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 54.18753pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 87.70146pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathsf{lct}}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\,:\!\mathcal{O}_{X}\odot_{X}{-}$\hss}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Proof.

First of all, the lower horizontal equivalences of exact categories
are established in
[
46
, Lemma 4.8.2]
for part (a)
and
[
46
, Lemma 4.8.4(a)]
for parts (b–c).
Therefore, commutativity of the square
diagram (
42
) in
Lemma
5.21
(a) implies
that the functor
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}

takes values in the full exact subcategory

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}
.
Similarly, commutativity of the square
diagram (
49
) in
Lemma
5.22
implies
that the functor
\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}\,\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

takes values in the full exact subcategory

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, while the functor

\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}\,\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

takes values in the full exact subcategory

\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.
This establishes the existence of all the functors on the diagrams
in parts (a) and (b).
The upper horizontal functors on these diagrams are exact by
formulas (
28
–
29
)
and Lemma
5.19
.

The upper horizontal functors on the diagrams in (a) and (b) are
adjoint to each other by formula (
37
).
Moreover, this adjunction agrees with the similar adjunction between
the two lower horizontal functors on the respective
diagrams
[
46
, formula (2.19) in Section 2.6]
, in
the sense that the vertical (forgetful) functors take the adunction
morphisms to the adjunction morphisms.
As the vertical functors are conservative (take nonisomorphisms to
nonisomorphisms), the fact that the lower horizontal adjunction
is a category equivalence implies that the upper horizontal adjunction
is a ctategory equivalence, too, in both parts (a) and (b).

Alternatively, the upper horizontal equivalences in parts (a) and (b)
can be proved directly and independently of the results
of
[
46
, Section 4.8]
by arguments similar to the proof of
part (c) below.
Then, for part (a), one needs to use
Theorems
4.5
(c)
and
4.27
(c).
For part (b), one needs to use
Theorems
4.12
(c)
and
4.32
(c).

Part (c): the functors

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
and

\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}\penalty{-}\,\colon\allowbreak\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
are exact by formula (
30
)
and Lemma
5.19
.
In order to establish their properties mentioned in the lemma, one
starts with considering the case of an affine scheme
U
with
a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}
over
U
.

In this case, one has
\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
as

\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\simeq\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
, as explained in
Sections
3.3
–
3.4
.
The equivalence of categories
\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
identifies
the full subcategory
\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
with
the full subcategory
\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}\subset\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Furthermore, all contraherent cosheaves on an affine scheme
U

are antilocal.
With these identifications in mind, the functor

\fHom_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{B},{-})\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
is
just the identity functor
\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}\allowbreak\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}
.
The functor
\mathcal{B}\odot_{\mathcal{B}}{-}\,\colon\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

is just the identity inclusion
\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{\mathsf{cot}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Returning to our scheme
X
, the result of
Theorem
4.18
(c) describes
the objects of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
as the direct summands of finitely
iterated extensions of the direct images of objects of

\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
from affine open subschemes

U_{\alpha}\subset X
with the quasi-coherent quasi-algebras

\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{A}|_{U_{\alpha}}
.
In the same notation, the result of
Theorem
4.32
(c)
and Remark
4.35
describes
the objects of
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
as the direct summands of
finitely iterated exensions of the direct images of objects of

\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
.

It remains to point out that the functors
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})

and
\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}
form commutative square diagrams with the direct
images from affine open subschemes, as per
the formulas (
39
)
and (
40
).
It follows from these observations that the functor

\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}

takes values in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
, the functor

\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}\,\colon\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes values
in
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
, and the resulting pair of adjoint functors
(shown by the upper horizontal double line on
the diagram (
53
) in part (c))
are mutually inverse equivalences.
(Cf.
[
46
, proof of Lemma 4.8.2]
.)
∎

to0.0pt{\hss$\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})\colon$}}\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 63.23065pt\raise-33.12892pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 102.27377pt\raise-33.12892pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\,:\!\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}$\hss}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

The direct image functors
f_{*}
and
f_{!}
on all
the diagrams
(
54
–
56
)
are exact.

Proof.

Part (a): the functor
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes

Y
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
to

X
-contraadjusted quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X

by
[
46
, Section 2.5]
,
[
54
, Lemma 1.7(c)]
, as mentioned
in Section
4.1
.
The functor
f_{*}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathsf{cta}}
is exact
by
[
46
, Corollary 4.1.14(a)]
; hence the functor

f_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}
is exact, too.

The functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes
antilocal contraherent cosheaves on
Y
to antilocal contraherent
cosheaves, and the functor
f_{!}\colon Y{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow X{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
is
exact by
[
46
, Corollary 4.6.3(a)]
.
Therefore, the functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{A}|_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\allowbreak\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes antilocal contraherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules to
antilocal contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules, and the functor

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
is exact (as it was
already mentioned in the proof of
Corollary
4.36
).

This proves the existence and exactness of all the functors on
the diagram (
54
).
The diagram is commutative by formula (
39
),
or alternatively, by
Lemma
5.20
(b).

The proof of part (b) is similar.
One can refer to Section
4.2

and
[
46
, Corollaries 4.1.14(b) and 4.6.3(b)]
.

Part (c): the functor
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
takes

\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules on
Y
to

\mathcal{A}
-cotorsion quasi-coherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X

by
[
54
, Lemma 1.7(b)]
, as explained in
Section
4.3
.
It is clear from part (a) or (b) that the functor

f_{*}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
is exact
as a restriction of an exact functor to full exact subcategories of
the source and target categories.

The functor
f_{!}\colon(Y,\mathcal{A}|_{Y}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}\allowbreak\longrightarrow(X,\mathcal{A}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
takes
antilocal
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-locally cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-modules to
antilocal
\mathcal{A}|_{Y}
-locally cotorsion contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules, and
the functor
f_{!}\colon\mathcal{A}|_{Y}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
is exact
by Corollary
4.36
.

This proves the existence and exactness of all the functors on
the diagram (
56
).
The diagram is commutative by formula (
39
),
or alternatively, by
Lemma
5.20
(b).
∎

The following two theorems form the main result of this
Section
5
.

Theorem 5.25
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with an open
covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.
Then, for any conventional derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,

-
, or
\varnothing
, there are natural triangulated equivalences
between the derived categories of quasi-coherent and
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on
X
,

(57)

\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.

The leftmost equivalence, induced by the exact inclusion of
exact/abelian categories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, is
the result of Corollary
5.8
.
The rightmost equivalence, induced by the exact inclusion of
exact categories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
, is the result of
Corollary
5.11
(a).
The middle equivalence is induced by the equivalence of exact
categories
\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\simeq\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}_{\mathsf{al}}
provided by
Lemma
5.23
(a).
∎

The notation for the resulting triangulated equivalence of
Theorem
5.25
is

(58)

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})\colon\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathcal{A}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}})\,:\!\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbb{L}}{-},}

where
\mathbb{R}\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})
means the right derived functor of
the contraherent
\fHom
functor
\fHom_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{A},{-})
, while

\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
stands for the left derived functor of
the contratensor product functor
\mathcal{A}\odot_{\mathcal{A}}{-}
.

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact semi-separated scheme with an open

\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{A}
be a quasi-coherent quasi-algebra over
X
.

\star=+
or
\varnothing
,
there is a commutative diagram of natural triangulated equivalences
between the derived categories of various abelian/exact categories of

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\mathcal{A}
-modules on

,

(a–c).

The upper horizontal line of triangulated equivalences is a particular

5.25
.
The triangulated equivalences in the left-hand part of the diagram

5.8

and Theorem
5.9
.
The horizontal triangulated equivalences in the right-hand part of

(a–c).
The vertical triangulated equivalences in the rightmost column of

.

Remark 5.27
.

In particular, the results of
Theorems
5.25
–
5.26
are applicable
to the sheaf of rings of (fiberwise) differential operators

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}
, as per
Example
3.6
.

6.
Contraherent CDG-Modules

The concepts of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras over schemes
and quasi-coherent CDG-modules over them were defined
in
[
52
, Section 2.4]
(in the context of the definition of
a quasi-module from
[
52
, Section 2.3]
, which is more narrow than
in our Section
1.4
).
In this section, we define locally contraherent CDG-modules over
quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras.

6.1.
Quasi-coherent and contraherent graded modules

It is advisable to think of graded objects in a category as collections
of objects indexed by the grading set or the grading group, such as
the group of integers
\mathbb{Z}
,
rather than
as direct sum
decompositions indexed by
\mathbb{Z}
.
Even though the direct sum notation is convenient and we will use it,
speaking, e. g., of “graded rings
B^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}B^{i}
”,
it is nevertheless more instructive to think of a graded ring
B^{*}
as
the collection of abelian groups
B^{i}
together with multiplication maps

B^{i}\times B^{j}\longrightarrow B^{i+j}
,
i
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, and a unit element

1\in B^{0}
, than to consider
B^{*}
as a single abelian group/ring
decomposed into a direct sum of the grading components
B^{i}
.

One way to explain this point-of-view preference is to mention that
the infinite direct sum is a nontrivial functor which need not commute
with other functors.
An even more important explanation, particularly in the contexts where
one is interested in the “contra” side of one’s story (involving,
e. g., contramodules or contraherent cosheaves), is that one often needs
to consider
direct products
of the grading components instead
of the direct sums.

So, the proper point of view is that the category of graded abelian
groups
\mathsf{Ab}^{\mathbb{Z}}
is just the Cartesian product of
\mathbb{Z}
copies of
the category of abelian groups
\mathsf{Ab}
.
There is not one but
two
forgetful functors
\mathsf{Ab}^{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}

assigning an ungraded abelian group to a graded one: the direct sum
functor
\Sigma\colon\mathsf{Ab}^{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}
and the direct product functor
