\Pi\colon\mathsf{Ab}^{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ab}
.
So we put
[
50
, Section 2.1]

\Sigma((A^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\bigoplus\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi((A^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}})=\prod\nolimits_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}.

For any
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring
B^{*}
, the ungraded abelian group

\Sigma B^{*}
has a natural ungraded ring structure.
For any
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
B^{*}
-module
M^{*}
,
both
the ungraded
abelian groups
\Sigma M^{*}
and
\Pi M^{*}
have natural

\Sigma B^{*}
-module structures.
The tensor product
N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
of a graded right
B^{*}
-module

N^{*}
and a graded left
B^{*}
-module
M^{*}
carries a natural grading,
and there is the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(M^{*},P^{*})
for any
two graded left
B^{*}
-modules
M^{*}
and
P^{*}
.
The tensor product and
\Hom
functors interact with the functors of
forgetting the grading according to the rules

\Sigma(N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*})\simeq\Sigma N^{*}\otimes_{\Sigma B^{*}}\Sigma M^{*}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \Pi\Hom^{*}_{B^{*}}(M^{*},P^{*})\simeq\Hom_{\Sigma B^{*}}(\Sigma M^{*},\Pi P^{*}).

The abelian categories of graded left and right modules over a graded
ring
B^{*}
will be denoted by
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
and
{\operatorname{\mathsf{Mod--}}}B^{*}
.
So we have two exact, faithful functors of forgetting the grading

\Sigma\colon B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
and
\Pi\colon B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

A graded left
B^{*}
-module
C^{*}
is said to be
cotorsion
if

\Ext^{1}_{B^{*}}(F^{*},C^{*})=0
for all flat graded left
B^{*}
-modules
F^{*}
.
Here
\Ext^{n}_{B^{*}}({-},{-})
,
n\geq 0
, denote the
\Ext
groups
computed in the category of graded left
B^{*}
-modules
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
The notion of a cotorsion graded
B^{*}
-module has all the properties
similar to those of its ungraded version, as spelled out in
Section
1.2
.
A graded left
B^{*}
-module
J^{*}
is said to be
injective
if
it is injective as an object of
B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

Given a commutative ring
R
, a graded
R
-
R
-bimodule
B^{*}
is said
to be a
graded quasi-module
over
R
if its grading component
bimodules
B^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, are quasi-modules over
R
(in the sense
of the definition in Section
1.4
).
A graded ring
A^{*}
endowed with a morphism of graded rings

R\longrightarrow A^{*}
(i. e., a ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow A^{0}
) is said to be
a
graded quasi-algebra
over
R
if the underlying graded

R
-
R
-bimodule of
A^{*}
is a graded quasi-module over
R

(cf. the discussion in Section
1.5
).
Here the underlying graded
R
-
R
-bimodule structure on
A^{*}
is
induced by the graded ring structure on
A^{*}
and the graded ring
homomorphism
R\longrightarrow A^{*}
.

Essentially all the basic results of commutative and noncommutative
ring and module theory remain true for graded modules over graded rings.
One just needs to restrict all the arguments and constructions to
homogeneous elements, etc.
This allows us to extend the theory developed in
Sections
3
–
5
to quasi-coherent and (locally) contraherent
graded modules over quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebras over schemes
without spelling out all the details anew.
One only needs to be careful with the functors of forgetting
the grading.

From our perspective, we prefer to think of (co)sheaves of graded
modules rather than graded (co)sheaves of modules.
So the graded generalization of the theory developed above in
this paper starts with generalizing
Section
2.1
to the case of a topological
space
X
endowed with a sheaf of graded rings
\mathcal{O}^{*}
.
The definitions of sheaves and cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules
in Section
2.1
are extended
straightforwardly to the case of sheaves and cosheaves of graded modules
over a sheaf of graded rings
\mathcal{O}^{*}
.
We denote the category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}^{*}
-modules on
X
by

(X,\mathcal{O}^{*}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Sh}}}
and the category of cosheaves of
\mathcal{O}^{*}
-modules on
X

by
(X,\mathcal{O}^{*}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Cosh}}}
.

Then, given a scheme
X
, a sheaf of graded
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules

\mathcal{B}^{*}
is said to be a
quasi-coherent graded quasi-module
over

X
if its grading component sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules

\mathcal{B}^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, are quasi-coherent quasi-modules over
X

(in the sense of the definition in
Section
3.1
).
A sheaf of graded rings
\mathcal{A}^{*}
on
X
endowed with a morphism of
sheaves of graded rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
is said to be
a
quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra
over
X
if the underlying
sheaf of graded
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-bimodules of
\mathcal{A}^{*}
is
a quasi-coherent graded quasi-module.
Here
\mathcal{O}_{X}
is considered as a sheaf of graded rings concentrated
in the grading
0
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{A}^{*}
be a sheaf of graded rings on
X

endowed with a morphism of sheaves of graded rings
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
.
A sheaf of graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules
\mathcal{M}^{*}
on
X
is said to be
a
quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
if the grading component
sheaves
\mathcal{M}^{i}
of
\mathcal{M}^{*}
are quasi-coherent as sheaves of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.
Given an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of
X
, a cosheaf of graded

\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
on
X
is said to be a
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
if the grading component cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}^{i}

of
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
are
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent as cosheaves of

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

The functor of forgetting the grading assigns to a quasi-coherent
graded algebra
\mathcal{A}^{*}
its underlying ungraded quasi-coherent
algebra
\Sigma\mathcal{A}^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{A}^{i}
.
The similar functor of forgetting the grading assigns to
a quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{*}
its underlying
ungraded quasi-coherent
\Sigma\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}^{i}
.
To a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
,
the functor of forgetting the grading assigns its underlying
ungraded
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
\Sigma\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module

\Pi\mathfrak{P}^{*}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{P}^{i}
.

We denote the category of quasi-coherent graded left
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules
on
X
by
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
, the category of quasi-coherent graded right

\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules by
{\operatorname{\mathsf{Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{A}^{*}
, and the category of
\mathbf{W}
-locally
contraherent graded (left)
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules on
X
by
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
So the functors of forgetting the grading are

\Sigma\colon\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}

and
\Pi\colon\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}\longrightarrow\Sigma\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
The category
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
is a Grothendieck abelian category, while

\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
is a naturally an exact category with exact functors
of infinite products.
The exact category structure on
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
is defined similarly
to the ungraded version of this definition given in
Section
3.3
.

The exact category of contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}

on
X
is
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}=\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}
.
The exact category of locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules

\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}
on
X
is defined as
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
So, for a cosheaf of
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
to be locally
contraherent, there must exist an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of
X
such that
the cosheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}^{i}
is
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
(one open covering
\mathbf{W}
has to be suitable for all
grading components
\mathfrak{P}^{i}
).

A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
on
X

is called
X
-locally cotorsion
if all its grading component

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves
\mathfrak{P}^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, are locally
cotorsion.
The
X
-locally injective

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules on
X
are defined similarly.
The exact categories of
X
-locally cotorsion (locally) contraherent
graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules are defined similarly to
Section
3.3
and denoted by

\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
.

A quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{*}
is called
flat

(or
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-flat
) if the graded module
\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
over
the graded ring
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
is flat for every affine open subscheme

U\subset X
.
This definition has all the properties similar to those of its
ungraded version, as per
Section
3.4
.
The full exact subcategory of flat quasi-coherent graded

\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules is denoted by
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-fl}}}}\subset\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}
.

A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
is
called
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-locally cotorsion
if, for every affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the graded left

\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
is cotorsion.
Once again, this definition has all the properties similar to those
of its ungraded version, as per
Section
3.4
.
The exact categories of
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-locally cotorsion (locally)
contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules are defined similarly to
Section
3.4
and denoted by

\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
, and
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
.

A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{J}^{*}
is
called
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-locally injective
if, for every affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the graded left

\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{J}^{*}[U]
is injective.
As above, the properties of this definition are very similar to those
of its ungraded version discussed in
Section
3.4
.
The exact categories of
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-locally injective (locally)
contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules are denoted by

\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
, and
\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{A}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lin}}}}
.

The direct image and inverse image functors for quasi-coherent
graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules and locally contraherent graded

\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules are constructed similarly to the discussion in
Sections
3.5
–
3.6
, and denoted in
the same way.
The contraherent
\fHom
and contratensor product functors for
quasi-coherent graded (bi)modules and (contraherent) cosheaves of
graded modules are constructed similarly to
Sections
5.5

and
5.7
, and denoted
by
(\mathcal{E}^{*},\mathcal{C}^{*})\longmapsto\fHom_{\mathcal{A}^{*}}(\mathcal{E}^{*},\mathcal{C}^{*})
and

(\mathcal{E}^{*},\mathfrak{P}^{*})\longmapsto\mathcal{E}^{*}\odot_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}\mathfrak{P}^{*}
.

The results of Sections
4

and
5
, including
Theorems
5.25
–
5.26
, remain valid
in the graded setting.
However, the graded versions of the category equivalences of
Section
5
connecting the quasi-coherent
and locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-modules do
not
form
commutative diagrams with ungraded versions of the same equivalences
and the functors of forgetting the grading.

It suffices to consider the case of an affine scheme
X=U
in order
to understand the situation.
In this case, the naïve co-contra correspondence reduces
essentially to identity functors or identity inclusion functors
assigning to a graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module the same graded

\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module, or at worst its coresolution, etc.
But the functors of forgetting the grading, which are
\Sigma
on
the quasi-coherent side and
\Pi
on the contraherent side, still
do
not
agree with each other.

6.2.
Curved DG-rings and curved DG-modules

The material of this section goes back to
[
43
]
.
The most complete exposition can be found in
[
50
, Sections 3.2,
4.2, and 6.1]
; and there are other expositions
in
[
45
, Section 3.1]
,
[
17
, Section 1.1]
,

[
52
, Sections 2.2, 2.4, and 3.1]
,
[
53
, Section 6.2]
,

[
62
, Section 1]
, etc.

A
curved DG-ring
(
CDG-ring
)
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
is
a set of data consisting of

•

a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring
B^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}B^{i}
;

•

an odd derivation
d
of degree
1
on
B^{*}
, that is

d\colon B^{i}\longrightarrow B^{i+1}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and

d(bc)=d(b)c+(-1)^{|b|}bd(c)
for all homogeneous elements

b
and
c\in B^{*}
of degrees
|b|
and
|c|\in\mathbb{Z}
;

•

an element
h\in B^{2}
.

The following two axioms must be satisfied:

(i)

d(d(b))=hb-bh
for all
b\in B^{*}
;

(ii)

d(h)=0
.

So, the square of the differential
d
on
B^{*}
is
not
equal
to zero; rather, it is equal to the commutator with the element
h
.
The element
h\in B^{2}
is called the
curvature element
of
a CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
A
DG-ring

A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=(A^{*},d)
can be defined as a CDG-ring

(A^{*},d,h)
with a vanishing curvature element,
h=0
.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
be two CDG-rings.
A
morphism of CDG-rings

f=(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
a set of data consisting of

•

a homomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded rings
f\colon B^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
;

•

an element
a\in A^{1}
.

The following two axioms must be satisfied:

(iii)

f(d_{B}(b))=d_{A}(f(b))+af(b)-(-1)^{|b|}f(b)a
for all elements

b\in B^{*}
of degree
|b|\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(iv)

f(h_{B})=h_{A}+d_{A}(a)+a^{2}
.

So, the morphism of graded rings
f
does
not
form a commutative
diagram with the differentials
d_{B}
and
d_{A}
; rather, the diagram is
commutative up to the graded commutator with the element
a
.
The element
a\in A^{1}
is called the
change-of-connection
element
of a morphism of CDG-rings
f=(f,a)
.
A
morphism of DG-rings

f\colon A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
can be defined
as a morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon(A^{*},d_{A},0)\longrightarrow(B^{*},d_{B},0)

with a vanishing change-of-connection element,
a=0
.

The
composition
of two morphisms of CDG-rings

(g,b)\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
(f,a)\colon\allowbreak B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is defined by the obvious formula
(f,a)\circ(g,b)=(f\circ g,\>a+f(b))\colon\allowbreak C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
A morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)
is said to be
strict
if
a=0
.
Morphisms of CDG-rings of the form
(\mathrm{id}_{B^{*}},a)\colon(B^{*},d^{\prime},h^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B^{*},d,h)
are called
change-of-connection morphisms
.
By the definition, one has
d^{\prime}(b)=d(b)+ab-(-1)^{|b|}ba
for
all
b\in B^{|b|}
and
h^{\prime}=h+d(a)+a^{2}
in this case.
Clearly, all change-of-connection morphisms of CDG-rings are
isomorphisms (i. e., invertible).
Every morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
factorizes
uniquely into the composition of a strict morphism followed by
a change-of-connection isomorphism,
(f,a)=(\mathrm{id}_{A^{*}},a)\circ(f,0)
.

A
left CDG-module

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
over a CDG-ring

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
is a set of data consisting of

•

a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded left
B^{*}
-module
M^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{i}
;

•

an odd derivation
d_{M}
of degree
1
on
M^{*}
compatible with
the odd derivation
d
on
B^{*}
, that is
d_{M}\colon M^{i}\longrightarrow M^{i+1}

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
d_{M}(bx)=d(b)x+(-1)^{|b|}bd_{M}(x)
for all
homogeneous elements
b\in B
and
x\in M
of degrees
|b|

and
|x|\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(v)

d_{M}(d_{M}(x))=hx
for all
x\in M^{*}
.

For a morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
a left CDG-module
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the induced
left CDG-module structure over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is given by
the pair
(M^{*},d^{\prime}_{M})
with the differential
d^{\prime}_{M}
on
M^{*}
defined
by the formula

(vi)

d^{\prime}_{M}(x)=d_{M}(x)+ax
for all
x\in M^{*}
.

A
right CDG-module

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
over a CDG-ring

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
is a set of data consisting of

•

a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded right
B^{*}
-module

N^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}N^{i}
;

•

an odd derivation
d_{N}
of degree
1
on
N^{*}
compatible with
the odd derivation
d
on
B^{*}
, that is
d_{N}\colon N^{i}\longrightarrow N^{i+1}

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
d_{N}(yb)=d_{N}(y)b+(-1)^{|y|}yd(b)
for all
homogeneous elements
b\in B
and
y\in N
of degrees
|b|
and

|y|\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(vii)

d_{N}(d_{N}(y))=-yh
for all
y\in N^{*}
.

For a morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
a right CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the induced
right CDG-module structure over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is given by
the pair
(N^{*},d^{\prime}_{N})
with the differential
d^{\prime}_{N}
on
N^{*}
defined
by the formula

(viii)

d^{\prime}_{N}(y)=d_{M}(y)-(-1)^{|y|}ya
for all
y\in N^{|y|}
.

Notice that the CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
does
not
have any
natural structure of a left or right CDG-module over itself (when

h\neq 0
), because of the mismatch between the formulas (i), (v),
and (vii).
However, any CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is naturally a
CDG-bimodule

over itself, in the sense of the next definition.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C^{*},d_{C},h_{C})
be two CDG-rings.
A
CDG-bimodule

K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a set of
data consisting of

•

a graded
B^{*}
-
C^{*}
-bimodule
K^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}K^{i}
;

•

an odd derivation
d_{K}
of degree
1
on
K^{*}
compatible with
the odd derivations
d_{B}
on
B^{*}
and
d_{C}
on
C^{*}
, that is

d_{K}\colon K^{i}\longrightarrow K^{i+1}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and both the identities

d_{K}(bz)=d_{B}(b)z+(-1)^{|b|}bd_{K}(z)
and
d_{K}(zc)=d_{K}(z)c+(-1)^{|z|}zd_{C}(c)
are satisfied for all
b\in B^{|b|}
,
z\in K^{|z|}
, and
c\in C^{|c|}
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(ix)

d_{K}(d_{K}(z))=h_{B}z-zh_{C}
for all
z\in K^{*}
.

Let
(f,a_{B})\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and

(g,a_{C})\colon C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{}^{\prime}\longrightarrow C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be two morphisms of CDG-rings.
For a CDG-bimodule
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the induced
CDG-bimodule structure over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{}^{\prime}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{}^{\prime}
on
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is given by the pair
(K^{*},d^{\prime}_{K})
with the differential
d^{\prime}_{K}
on

K^{*}
defined by the formula

(x)

d^{\prime}_{K}(z)=d_{K}(z)+a_{B}z-(-1)^{|z|}za_{C}
for all
z\in K^{|z|}
.

Given a graded ring
B^{*}
and two graded left
B^{*}
-modules
L^{*}
and

M^{*}
, we denote by
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})
the graded abelian group
of homogeneous
B^{*}
-module maps
L^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}
of various degrees

n\in\mathbb{Z}
with the following sign rule.
The elements of
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{n}(L^{*},M^{*})
are homogeneous additive maps

f\colon L^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}
of degree
n
such that, for every
b\in B^{|b|}

and
l\in L^{|l|}
one has
f(bl)=(-1)^{n|b|}bf(l)
in
M^{*}
.

For any left CDG-modules
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(L^{*},d_{L})
and
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
over
a CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})

is endowed with a natural differential
d
given by the usual formula

d(f)(l)=d_{M}(f(l))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{L}(l))
for all
l\in L^{|l|}
and

f\in\Hom_{B^{*}}^{|f|}(L^{*},M^{*})
.
The differential
d
on
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})
squares to zero:
one has
d(d(f))=0
for all
f\in\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})
, because
the two curvature-related terms arising from
d_{M}^{2}
and
d_{L}^{2}

cancel each other in the expression for
d^{2}
.
So the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})
with
the differential
d
is a complex, which we denote by

\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.

Given a graded ring
B^{*}
and two graded right
B^{*}
-modules
R^{*}
and

N^{*}
, we denote by
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
the graded abelian
group of homogeneous
B^{*}
-module maps
R^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
of various
degrees
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
In the case of right modules, there is no sign rule.
The elements of
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{n}(R^{*},N^{*})
are homogeneous additive
maps
f\colon R^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
of degree
n
such that, for every

b\in B^{|b|}
and
r\in R^{|r|}
one has
f(rb)=f(r)b
in
N^{*}
.

For any right CDG-modules
R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d_{R})
and
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
over
a CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the graded abelian group

\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
is endowed with a natural
differential
d
given by the same formula
d(f)(r)=d_{N}(f(r))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{R}(r))
for all
r\in R^{|r|}
and

f\in\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{|f|}(R^{*},N^{*})
.
Similarly to the case of left modules, the differential
d
on

\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
squares to zero:
one has
d(d(f))=0
for all
f\in\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
.
So the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
with
the differential
d
is a complex, which we denote by

\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.

Given a graded ring
B^{*}
, a graded right
B^{*}
-module
N^{*}
, and
a graded left
B^{*}
-module
M^{*}
, the tensor product
N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}

is naturally a graded abelian group, as mentioned in
Section
6.1
.
The definition of such tensor product is the usual one, and there is
no sign rule involved.

For any left CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
and any right CDG-module

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
over a CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the graded abelian group

N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
is endowed with a natural differential
d
given
by the usual formula
d(y\otimes x)=d_{N}(y)\otimes x+(-1)^{|y|}y\otimes d_{M}(x)

for all
y\in N^{|y|}
and
x\in M^{|x|}
.
The differential
d
on
N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
squares to zero: one has

d(d(y\otimes x))=0
for all
y\otimes x\in N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
.
Once again, this happens because the two curvature-related terms
arizing from
d_{N}^{2}
and
d_{M}^{2}
cancel each other in the expression
for
d^{2}
.
So the graded abelian group
N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
is a complex, which we
denote by
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

More generally, let
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be three
CDG-rings.
Let
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and let

M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Then the tensor product of graded bimodules
N^{*}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{*}
,
endowed by the differential given by the usual formula as above,
is a CDG-bimodule over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We denote this CDG-bimodule by
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes_{B^{*}}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Similarly, let
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
and let
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Then the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})
, endowed with
the differential given by the usual formula as above, is
a CDG-bimodule over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We denote this CDG-bimodule by
\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
We refer to
[
50
, Section 6.1]
for the details, including
the sign rule.

Finally, let
R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
and let
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-bimodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Then the graded abelian group
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(R^{*},N^{*})
,
endowed with the differential given by the usual formula as above,
a CDG-bimodule over
C^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We denote this CDG-bimodule by
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
The details can be found in
[
50
, Section 6.1]

(see also
[
62
, Section 1]
).

6.3.
Quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras

This section provides numerous supplementary details for a much more
sketchy exposition in
[
52
, Section 2.4]
.

The definition of a graded quasi-algebra over a commutative ring
R

was given in Section
6.1
, based on
the discussion in Sections
1.4
–
1.5
.
The definitions of a CDG-ring and a morphism of CDG-rings were
given in Section
6.2
.

A
CDG-quasi-algebra

A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over a commutative ring
R
is
a CDG-ring
(A^{*},d,h)
endowed with a morphism of graded rings

R\longrightarrow A^{*}
making the graded ring
A^{*}
a graded quasi-algebra
over
R
.
Let us emphasize that the differential
d\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
is

not
presumed to be
R
-linear.
In fact,
no
condition of compatibility between the CDG-ring
structure and the graded quasi-algebra structure on
A^{*}
is imposed.

A
morphism
of CDG-quasi-algebras
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

R
is a morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
such that
the triangular diagram of graded ring homomorphisms
R\longrightarrow B^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
is commutative.
The composition of morphisms of CDG-q uasi-algebras is defined in
the obvious way, based on the construction of the composition of
morphisms of CDG-ring in Section
6.2
.

The aim of the present section is to work out the localization and
globalization of CDG-quasi-algebras over Zariski affine open
coverings of schemes.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be
a graded quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
r\in R
be an element and
d_{A}\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
be an odd
derivation of degree
1
.
Consider the graded quasi-algebra
A^{*}[r^{-1}]=R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}A^{*}

over the commutative ring
R[r^{-1}]
, as per the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(a).
Then there exists a unique extension of
d_{A}
to an odd derivation

d_{A[r^{-1}]}\colon A^{*}[r^{-1}]\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]
of degree
1
forming
a commutative square diagram with the derivation
d_{A}
and the natural
(localization) homomorphism of graded rings
A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]
.

Proof.

It suffices to say that the graded ring
A^{*}[r^{-1}]
is freely
generated by the graded ring
A^{*}
and a formal inverse element
r^{-1}

to the element
r\in A^{0}
.
Then it follows that the assignment
d_{A[r^{-1}]}(a)=d_{A}(a)

for
a\in A^{*}
and
d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-1})=-r^{-1}d_{A}(r)r^{-1}
extends
uniquely to an odd derivation
d_{A[r^{-1}]}\colon A^{*}[r^{-1}]\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]
.
It suffices to check that the only relations
rr^{-1}=1=r^{-1}r

are preserved, that is
d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r)r^{-1}+rd_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-1})=0=d_{A}(1)
and

r^{-1}d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r)+d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-1})r=0=d_{A}(1)

(see
[
50
, Lemma 3.18]
).

More explicitly, let
n\geq 0
be an integer.
The formula

(60)

d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-n}a)=r^{-n}d_{A}(a)-\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}r^{-i}d_{A}(r)r^{n-i+1}a\quad\text{for all $a\in A^{*}$}

where
r\in R
is viewed as an element of
A^{0}
, defines
the derivation
d_{A[r^{-1}]}
.
Equivalently,

(61)

d_{A[r^{-1}]}(br^{-n})=d_{A}(b)r^{-n}-(-1)^{|b|}\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}br^{-i}d_{A}(r)r^{n-i+1}\quad\text{for all $b\in A^{|b|}$}.

To prove that the two formulas agree with each other and define
an odd derivation
d_{A[r^{-1}]}\colon A^{*}[r^{-1}]\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]

compatible with
d_{A}
, denote temporarily by
d^{\prime}
the output of
the formula (
60
) and by
d^{\prime\prime}

the output of the formula (
61
).
Clearly,
d^{\prime}(r^{-0}a)=d_{A}(a)
and
d^{\prime\prime}(br^{-0})=d_{A}(b)
.
To show that the maps
d^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime\prime}\colon A^{*}[r^{-1}]\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]

are well-defined, one needs to check that
d^{\prime}(r^{-n-1}(ra))=d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a)

and
d^{\prime\prime}((br)r^{-n-1})=d^{\prime\prime}(br^{-n})
in
A^{*}[r^{-1}]
for all

a\in A^{|a|}
and
b\in A^{|b|}
.

Then one observes that
d_{A}(r^{n})r^{-n}a+r^{n}d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a)=d_{A}(a)

and
d^{\prime\prime}(br^{-n})r^{n}+(-1)^{|b|}br^{-n}d_{A}(r^{n})=d_{A}(b)
.
Furthermore, the identities
d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a_{1}a_{2})=d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a_{1})a_{2}+(-1)^{|a_{1}|}r^{-n}a_{1}d_{A}(a_{2})
and
d^{\prime\prime}(b_{1}b_{2}r^{-n})=d_{A}(b_{1})b_{2}r^{-n}+(-1)^{|b_{1}|}b_{1}d^{\prime\prime}(b_{2}r^{-n})
for all
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
b_{1}
,

b_{2}\in A^{*}
are also easy to check.
Now one can compute that the equation
r^{-n}a=br^{-n}
in

A^{*}[r^{-1}]
implies

r^{n}d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a)r^{n}=d_{A}(r^{n}r^{-n}ar^{n})-d_{A}(r^{n})r^{-n}ar^{n}-(-1)^{|a|}r^{n}r^{-n}ad_{A}(r^{n})\\
=d_{A}(r^{n}br^{-n}r^{n})-d_{A}(r^{n})br^{-n}r^{n}-(-)^{|b|}r^{n}br^{-n}d_{A}(r^{n})=r^{n}d^{\prime\prime}(br^{-n})r^{n},

hence
d^{\prime}(r^{-n}a)=d^{\prime\prime}(br^{-n})
and
d^{\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}
.
So the notation
d_{A[r^{-1}]}=d^{\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}\colon A^{*}[r^{-1}]\allowbreak\longrightarrow A^{*}[r^{-1}]
is unambiguous.
It remains to notice that
d^{\prime}(r^{-n}x)=d^{\prime}(r^{-n})x+r^{-n}d^{\prime}(x)

and
d^{\prime\prime}(yr^{-n})=d^{\prime\prime}(y)r^{-n}+(-1)^{|y|}yd^{\prime\prime}(r^{-n})
for all

x
,
y\in A^{*}[r^{-1}]
, which allows one to deduce the desired equation

d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-n}abr^{-n})=d_{A[r^{-1}]}(r^{-n}a)br^{-n}+(-1)^{|a|}r^{-n}ad_{A[r^{-1}]}(br^{-n})
for all
a
,
b\in A^{*}
.

The uniqueness of
d_{A[r^{-1}]}
holds because the graded ring

A^{*}[r^{-1}]
is generated by
A^{*}
and
r^{-1}
, and one has

d(r^{-1})=-r^{-1}d(r)r^{-1}
for any odd derivation
d
on a graded
algebra
B^{*}
with an invertible element
r\in B^{0}
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be
a graded quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
d_{A}\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
be an odd derivation of degree
1
.
Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Consider the graded quasi-algebra
B^{*}=S\otimes_{R}A^{*}\simeq A^{*}\otimes_{R}S

over the commutative ring
S
, as per the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(a).
Then there exists a unique extension of
d_{A}
to an odd derivation

d_{B}\colon B^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
of degree
1
forming a commutative square
diagram with the derivation
d_{A}
and the natural homomorphism of graded
rings
A^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
.

Proof.

Let
r_{1}
, …,
r_{N}\in R
be a finite family of elements such
that
\Spec S=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}\Spec R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\subset\Spec R
.
Then we have homomorphisms of commutative ring
R\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]
for all
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, and natural isomorphisms
of commutative rings
R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\simeq S[f(r_{\alpha})^{-1}]
.

To prove uniqueness of
d_{B}
, consider the graded quasi-algebra

C_{\alpha}^{*}=S[f(r_{\alpha})^{-1}]\otimes_{S}B^{*}\simeq R[r_{\alpha}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}A^{*}
.
Suppose given two odd derivations
d^{\prime}_{B}
and
d^{\prime\prime}_{B}\colon B^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}

satisfying the conditions in the lemma.
By Lemma
6.1
applied to the graded
quasi-algebra
B^{*}
over
S
and the element
f(r_{\alpha})\in S
, there
exists a unique odd derivation
d^{\prime}_{\alpha}\colon C_{\alpha}^{*}\longrightarrow C_{\alpha}^{*}

forming a commutative square diagram with
d^{\prime}_{B}
, and a unique odd
derivation
d^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}\colon C_{\alpha}^{*}\longrightarrow C_{\alpha}^{*}
forming
a commutative square diagram with
d^{\prime\prime}_{B}
.
As both the derivations
d^{\prime}_{B}
and
d^{\prime\prime}_{B}
form commutative square
diagrams with
d_{A}
, it follows that both the derivations
d^{\prime}_{\alpha}

and
d^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}
form commutative square diagrams with
d_{A}
.
Applying Lemma
6.1
to the graded
quasi-algebra
A^{*}
over
R
and the element
r_{\alpha}\in R
, we
conclude that
d^{\prime}_{\alpha}=d^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}
.
Since this holds for all
1\leq\alpha\leq N
and the map of graded rings

B^{*}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}C_{\alpha}^{*}
is injective, it follows
that
d^{\prime}_{B}=d^{\prime\prime}_{B}
, as desired.

To prove existence, consider a subsequence of indices

1\leq\alpha_{1}<\dotsb<\alpha_{k}\leq N
, where
k\geq 1
.
Put
C_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}^{*}=R[r_{\alpha_{1}}^{-1},\dotsc,r_{\alpha_{k}}^{-1}]\otimes_{R}A^{*}
.
By Lemma
6.1
, there exists
a unique odd derivation
d_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}\colon C_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}^{*}\longrightarrow C_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}^{*}

on the graded ring
C_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}^{*}
forming
a commutative square diagram with
d_{A}
.
Consider the Čech
coresolution (
2
) of
the graded quasi-module
B^{*}=S\otimes_{R}A^{*}
over
S
,

(62)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muB^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}C_{\alpha}^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}C_{\alpha,\beta}^{*}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muC_{1,\dotsc,N}^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

(see the proof of Lemma
1.14
(a)).
Endowing every term of the Čech
coresolution (
62
), except
the leftmost one, with its own “vertical” differential equal to
the direct sum of the respective derivations

d_{\alpha_{1},\dotsc,\alpha_{k}}
, we notice that it follows from
Lemma
6.1
that the vertical
and “horizontal” (Čech) differentials
on (
62
) commute.
Since the coresolution (
62
)
is exact, we can pass to the kernel and conclude that there exists
a unique differential
d_{B}
on
B^{*}
making a commutative square diagram
with the vertical differential on
\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}C_{\alpha}^{*}

and the leftmost nontrivial horizontal differential
in (
62
).
Since the latter differential
B^{*}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}C_{\alpha}^{*}
is an injective homomorphism of graded rings, while
the vertical differential on
\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}C_{\alpha}^{*}
is
an odd derivation, it follows that
d_{B}
is an odd derivation on
B^{*}
,
as desired.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be
a graded quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, be a finite collection
of homomorphisms of commutative rings such that the collection of
induced maps of the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an affine open covering of an affine scheme.
Consider the graded quasi-algebras
B_{\alpha}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A^{*}

over the commutative rings
S_{\alpha}
and the graded quasi-algebras

C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}A^{*}
over the commutative
rings
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}
, as per the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(a).
Suppose given, for every index
\alpha
, an odd derivation
d_{\alpha}\colon B_{\alpha}^{*}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{*}
of degree
1
on the graded
ring
B_{\alpha}^{*}
.
Assume that, for every pair of indices
\alpha
and
\beta
,
the two odd derivations on the graded ring
C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}

arising from the odd derivations
d_{\alpha}^{*}
and
d_{\beta}^{*}

on the graded rings
B_{\alpha}^{*}
and
B_{\beta}^{*}
, as per
the construction of
Lemma
6.2
, agree.
Then there exists a unique odd derivation
d\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
of
degree
1
on the graded ring
A^{*}
such that, for every
index
\alpha
, the odd derivation
d_{\alpha}
on the graded ring

B_{\alpha}^{*}
arises from the odd derivation
d
on the graded ring

A^{*}
via the construction of
Lemma
6.2
.

Proof.

The argument, based on the Čech coresolution

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muA^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{N}B_{\alpha}^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq N}C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb,

is similar to the proof of the existence assertion in
Lemma
6.2
.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be
a graded quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
be a CDG-ring structure on
A^{*}
.
Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Consider the graded quasi-algebra
B^{*}=S\otimes_{R}A^{*}\simeq A^{*}\otimes_{R}S

over the commutative ring
S
, as per the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(a).
Then there exists a unique CDG-ring structure
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})

on the graded ring
B^{*}
such that the natural homomorphism of
graded rings
g=S\otimes_{R}f\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
is a strict morphism of
CDG-rings
(g,0)\colon A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

Proof.

For any strict morphism of CDG-rings
(g,0)\colon(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})\longrightarrow(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
, one has
h_{B}=g(h_{A})
.
Thus the uniqueness assertion of the lemma follows from
the uniqueness assertion of
Lemma
6.2
.
To prove the existence, it remains to show that the odd derivation

d_{B}\colon B^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
provided by
Lemma
6.2
together
with the curvature element
h_{B}=g(h_{A})
form a CDG-ring structure
on
B^{*}
.
There are two equations (i–ii) from
Section
6.2
that we need to check.
The equation (ii),
d_{B}(h_{B})=0
, holds because

d_{B}(h_{B})=d_{B}(g(h_{A}))=g(d_{A}(h_{A}))=g(0)=0
.
To check the equation (i) for the square
d_{B}^{2}
of
the derivation
d_{B}
, notice that both
d_{B}^{2}
and the commutator
map
[h_{B},{-}]
are even derivations of degree
2
on
B^{*}
.
Both of them form a commutative square diagram with one and the same
even derivation
d_{A}^{2}=[h_{A},{-}]
on
A^{*}
.
Now one needs to go through the rounds of the proofs of
Lemmas
6.1

and
6.2
and check that
the uniqueness assertions of the two lemmas apply to even derivations
of degree
2
just as well as to odd derivations of degree
1
.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be
a graded quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, be a finite collection
of homomorphisms of commutative rings such that the collection of
induced maps of the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an affine open covering of an affine scheme.
Consider the graded quasi-algebras
B_{\alpha}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A^{*}

over the commutative rings
S_{\alpha}
and the graded quasi-algebras

C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}A^{*}
over the commutative
rings
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}
, as per the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(a).
Suppose given, for every index
\alpha
, a CDG-ring structure

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha}=(B^{*}_{\alpha},d_{\alpha},h_{\alpha})
on the graded
ring
B_{\alpha}^{*}
.
Assume that, for every pair of indices
\alpha
and
\beta
,
the two CDG-ring structures on the graded ring
C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}

arising from the CDG-ring structures
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha}
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\beta}

on the graded rings
B_{\alpha}^{*}
and
B_{\beta}^{*}
, as per
the construction of
Lemma
6.4
, are
equal to each other (in the strict sense of the equality of the two
differentials and the two change-connection elements).
Then there exists a unique CDG-ring structure
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d,h)
on
the graded ring
A^{*}
such that, for every index
\alpha
,
the CDG-ring structure
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha}
on the graded ring
B_{\alpha}^{*}

arises from the CDG-ring structure
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded ring
A^{*}

via the construction of
Lemma
6.4
.

Proof.

The existence of an odd derivation
d\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
of degree
1

forming commutative square diagrams with the odd derivations

d_{\alpha}\colon B_{\alpha}^{*}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{*}
is a particular case of
the result of Lemma
6.3
.
Using the Čech coresolution from the proof of the latter lemma,
one can see that there exists a unique element
h\in A^{2}
whose
images in
B_{\alpha}^{2}
are equal to
h_{\alpha}
for all indices
\alpha
.
The equations (i–ii) from the definition of a CDG-ring in
Section
6.2
hold for
(A^{*},d,h)
,
since they hold for
(B_{\alpha}^{*},d_{\alpha},h_{\alpha})
and the graded
ring homomorphism
A^{*}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{N}B_{\alpha}^{*}

is injective.
∎

The following definition goes back to
[
45
, Section B.1]
,

[
17
, Section 1.2]
, and
[
52
, Section 2.4]
.
Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
A
quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
the scheme
X
with the open covering
\mathbf{W}
is a set of data
consisting of

•

a quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra

\mathcal{B}^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{B}^{i}
over
X
(see
Sections
3.1

and
6.1
for the definition);

•

an odd derivation
d_{U}=d_{\mathcal{B},U}\colon\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
of
degree
1
on the graded ring
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
and a curvature element

h_{U}=h_{\mathcal{B},U}\in\mathcal{B}^{2}(U)
given for each affine open subscheme

U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
;

•

a change-of-connection element
a_{VU}=a_{\mathcal{B},VU}\in\mathcal{B}^{1}(V)

given for each pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

The following three axioms must be satisfied:

(xi)

for each affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
,
the triple
(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
is a CDG-ring
(see Section
6.2
for the definition);

(xii)

for each pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the pair
(\rho_{\mathcal{B},VU},a_{\mathcal{B},VU})
is
a morphism of CDG-rings
(\rho_{\mathcal{B},VU},a_{\mathcal{B},VU})\colon\allowbreak(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{B},V},h_{\mathcal{B},V})
,
where
\rho_{UV}=\rho_{\mathcal{B},UV}\colon\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{*}(V)
is
the restriction morphism in the sheaf of graded rings
\mathcal{B}^{*}
on
X
;

(xiii)

for each triple of affine open subschemes
T\subset V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the morphisms of CDG-rings

(\rho_{VU},a_{VU})\colon(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{U},h_{U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V),d_{V},h_{V})
,

(\rho_{TV},a_{TV})\colon(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V),d_{V},h_{V})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(T),d_{T},h_{T})
,
and
(\rho_{TU},a_{TU})\colon(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{U},h_{U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(T),d_{T},h_{T})

form a commutative triangular diagram in the category of CDG-rings;
in other words, the equation
a_{\mathcal{B},TU}=a_{\mathcal{B},TV}+\rho_{\mathcal{B},TV}(a_{\mathcal{B},VU})
holds in
\mathcal{B}^{1}(T)
.

A
morphism of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras

f^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\colon\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over the scheme
X
with the open covering
\mathbf{W}
is
a set of data consisting of

•

a morphism of quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebras
f\colon\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
over
X
;

•

a change-of-connection element
a_{f,U}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(U)
given
for each affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

The following two axioms must be satisfied:

(xiv)

for each affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
,
the pair
(f(U),a_{f,U})
is a morphism of CDG-rings

(f(U),a_{f,U})\colon(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{A},U},h_{\mathcal{A},U})
, where
f(U)\colon\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)

is the homomorphism of graded rings assigned to the affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
by the morphism of quasi-coherent graded
quasi-algebras
f\colon\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
over
X
;

(xv)

for each pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the square diagram of morphisms of CDG-rings

\textstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f(U),a_{f,U})}
\scriptstyle{(\rho_{\mathcal{B},VU},a_{\mathcal{B},VU})}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{A},U},h_{\mathcal{A},U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\rho_{\mathcal{A},VU},a_{\mathcal{A},VU})}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{B},V},h_{\mathcal{B},V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f(V),a_{f,V})}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{A},V},h_{\mathcal{A},V})}

is commutative; in other words, the equation

a_{\mathcal{A},VU}+\rho_{\mathcal{A},VU}(a_{f,U})=f(V)(a_{\mathcal{B},VU})+a_{f,V}

holds in
\mathcal{A}^{1}(V)
.

The
composition
of morphisms of quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebras is defined in the obvious way, based on
the definition of the composition of morphisms of CDG-rings
in Section
6.2
.
We leave it to the reader to fill the straightforward details.

Corollary 6.6
.

The category of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras on a scheme
X

does not depend on an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
Specifically, the functor of restriction to affine open subschemes
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
is an equivalence between the category of
quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras on the scheme
X
with the open
covering
\{X\}
and the category of quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebras on the scheme
X
with the open covering
\mathbf{W}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra on the scheme

X
with the open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
We need to explain how to extend
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
to a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra on the scheme
X
with the open covering
\{X\}
.

Let
U\subset X
be an affine open subscheme, and let

U=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{N}V_{\alpha}
be a finite covering of
U
by
affine open subschemes
V_{\alpha}
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
For every pair of indices
\alpha
and
\beta
, put

V_{\alpha\beta}=V_{\alpha}\cap V_{\beta}\subset U
and

c_{\alpha\beta}=a_{V_{\alpha\beta}V_{\alpha}}-a_{V_{\alpha\beta}V_{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}^{1}(V_{\alpha\beta})
.
It follows from axiom (xiii) that the collection of elements

c=(c_{\alpha\beta})_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{N}
is a Čech
1
-cocycle
with coefficients in the sheaf of abelian groups
\mathcal{B}^{1}|_{U}
on
the scheme
U
with respect to the open covering

U=\bigcup_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}
.
Since the sheaf
\mathcal{B}^{1}|_{U}
, viewed as a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-modules
with respect either to the left or to the right
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-module
structure, is quasi-coherent, the Čech
1
-cocycle
c
is
a coboundary (see, e. g.,
[
32
, Section Tag 01X8]
).
This means that there exists a collection of elements

b_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{B}^{1}(V_{\alpha})
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, such that

c_{\alpha\beta}=\rho_{V_{\alpha\beta}V_{\alpha}}(b_{\alpha})-\rho_{V_{\alpha\beta}V_{\beta}}(b_{\beta})
for all indices
\alpha

and
\beta
.

Now, for every index
\alpha
, consider the CDG-ring structure

(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V_{\alpha}),d^{\prime}_{\alpha},h^{\prime}_{\alpha})
on the graded ring

\mathbf{B}^{*}(V_{\alpha})
such that
(\mathrm{id},-b_{\alpha})
is a change-of-connection
morphism of CDG-rings
(\mathrm{id},-b_{\alpha})\colon(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V_{\alpha}),d^{\prime}_{\alpha},h^{\prime}_{\alpha})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V_{\alpha}),d_{\mathcal{B},V_{\alpha}},h_{\mathcal{B},V_{\alpha}})
.
Then the collection of CDG-ring structures

(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V_{\alpha}),d^{\prime}_{\alpha},h^{\prime}_{\alpha})
satisfies the assumptions
of Lemma
6.5
, so it
arises from a unique CDG-ring structure
(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d^{\prime},h^{\prime})
on
the graded ring
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
.
At this point, we put
(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})=(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and
a_{V_{\alpha}U}=-b_{\alpha}
.
The remaining verifications are easier, and left to the reader.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
U=\Spec R
be an affine scheme.
Then the rule assigning the CDG-quasi-algebra

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)=(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
to a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
U
defines a natural equivalence
between the category of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras over
U

and the category of CDG-quasi-algebras over
R
.

Proof.

The functor in one direction is described in the formulation of
the lemma.
The inverse functor assigns to a CDG-quasi-algebra

A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
over
R
the collection of CDG-quasi-algebras

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{V}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
over the rings of functions

S=\mathcal{O}_{U}(V)
on affine open subschemes
V\subset U
constructed in
Lemma
6.4
.
So we put
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(V)=(\mathcal{A}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{A},V},h_{\mathcal{A},V})=B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{V}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
.
The distinctive property of the resulting quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
is that all the change-of-connection
elements
a_{\mathcal{A},TV}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(T)
vanish, i. e.,
a_{\mathcal{A},TV}=0
for all
affine open subschemes
T\subset V\subset U
.
The point is that this property is, of course, not preserved by
isomorphisms of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras.

Given an arbitrary quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
,
put
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)
and consider the related quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
, as in the previous paragraph.
Then, first of all, we have a natural isomorphism of graded
quasi-algebras
f_{V}\colon\mathcal{A}^{*}(V)=\mathcal{O}_{U}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}(U)}A^{*}\simeq\mathcal{B}^{*}(V)
,
since
\mathcal{B}^{*}
is a quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra over
U
.
Now the collection of (essentially, change-of-connection) isomorphisms
of CDG-rings
(f_{V},a_{\mathcal{B},UV})\colon\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(V)=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{B},V},h_{\mathcal{B},V})=\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(V)
defines a natural
isomorphism of quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras

\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
. ∎

6.4.
Quasi-coherent and contraherent CDG-modules

In this section and the next one we use the opportunity to provide
supplementary details to the expositions in
[
45
, Section B.1]
,

[
17
, Section 1.2]
, and
[
52
, Section 2.4]
concerning
quasi-coherent CDG-modules.
Then we also introduce locally contraherent CDG-modules over
quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebras over schemes.

The following well-known definition was already mentioned in
Section
6.2
.
Let
A^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}A^{i}
be a graded ring and

M^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{i}
be a graded left
A^{*}
-module.
Let
d_{A}\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
be an odd derivation of degree
1
.
A homogeneous additive map
d_{M}\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}
of degree
1
is
said to be an
odd derivation on
M
compatible with
the odd derivation
d_{A}
on
A
if one has
d_{M}(ax)=d_{A}(a)x+(-1)^{|a|}ad_{M}(x)
for all homogeneous elements
a\in A^{|a|}

and
x\in M^{|x|}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
A^{*}
and
B^{*}
be graded rings endowed with odd derivations

d_{A}\colon A^{*}\longrightarrow A^{*}
and
d_{B}\colon B^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
of degree
1
.
Let
A^{*}\longrightarrow B^{*}
be a homomorphism of graded rings forming
a commutative square diagram with the derivations
d_{A}
and
d_{B}
.
In this setting:

(a)
Let
M^{*}
be a graded left
A^{*}
-module endowed with
an odd derivation
d_{M}\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}
of degree
1
compatible
with the odd derivation
d_{A}
on
A^{*}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
N^{*}=B^{*}\otimes_{A^{*}}M^{*}
.
Then there exists a unique odd derivation
d_{N}\colon N^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}

compatible with the derivation
d_{B}
on
B^{*}
such that the natural
map
\iota\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
forms a commutative square diagram with
the derivations
d_{M}
and
d_{N}
.

(b)
Let
P^{*}
be a graded left
A^{*}
-module endowed with
an odd derivation
d_{P}\colon P^{*}\longrightarrow P^{*}
of degree
1
compatible
with the odd derivation
d_{A}
on
A^{*}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
Q^{*}=\Hom_{A^{*}}^{*}(B^{*},P^{*})
.
Then there exists a unique odd derivation
d_{Q}\colon Q^{*}\longrightarrow Q^{*}

compatible with the derivation
d_{B}
on
B^{*}
such that the natural
map
\pi\colon Q^{*}\longrightarrow P^{*}
forms a commutative square diagram with
the derivations
d_{Q}
and
d_{P}
.

Proof.

Part (a): to prove the existence, it suffices to put

d_{N}(b\otimes x)=d_{B}(b)\otimes x+(-1)^{|b|}b\otimes d_{M}(x)
for all

b\otimes x\in B^{|b|}\otimes M^{|x|}
.
The uniqueness follows from the fact that the graded
B^{*}
-module

N^{*}
is generated by the image of the homogenenous map

\iota\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
.

Part (b): to prove the existence, it suffices to put

d_{Q}(f)(b)=d_{P}(f(b))-(-1)^{|f|}f(d_{B}(b))
for all
b\in B^{|b|}

and
f\in\Hom_{A^{*}}^{|f|}(B^{*},P^{*})
.
The uniqueness follows from the fact that the graded
B^{*}
-module

Q^{*}
is
cogenerated
by the homogeneous map

\pi\colon Q^{*}\longrightarrow P^{*}
.
The latter statement means that, for every nonzero homogeneous
element
g\in Q^{|g|}
there exists a homogeneous element
b\in B^{|b|}

such that
\pi(bg)\neq 0
in
P^{*}
(as one can easily check).
Now, for every odd derivation
d_{Q}
on
Q^{*}
satisfying
the conditions of part (b) and every element
f\in Q^{|f|}
, we have

bd_{Q}(f)=(-1)^{|b|}d_{Q}(bf)-(-1)^{|b|}d_{B}(b)f
, hence the element

\pi(bd_{Q}(f))=(-1)^{|b|}\pi(d_{Q}(bf))-(-1)^{|b|}\pi(d_{B}(b)f)=(-1)^{|b|}d_{P}(b\pi(f))-(-1)^{|b|}d_{B}(b)\pi(f)\in P^{*}
is uniquely
determined by
d_{B}
and
d_{P}
.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
be two
CDG-rings, and let
(f,0)\colon A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow B^{*}
be a strict morphism
of CDG-rings.
In this setting:

(a)
Let
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
be a left CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
N^{*}=B^{*}\otimes_{A^{*}}M^{*}
.
Then there exists a unique structure of CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded module
N^{*}
over
B^{*}
such that
the natural map
\iota\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
forms a commutative square
diagram with the derivations
d_{M}
and
d_{N}
.

(b)
Let
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(P^{*},d_{P})
be a left CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
Q^{*}=\Hom_{A^{*}}^{*}(B^{*},P^{*})
.
Then there exists a unique structure of CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(Q^{*},d_{Q})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded module
Q^{*}
over
B^{*}
such that
the natural map
\pi\colon Q^{*}\longrightarrow P^{*}
forms a commutative square
diagram with the derivations
d_{Q}
and
d_{P}
.

Proof.

Part (a): the uniqueness follows immediately from
Lemma
6.8
(a).
To prove the existence, notice that
(B^{*},d_{B})
is a CDG-bimodule
over the CDG-rings
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, while
(M^{*},d_{M})
is
a CDG-bimodule over the CDG-rings
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
(\mathbb{Z},0,0)
.
The construction of the tensor product of CDG-bimodules from
Section
6.2
provides the desired
CDG-module structure
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\otimes_{A^{*}}M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the tensor product

N^{*}=B^{*}\otimes_{A^{*}}\penalty M^{*}
.

Part (b): the uniqueness follows immediately from
Lemma
6.8
(b).
To prove the existence, notice that
(B^{*},d_{B})
is a CDG-bimodule
over the CDG-rings
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
The construction of the
\Hom
CDG-module from
Section
6.2
provides the desired
CDG-module structure
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\Hom_{A^{*}}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
on
the graded
B^{*}
-module
Q^{*}=\Hom_{A^{*}}^{*}(B^{*},P^{*})
.
∎

Corollary 6.10
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
be
a CDG-quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such that
the related morphism of affine schemes
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion.
Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d_{B},h_{B})
be the induced CDG-quasi-algebra
structure on the graded quasi-algebra
B^{*}=S\otimes_{R}A^{*}
over
S
,
as in Lemma
6.4
.
In this setting:

(a)
Let
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
be a left CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
N^{*}=S\otimes_{R}M^{*}
, as per
the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(b).
Then there exists a unique structure of CDG-module
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded module
N^{*}
over
B^{*}
such that
the natural map
\iota\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
forms a commutative square
diagram with the derivations
d_{M}
and
d_{N}
.

(b)
Let
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(P^{*},d_{M})
be a left CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
Consider the graded left
B^{*}
-module
Q^{*}=\Hom^{*}_{R}(S,P^{*})
, as per
the graded version of
Lemma
1.20
(c).
Then there exists a unique structure of CDG-module
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(Q^{*},d_{Q})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded module
Q^{*}
over
B^{*}
such that
the natural map
\pi\colon Q^{*}\longrightarrow P^{*}
forms a commutative square
diagram with the derivations
d_{Q}
and
d_{P}
.

Proof.

In view of the alternative proofs of
Lemma
1.20
(b–c),
this is a special case of Lemma
6.9
.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(A^{*},d_{A},h_{A})
be
a CDG-quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
R\longrightarrow S_{\alpha}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq k
, be a finite collection
of homomorphisms of commutative rings such that the collection of
induced maps of the spectra
\Spec S_{\alpha}\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an affine open covering of an affine scheme.
Consider the induced CDG-quasi-algebra structures

B_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B_{\alpha}^{*},d_{\alpha},h_{\alpha})
and
C_{\alpha\beta}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(C_{\alpha\beta}^{*},d_{\alpha\beta},h_{\alpha\beta})
on the graded
quasi-algebras
B_{\alpha}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}A^{*}
and

S_{\alpha\beta}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}A^{*}
over the rings

S_{\alpha}
and
S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}
, as per
Lemma
6.4
.
In this setting:

(a)
Let
M^{*}
be a graded left
A^{*}
-module.
Suppose given, for every index
\alpha
, a CDG-module structure

(N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha},d_{N,\alpha})
over the CDG-ring
B_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
the graded left
B_{\alpha}^{*}
-module
N_{\alpha}^{*}=S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}M^{*}
.
Assume that, for every pair of indices
\alpha
and
\beta
,
the two CDG-module structures over the CDG-ring
C_{\alpha\beta}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

on the graded
C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}
-module
(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta})\otimes_{S_{\alpha}}N_{\alpha}^{*}\simeq(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta})\otimes_{S_{\beta}}N_{\beta}^{*}
arising from the CDG-module structures
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha}

and
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\beta}
on the graded modules
N^{*}_{\alpha}
and
N^{*}_{\beta}
,
as per the construction of
Corollary
6.10
(a),
coincide with each other.
Then there exists a unique CDG-module structure
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})

over the CDG-ring
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded
A^{*}
-module
M^{*}

such that, for every index
\alpha
, the CDG-module structure

N_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
arises from the CDG-module
structure
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
via the construction of
Corollary
6.10
(a).

(b)
Let
P^{*}
be a graded left
A^{*}
-module such that,
for every degree
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
R
-module
P^{i}
is contraadjusted.
Suppose given, for every index
\alpha
, a CDG-module structure

(Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha},d_{Q,\alpha})
over the CDG-ring
B_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
the graded left
B_{\alpha}^{*}
-module

Q_{\alpha}^{*}=\Hom^{*}_{R}(S_{\alpha},P^{*})
.
Assume that, for every pair of indices
\alpha
and
\beta
,
the two CDG-module structures over the CDG-ring
C_{\alpha\beta}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

on the graded
C_{\alpha\beta}^{*}
-module
\Hom_{S_{\alpha}}^{*}(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta},\>Q_{\alpha}^{*})\simeq\Hom_{S_{\beta}}^{*}(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta},\>Q_{\beta}^{*})
arising from the CDG-module
structures
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\alpha}
and
Q^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}_{\beta}
on the graded modules

Q^{*}_{\alpha}
and
Q^{*}_{\beta}
, as per the construction of
Corollary
6.10
(b),
coincide with each other.
Then there exists a unique CDG-module structure
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(P^{*},d_{M})

over the CDG-ring
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on the graded
A^{*}
-module
P^{*}

such that, for every index
\alpha
, the CDG-module structure

Q_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
arises from the CDG-module
structure
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
via the construction of
Corollary
6.10
(b).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
Lemma
6.3
.
In part (a), the argument is based on exactness of the Čech
coresolution (
5
)

(63)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{k}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}M^{*}\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq k}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta}\otimes_{R}M^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\dotsb

One endows the middle term
of (
63
) with a “vertical”
differential constructed as the direct sum of
the derivations
d_{N,\alpha}
.
Then one endows all the terms
of (
63
) going further to the right
with the vertical differentials constructed as the direct sums of
the induced derivations on the tensor products, produced by
the construction of
Corollary
6.10
(a).
The vertical differentials form commutative square diagrams with
the horizontal (Čech) differentials, and it remains to construct
the desired differential
d_{M}\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}
by passing to the kernel.
The pair
(M^{*},d_{M})
is a CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
because
the direct sum
\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{k}N_{\alpha}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a CDG-module
over
A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and the map
M^{*}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{k}N_{\alpha}^{*}=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{k}S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}M^{*}
is injective.

The proof of part (b) is dual-analogous and based on exactness of
the Čech resolution (
6
)

(64)

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq k}\Hom_{R}^{*}(S_{\alpha}\otimes_{R}S_{\beta},\>P^{*})\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\bigoplus\nolimits_{\alpha=1}^{k}\Hom_{R}^{*}(S_{\alpha},P^{*})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muP^{*}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0

(see
[
46
, formula (1.3) in Lemma 1.2.6(b)]
).
The exactness of (
64
) depends on
the assumption that the grading components of
P^{*}
are contraadjusted

R
-modules; that is where this assumption is used.
∎

Let
(f,a)\colon B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow A^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a morphism of CDG-rings.
Let
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})
be a left CDG-module over
A^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and let

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
be a left CDG-module over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We will say that a homogeneous additive map
g\colon N^{*}\longrightarrow M^{*}

of degree
0
is a
map of CDG-modules compatible with
the morphism of CDG-rings
(f,a)
if
g
is a morphism of
graded
B^{*}
-modules and the map
g
forms a commutative
square diagram with the differential
d_{N}
on
N^{*}
and
the differential
d^{\prime}_{M}
on
M^{*}
given by the formula (vi) from
Section
6.2
.
Similarly, a homogeneous additive map
g\colon M^{*}\longrightarrow N^{*}
of degree
0

is a
map of CDG-modules compatible with the morphism of
CDG-rings
(f,a)
if
g
is a morphism of graded
B^{*}
-modules
and the map
g
forms a commutative square diagram with
the differential
d_{N}
on
N^{*}
and the differential
d^{\prime}_{M}
on
M^{*}

given by the same formula (vi).
In the context of right CDG-modules, the notion of a map of
CDG-modules compatible with a morphism of CDG-rings is defined
similarly using formula (viii) from
Section
6.2
.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
(as defined in
Section
6.3
).
A
quasi-coherent left CDG-module

\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

is a set of data consisting of

•

a quasi-coherent graded left module
\mathcal{M}^{*}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}^{i}
over the quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{*}
over
X

(see Sections
3.3

and
6.1
for the definition);

•

an odd derivation
d_{\mathcal{M},U}\colon\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
of
degree
1
on the graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module
\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
compatible
with the odd derivation
d_{\mathcal{A},U}\colon\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)

on the graded ring
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
, given for each affine open
subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(xvi)

for each pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the map of restriction of sections

\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(V)
in the quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}^{*}
is a map
of CDG-modules compatible with the morphism of CDG-rings

(\rho_{\mathcal{A},VU},a_{\mathcal{A},VU})\colon(\mathcal{A}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{A},U},h_{\mathcal{A},U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{A},V},h_{\mathcal{A},V})
.

The definition of a
quasi-coherent right CDG-module

\mathcal{N}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is similar.

Let
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a morphism of quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebras over
X
(as defined in
Section
6.3
), and let

\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
be its underlying morphism of quasi-coherent
graded quasi-algebras.
Given a quasi-coherent left CDG-module
\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
the morphism of quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebras
\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}

allows one to endow the quasi-coherent graded left
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module

\mathcal{M}^{*}
with the structure of a quasi-coherent graded left

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-module.
In this context, the quasi-coherent graded left
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{*}

acquires a natural structure of quasi-coherent left CDG-module over

\mathcal{B}^{*}
, given by differentials
d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M},U}\colon\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)

obtained from the differentials
d_{\mathcal{M},U}
using the same formula (vi)
from Section
6.2
.
We leave details to the reader.

Corollary 6.12
.

The notion of a quasi-coherent CDG-module over a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over a scheme
X
does not depend on
an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
Specifically, given a quasi-coherent graded left
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module

\mathcal{M}^{*}
, any structure of a quasi-coherent CDG-module over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

on
\mathcal{M}^{*}
defined for the open covering
\mathbf{W}
can be uniquely extended
to a structure of quasi-coherent CDG-module over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
\mathcal{M}^{*}

defined for the open covering
\{X\}
of the scheme
X
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the case of an affine scheme
X=U
with
some open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
In this case, following (the proof of)
Lemma
6.7
, one can
assume that all the restriction morphisms of CDG-rings in
the quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
are strict,
i. e., the change-of-connection elements
a_{\mathcal{A},TV}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(T)

vanish for all affine open subschemes
T\subset U\subset X
.
In this case, it remains to pick a finite affine open covering

U=\bigcup_{\alpha=1}^{k}V_{\alpha}
of the affine scheme
U
subordinate
to
\mathbf{W}
.
Then the desired assertion is provided by
Lemma
6.11
(a).
∎

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-module

\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a set of data consisting of

•

a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded left module

\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{P}^{i}
over the quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebra

\mathcal{A}^{*}
over
X
(see Sections
3.3

and
6.1
for the definition);

•

an odd derivation
d_{\mathfrak{P},U}\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
of
degree
1
on the graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
compatible with
the odd derivation
d_{\mathcal{A},U}\colon\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
on the graded
ring
\mathcal{A}^{*}(U)
, defined for each affine open subscheme
U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

The following axiom must be satisfied:

(xvii)

for each pair of affine open subschemes
V\subset U\subset X

subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
, the map of corestriction of cosections

\mathfrak{P}^{*}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
in the locally contraherent cosheaf
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
is
a map of CDG-modules compatible with the morphism of CDG-rings

(\rho_{\mathcal{A},VU},a_{\mathcal{A},VU})\colon(\mathcal{A}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{A},U},h_{\mathcal{A},U})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{*}(V),d_{\mathcal{A},V},h_{\mathcal{A},V})
.

Let
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a morphism of quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebras over
X
and let
\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
be its
underlying morphism of quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebras.
Given a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-module
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the morphism of quasi-coherent graded quasi-algebras

\mathcal{B}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{*}
allows one to endow the locally contraherent
graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
with the structure of a locally
contraherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-module.
In this context, the
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent graded left

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{*}
acquires a natural structure of
a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-module over
\mathcal{B}^{*}
, given by
differentials
d^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{P},U}\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
obtained from
the differentials
d_{\mathfrak{P},U}
using formula (vi) from
Section
6.2
.
We leave details to the reader.

Corollary 6.13
.

The notion of a locally contraherent CDG-module structure over
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over a scheme
X
on
a given locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
does not
depend on an open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
Specifically, let
\mathbf{W}^{\prime}
be an open covering of
X
