such that
the open covering
\mathbf{W}
is subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
’.
Given a
\mathbf{W}^{\prime}
-locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{A}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
,
any structure of a
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-module over

\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
can be uniquely extended to a structure of

\mathbf{W}^{\prime}
-locally contraherent CDG-module over
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
.

Proof.

This is dual-analogous to
Corollary
6.12
.
It suffices to consider the case of an affine scheme
X=U
with
the open covering
\mathbf{W}^{\prime}=\{U\}
and some open covering
\mathbf{W}
.
In this case, following (the proof of)
Lemma
6.7
, one can
assume that all the restriction morphisms of CDG-rings in
the quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
are strict,
i. e., the change-of-connection elements
a_{\mathcal{A},TV}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(T)

vanish for all affine open subschemes
T\subset U\subset X
.
Then the desired assertion is provided by
Lemma
6.11
(b).
∎

6.5.
DG-categories of quasi-coherent and locally contraherent
CDG-modules

We refer to
[
45
, Section 1.2]
,
[
52
, Section 1]
,
or
[
60
, Section 1]
for a background discussion of DG-categories
suitable for our context.

Let us emphasize that, following the approach of
[
52
,
60
]
,
we take the
strict point of view
on DG-categories and
DG-functors.
This means that the complexes of morphisms in DG-categories are
considered up to isomorphism and
not
up to quasi-isomorphism.
So we work with DG-categories up to equivalences of DG-categories
and
not
up to quasi-equivalences.
See
[
52
, Introduction]
or
[
60
, end of Section 0.4]
.

We will use the notation similar to that in
[
52
,
60
]
.
In particular, for any DG-category
\mathbf{E}
, we denote by
\mathsf{Z}^{0}(\mathbf{E})

the preadditive category of closed morphisms of degree
0
in
\mathbf{E}
,
and by
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
the preadditive category of degree
0
cohomology
of
\mathbf{E}
(i. e., closed morphisms of degree
0
up to homotopy).
For any DG-category
\mathbf{E}
with a zero object, shifts, and cones,
the homotopy category
\mathsf{H}^{0}(\mathbf{E})
is naturally a triangulated category.
See
[
52
, Section 1.3]
or
[
60
, Sections 1.3–1.5]
.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(B^{*},d,h)
be a CDG-ring.
For any two left CDG-modules
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(L^{*},d_{L})
and
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(M^{*},d_{M})

over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the complex of abelian groups

\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
was constructed in
Section
6.2
.
Given three left CDG-modules
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and
M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the obvious map of composition of homogeneous maps of
graded
B^{*}
-modules

\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(L^{*},M^{*})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(K^{*},L^{*})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{B^{*}}^{*}(K^{*},M^{*})

is a morphism of complexes of abelian groups

\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},L^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{B^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},M^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}).

This construction defines the
DG-category of left CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, which we will denote by
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}
.

Similarly, for any two right CDG-modules
R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(R^{*},d_{R})
and

N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=(N^{*},d_{N})
, the construction from
Section
6.2
produces a complex of
abelian groups
\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
Given three right CDG-modules
R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and
K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the composition of homogeneous maps of graded
B^{*}
-modules
provides a composition morphism of complexes of abelian groups

\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},N^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{B^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},K^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}).

This defines the
DG-category of right CDG-modules
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, which we will denote by
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Mod--}}}B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
For any DG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the DG-categories
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}
and

{\operatorname{\mathbf{Mod--}}}B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
have zero objects, shifts, and cones; in fact, they
even have twists, infinite direct sums, and infinite products.

Let
X
be a scheme and
\mathcal{B}^{*}
be a quasi-coherent graded
quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathcal{L}^{*}
and
\mathcal{M}^{*}
be two quasi-coherent graded left

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules.
We denote by
\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{*},\mathcal{M}^{*})
the graded abelian group
of homogeneous maps of quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules
\mathcal{L}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}
of various degrees
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The sign rule for compatibility with the action of
\mathcal{B}^{*}
is
as written down in Section
6.2
.
The notation
\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}^{*}(\mathcal{R}^{*},\mathcal{N}^{*})
for two quasi-coherent
graded right
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules
\mathcal{R}^{*}
and
\mathcal{N}^{*}
has similar meaning.

Similarly, for any locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules

\mathfrak{P}
and
\mathfrak{Q}
, we denote by
\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},*}(\mathfrak{P}^{*},\mathfrak{Q}^{*})
the graded
abelian group of homogeneous maps of locally contraherent graded

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules
\mathfrak{P}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}
of various degrees
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The sign rule for compatibility with the action of
\mathcal{B}^{*}
is
as in Section
6.2
.

Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathcal{L}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be two quasi-coherent left CDG-modules
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(see Section
6.4

for the definitions).
Then the differential
d
on the graded abelian group

\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{*},\mathcal{M}^{*})
is constructed as follows.

Suppose given a homogeneous map of quasi-coherent graded

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules
f\in\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{|f|}(\mathcal{L}^{*},\mathcal{M}^{*})
.
For every affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
,
we have two left CDG-modules
(\mathcal{L}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{L},U})
and

(\mathcal{M}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{M},U})
over the CDG-ring

(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
.
We also have a homogeneous map of graded left
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
-modules

f(U)\colon\mathcal{L}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
of degree
|f|
.

Based on these data, the construction of the differential on
the complex of morphisms between two left CDG-modules, as spelled
out in Section
6.2
, provides
a homogeneous map of graded left
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
-modules

d(f(U))\colon\mathcal{L}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
of degree
|f|+1
.
The homogeneous map of quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules

d(f)\in\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{|f|+1}(\mathcal{L}^{*},\mathcal{M}^{*})
is defined by the rule

d(f)(U)=d(f(U))\colon\mathcal{L}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)
for all affine open
subschemes
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

It is straightforward to check, using formula (vi) from
Section
6.2
and axiom (xvi)
from Section
6.4
,
that the maps
d(f(U))
and
d(f(V))
form commutative square diagrams
with the restriction maps
\mathcal{L}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}^{*}(V)
and

\mathcal{M}^{*}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(V)
for all affine open subschemes

V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
So the homogeneous map of quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules

d(f)\colon\mathcal{L}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}
is well-defined.
The resulting differential
d
on the graded abelian group

\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{*},\mathcal{M}^{*})
makes it a complex, which we denote
by
\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathcal{L}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
Obviously, this construction does not depend on the choice of
an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of the scheme
X
.

Given three quasi-coherent left CDG-modules
\mathcal{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
\mathcal{L}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
and
\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the operation of composition of
homogeneous maps of quasi-coherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules provides
the composition morphism of complexes of abelian groups

\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathcal{L}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathcal{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathcal{L}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(\mathcal{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}).

This defines the
DG-category of quasi-coherent left
CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, which we will denote by
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Qcoh}}}
.
The
DG-category of quasi-coherent right CDG-modules
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is defined similarly and denoted by
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.

For any quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
a scheme
X
, the DG-categories
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Qcoh}}}
and
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Qcoh--}}}\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

have zero objects, shifts, and cones; in fact, they even have twists
and infinite direct sums.

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
U=\Spec R
be an affine scheme,
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra over
U
, and
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)=(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
be the corresponding CDG-quasi-algebra
over
R
, as per Lemma
6.7
.
Then the rule assigning the CDG-module
\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)=(\mathcal{M}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{M},U})

over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)
to a quasi-coherent CDG-module
\mathcal{M}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a natural equivalence between the DG-category of
quasi-coherent CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and the DG-category of
CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)
,

(65)

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U){\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}.

Proof.

This is essentially the same argument as the proof of
Corollary
6.12
.
According to (the proof of)
Lemma
6.7
, one can assume
that all the restriction morphisms of CDG-rings in
the quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
are strict.
Then the assertion follows from the equivalence
\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Qcoh}}}\simeq\mathcal{B}^{*}(U){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
on the level of the categories of graded modules together
with Corollary
6.10
(a).
∎

The locally contraherent version of the constructions above is similar.
Let
X
be a scheme with an open covering
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be
a quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra over
X
.
Let
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be two
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
(see
Section
6.4
for the definitions).
Then the differential
d
on the graded abelian group

\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},*}(\mathfrak{P}^{*},\mathfrak{Q}^{*})
is constructed as follows.

Suppose given a homogeneous map of locally contraherent graded

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules
f\in\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},|f|}(\mathfrak{P}^{*},\mathfrak{Q}^{*})
.
For every affine open subscheme
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
,
we have two left CDG-modules
(\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U],d_{\mathfrak{P},U})
and

(\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[U],d_{\mathfrak{Q},U})
over the CDG-ring

(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
.
We also have a homogeneous map of graded left
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
-modules

f[U]\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[U]
of degree
|f|
.

Based on these inputs, the construction of the differential on
the complex of morphisms between two left CDG-modules, as spelled
out in Section
6.2
, provides
a homogeneous map of graded left
\mathcal{B}^{*}(U)
-modules

d(f[U])\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[U]
of degree
|f|+1
.
The homogeneous map of locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules

d(f)\in\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},|f|+1}(\mathfrak{P}^{*},\mathfrak{Q}^{*})
is defined by the rule

d(f)[U]=d(f[U])\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[U]
for all affine open
subschemes
U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.

It is straightforward to check, using formula (vi) from
Section
6.2
and axiom (xvii)
from Section
6.4
,
that the maps
d(f[V])
and
d(f[U])
form commutative square diagrams
with the corestriction maps
\mathfrak{P}^{*}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U]
and

\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[V]\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}[U]
for all affine open subschemes

V\subset U\subset X
subordinate to
\mathbf{W}
.
So the homogeneous map of locally contraherent graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules

d(f)\colon\mathfrak{P}^{*}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}^{*}
is well-defined.
The resulting differential
d
on the graded abelian group

\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},*}(\mathfrak{P}^{*},\mathfrak{Q}^{*})
makes it a complex, which we denote
by
\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})
.
Obviously, this construction does not depend on the choice of
an open covering
\mathbf{W}
of the scheme
X
such that both
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and

\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
are
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-modules (i. e., both

\mathfrak{P}^{i}
and
\mathfrak{Q}^{i}
are
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent cosheaves on
X

for all degrees
i\in\mathbb{Z}
).

Given three
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent left CDG-modules

\mathfrak{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
,
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, and
\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, the operation
of composition of homogeneous maps of locally contraherent graded

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-modules provides the composition morphism of complexes
of abelian groups

\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(\mathfrak{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom^{\mathcal{B}^{*},{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(\mathfrak{K}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}},\mathfrak{Q}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}).

This defines the
DG-category of
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent
CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
, which we will denote by

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.
As usual, we put
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}=\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\{X\}}
, and
call the objects of
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}
the
contraherent CDG-modules
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
.
We also put
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{W}}\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
.

A
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-module
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is
said to be
X
-locally cotorsion
,
X
-locally injective
,

\mathcal{B}^{*}
-locally cotorsion
, or
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-locally injective

if its underlying graded
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-module
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
has the respective
property (defined in Section
6.1
).
We denote the full DG-subcategory of
X
-locally cotorsion

\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-modules by
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}
and the full DG-subcategory of
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-locally
cotorsion
\mathbf{W}
-locally contraherent CDG-modules by

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}^{\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}
.
The notation
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}

has similar meaning.

For any quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over a scheme
X

and any open covering
\mathbf{W}
of
X
, the DG-category
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}

has a zero object, shifts, and cones; in fact, it even has twists
and infinite direct products.
The full DG-subcategories
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}^{X{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Lcth}}}_{\mathbf{W}}
are closed under
shifts, twists, and direct products (hence also under cones).

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a CDG-ring endowed with
a ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow B^{0}
.
Let us say that a CDG-module
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is

R
-contraadjusted
(respectively,
R
-cotorsion
) if
the
R
-module
P^{i}
is contraadjusted (resp., cotorsion) for every
degree
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Let us also say that a CDG-module
P^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is

B^{*}
-cotorsion
if the graded
B^{*}
-module
P^{*}
is cotorsion.
Let us denote the full DG-subcategories of
B^{*}
-cotorsion,

R
-cotorsion, and
R
-contraadjusted left CDG-modules over
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}

by
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}\subset B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}\subset B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}
.
Clearly, all the three full DG-subcategories are closed under shifts,
twists, and direct products (hence also under cones) in
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}
.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
U=\Spec R
be an affine scheme,
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
be a quasi-coherent
CDG-quasi-algebra over
U
, and
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)=(\mathcal{B}^{*}(U),d_{\mathcal{B},U},h_{\mathcal{B},U})
be the corresponding CDG-quasi-algebra
over
R
, as per Lemma
6.7
.
Then the rule assigning the CDG-module

\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}[U]=(\mathfrak{P}^{*}[U],d_{\mathfrak{P},U})
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)
to a contraherent
CDG-module
\mathfrak{P}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
is a natural equivalence between
the DG-category of contraherent CDG-modules over
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
and
the DG-category of
R
-contraadjusted CDG-modules over
the CDG-ring
B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}=\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}(U)
,

(66)

\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}\simeq B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}.

The equivalence of
DG-categories (
66
) restricts
to equivalences of DG-categories

(67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}

\displaystyle\simeq B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}},

(68)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}

\displaystyle\simeq B^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}{\operatorname{\mathbf{--Mod}}}^{B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}.

Proof.

This is dual-analogous to
Lemma
6.14
, and essentially
the same argument as the proof of
Corollary
6.13
.
According to (the proof of)
Lemma
6.7
, one can assume
that all the restriction morphisms of CDG-rings in
the quasi-coherent CDG-quasi-algebra
\mathcal{B}^{\text{\smaller$\scriptstyle\blackdiamond$}}
on
U
are strict.
Then the assertions follow from the equivalences
\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}\simeq B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cta}}}}
,
\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{U{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\simeq B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
,

\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Ctrh}}}^{\mathcal{B}^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-lct}}}}\simeq B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}^{B^{*}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-cot}}}}
on the level
of the categories of graded modules together with
Corollary
6.10
(b).
∎

Appendix A
Quite Quasi-Modules and Quite Quasi-Algebras

In this appendix we collect alternative versions of some results
from Sections
1.4
–
1.7
applicable to a more
narrow class of quasi-modules and quasi-algebras but to a wider
class of homomorphisms of commutative rings
R\longrightarrow S
.

A.1.
Quite quasi-modules and strong quasi-modules

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
B
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule.
The natural ordinal-indexed filtration
F
on
B
is defined by
the rules

•

F_{0}B\subset B
is the
R
-
R
-subbimodule consisting
of all elements
b\in B
such that
rb-br=0
in
B
for all
r\in R
;

•

for every ordinal
\alpha>0
, the
R
-
R
-subbimodule

F_{\alpha}B\subset B
consists of all elements
b\in B
such that

rb-br\in\bigcup_{\beta<\alpha}F_{\beta}B
for all
r\in R
.

Obviously, one has
F_{\beta}B\subset F_{\alpha}B
for all ordinals

\beta\leq\alpha
.

We say that an
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
is a
quite quasi-module
over
R

[
56
, Section 2.3]
if
B=\bigcup_{\beta}F_{\beta}B
,
where the union is taken over all the ordinals
\beta
.
Equivalently,
B
is a quite quasi-module over
R
if and only if
there exists an ordinal
\alpha
such that
B=\bigcup_{\beta<\alpha}F_{\beta}B
.
All quite quasi-modules are quasi-modules over
R
in the sense
of Section
1.4
; but the converse is

not
true in general
[
56
, Examples 1.8, 2.8, and 3.8]
.

For any
R
-module
M
, viewed as an
R
-
R
-bimodule on which
the left and right actions of
R
agree, one has
F_{0}M=M
.
So
M
is quite quasi-module over
R
.
An
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
is a quite quasi-module over
R
if and only
if it belongs to the minimal full subcategory in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
containing

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
and closed under extensions and filtered inductive limits,
or equivalently, under infinite direct sums, extensions, subobjects,
and quotients
[
56
, Section 2.3]
.
The class of all quite quasi-modules is a localizing subcategory,
or in the terminology of
[
69
, Sections VI.2–3]
, a hereditary
torsion class in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
The full subcategory of quite quasi-modules in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
is also
closed under tensor products over
R

[
56
, Proposition 2.9]
.

We say that an
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
is a
strong quasi-module
over
R

[
56
, Section 2.2]
if
B=\bigcup_{n<\omega}F_{n}B

(where the union is taken over the nonnegative integers
n
.
In the more common terminology of
[
7
, Section 1.1]
, such
bimodules over a commutative ring are called
differential
bimodules
.

So, by the definition, any strong quasi-module is a quite quasi-module.
The converse is
not
true in general, for the basic reason that
the class of strong quasi-modules
need not
be closed under
extensions in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R

[
56
, Examples 1.5, 2.5, and 3.3]
.

Nevertheless, the class of strong quasi-modules is clearly always
closed under submodules, quotients, and infinite direct sums in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
In the terminology similar to
[
69
, Sections VI.1–3]
, one
can express the previous sentence by saying that the class of
strong quasi-modules is a
hereditary pretorsion class
in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
So the full subcategory of strong quasi-modules in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
is
a Grothendieck abelian category (just as the full subcategories of
quite quasi-modules and all quasi-modules).
The full subcategory of strong quasi-modules in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
is also
closed under tensor products over
R

[
56
, Proposition 2.6]
.

Given a homomorphism of commutative rings
K\longrightarrow R
, we say that
an
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
is an
R
-
R
-bimodule over
K

if the left and right actions of
K
on
B
agree.
The full subcategory of
R
-
R
-bimodules over
K
is denoted by

R_{K}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}{}_{K}R\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
.
If the ring
R
is finitely generated as a commutative
K
-algebra,
then all the three classes of quasi-modules, quite quasi-modules,
and strong quasi-modules coincide in
R_{K}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}{}_{K}R

(by
[
56
, Lemmas 1.3 and 2.1]
; see
[
56
, Section 2.3]
).

We refer to
[
69
, Chapter XI]
,
[
24
, Section 4]
,
and
[
52
, Section 2.3]
for a discussion of
flat
epimorphisms
of commutative rings.
Following these references, if
\sigma\colon R\longrightarrow S
is a flat
epimorphism of commutative rings, then the natural maps

S\rightrightarrows S\otimes_{R}S\longrightarrow S
are isomorphisms, the functor of
restriction of scalars
\sigma_{*}\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is fully
faithful, and its essential image
\sigma_{*}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
is closed under
extensions, infinite direct sums, infinite direct products, kernels,
and cokernels in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.

In particular, if
R\longrightarrow S
is a homomorphism of commutative rings
such that the induced map of the spectra
\Spec S\longrightarrow\Spec R
is
an open immersion of affine schemes, then
R\longrightarrow S
is a flat
epimorphism.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\sigma\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings.
Let
B
be a quite quasi-module over
R
.
Then

(a)
the natural maps
S\otimes_{R}B\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}B\otimes_{R}S\longleftarrow B\otimes_{R}S
are isomorphisms;

(b)
the
S
-
S
-bimodule
S\otimes_{R}B
is a quite quasi-module
over
S
.

Proof.

This is a straightforward generalization of
[
52
, Lemma 2.2]

and a quite quasi-module version of
Lemma
1.14
.
Part (a): it suffices to show that the
R
-
S
-bimodule
B\otimes_{R}S
,
viewed as an
R
-module with the left action of
R
, belongs to
the full subcategory
\sigma_{*}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Indeed, put
G_{\alpha}(B\otimes_{R}S)=F_{\alpha}B\otimes_{R}S
for every
ordinal
\alpha
(where
F
is the natural ordinal-indexed increasing
filtration on the
R
-
R
-bimodule
B
defined above).
Then we have
B\otimes_{R}S=\bigcup_{\beta}G_{\alpha}(B\otimes_{R}S)
.
Furthermore, for ever ordinal
\alpha
, the left and right actions
of
R
on the successive quotient bimodule

G_{\alpha}(B\otimes_{R}S)\Big/\bigcup\nolimits_{\beta<\alpha}G_{\beta}(B\otimes_{R}S)\simeq\biggl(F_{\alpha}B\Big/\bigcup\nolimits_{\beta<\alpha}F_{\beta}B\biggr)\otimes_{R}S

agree.
Therefore, the left
R
-module structures on the successive
quotients come via the restriction of scalars from some
S
-module
structures.
Since the full subcategory
\sigma_{*}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is
closed under extensions and inductive limits, the desired assertion
follows.
The proof of part (b) is similar to that of
[
52
, Lemma 2.2(b)]
.
∎

Corollary A.2
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings.
Let
A
be an
R
-
R
-bimodule,
B
be an
S
-
S
-bimodule,
and
A\longrightarrow B
be a homomorphism of
R
-
R
-bimodules.
Assume that the
R
-
R
-bimodule
A
is a quite quasi-module
over
R
.
Then the induced morphism of
S
-
R
-bimodules
S\otimes_{R}A\longrightarrow B

is an isomorphism if and only if the induced morphism of

R
-
S
-bimodules
A\otimes_{R}S\longrightarrow B
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is a generalization of
[
52
, Corollary 2.3]
and
a quite quasi-module version of
Corollary
1.15
.
The assertion follows from
Lemma
A.1
(a).
∎

A.2.
Quite quasi-algebras

Let
R
be a commutative ring.
Similarly to Section
1.5
,
a
quite quasi-algebra

A
over
R
is defined as an associative,
unital ring endowed with a ring homomorphism
R\longrightarrow A
such that,
viewed as an
R
-
R
-bimodule,
A
is a quite quasi-module over
R
.
Alternatively, a quite quasi-algebra over
R
is the same thing as
a monoid object in the monoidal full subcategory of quite quasi-modules
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod--}}}R
(notice that the tensor products of quite quasi-modules
over
R
are quite quasi-modules by
[
56
, Proposition 2.9]
).

Lemma A.3
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings and

A
be a quite quasi-algebra over
R
. In this context:

(a)
The tensor product
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq S\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{R}S\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
(as per Lemma
A.1
)
has a unique structure of associative ring for which the natural map

S\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
is a ring homomorphism inducing the given

S
-
S
-bimodule structure on
S\otimes_{R}A
and
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A

is a ring homomorphism.
Endowed with this ring structure,
S\otimes_{R}A
becomes a quite
quasi-algebra over
S
.

(b)
For any left
A
-module
M
, the left
S
-module of
tensor product
S\otimes_{R}M
admits a unique extension of its
S
-module
structure to a structure of left module over
S\otimes_{R}A
such that

M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an
A
-module morphism.

(c)
For any left
A
-module
P
, the left
S
-module of
homomorphisms
\Hom_{R}(S,P)
admits a unique extension of its

S
-module structure to a structure of left module over
S\otimes_{R}A

such that
\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow P
is an
A
-module morphism.

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
1.20
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings and

A
be a quite quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
for any left
A
-module
M
and any right module
N

over
S\otimes_{R}A
, the map of tensor products
N\otimes_{A}M\longrightarrow N\otimes_{S\otimes_{R}A}(S\otimes_{R}M)
induced by the ring and module maps

A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an isomorphism;

(b)
for any left
A
-module
M
and any left module
Q

over
S\otimes_{R}A
, the map of Hom modules
\Hom_{S\otimes_{R}A}(S\otimes_{R}M,\>Q)\longrightarrow\Hom_{A}(M,Q)
induced by the ring and module maps
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
M\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}M
is an isomorphism;

(c)
for any left module
N
over
S\otimes_{R}A
and any left

A
-module
P
, the map of Hom modules
\Hom_{S\otimes_{R}A}(N,\Hom_{R}(S,P))\longrightarrow\Hom_{A}(N,P)
induced by the ring and module maps
A\longrightarrow S\otimes_{R}A
and
\Hom_{R}(S,P)\longrightarrow P
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is quite quasi-algebra version of
Lemma
1.21
.
All the assertions follow from the alternative proofs of
Lemma
A.3
(b–c)
(which are similar to the alternative proofs of
Lemma
1.20
(b–c)).
∎

Corollary A.5
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings and

A
be a quite quasi-algebra over
R
. Then

(a)
the ring
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
is flat as a left and
right module over
A
;

(b)
for any flat left
A
-module
F
, the left module

S\otimes_{R}F
is flat over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(c)
for any projective left
A
-module
F
, the left module

S\otimes_{R}F
is projective over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(d)
for any cotorsion left
A
-module
C
, the left module

\Hom_{R}(S,C)
is cotorsion over
S\otimes_{R}A
;

(e)
for any injective left
A
-module
J
, the left module

\Hom_{R}(S,J)
is injective over
S\otimes_{R}A
.

Proof.

Similar to Corollary
1.22

and based on
Lemma
A.4

or the alternative proofs of
Lemma
A.3
(b–c).
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative ring and
A

be a quite quasi-algebra over
R
.
Let
E
be an associative ring and
E\longrightarrow A
be a ring homomorphism.
Assume that the ring
A
is flat as a left
E
-module.
Then the ring
S\otimes_{R}A\simeq A\otimes_{R}S
is also flat as a left

E
-module.

Proof.

This is similar to
Lemma
1.25
.
The assertion follows from
Corollary
A.5
(a).
∎

A.3.
Flaprojective modules

This short section, corresponding to
Section
1.6
, consists of one lemma only.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings,
A
be
a quite quasi-algebra over
R
, and
B=S\otimes_{R}A=A\otimes_{R}S
be the related
quite quasi-algebra over
S
as per
Lemma
A.3
(a).
In this context:

(a)
any
B/S
-flaprojective
B
-module is also

A/R
-flaprojective as an
A
-module;

(b)
if
F
is an
A/R
-flaprojective left
A
-module,
then
S\otimes_{R}F
is a
B/S
-flaprojective left
B
-module.

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
1.35
.
A reference to the alternative proof of
Lemma
A.3
(b) is used
in the proof of part (b).
∎

A.4.
Robustly flaprojective bimodules and flepi flat modules

The most substantial new results of
Appendix
A
are contained in this section,
which complements the discussion in
Section
1.7
.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
R\longrightarrow S
be a flat epimorphism of commutative rings,
A
and

K
be quite quasi-algebras over
R
, and
B=S\otimes_{R}A
and
L=S\otimes_{R}K

be the related quite quasi-algebras over
S
.
In this context:

(a)
Let
G
be a
(B/S,L)
-robustly flaprojective

B
-
L
-bimodule.
Then
G
is also
(A/R,K)
-robustly flaprojective as
an
A
-
K
-bimodule.

(b)
Let
F
be an
A
-
K
-bimodule whose underlying

R
-
R
-bimodule is a quite quasi-module over
R
.
Assume that the
A
-
K
-bimodule
F
is
(A/R,K)
-robustly
flaprojective.
Then the
B
-
L
-bimodule
S\otimes_{R}F\simeq S\otimes_{R}F\otimes_{R}S\simeq F\otimes_{R}S
is
(B/S,L)
-robustly flaprojective.

Proof.

This is similar to
Lemma
1.39
.
The proof of part (a) is based on
Corollary
A.5
(b)
and Lemma
A.7
(a).
The proof of part (b) is based on
Corollary
A.5
(a)
and Lemma
A.7
(b).
∎

Let us define two more cotorsion pairs in the category of modules
over a commutative ring
R
.
Let us say that an
R
-module
C
is
flepi cotorsion
if for
every flat epimorphism of commutative rings
R\longrightarrow S
one has

\Ext^{1}_{R}(S,C)=0
.
We will say that an
R
-module
F
is
flepi flat
if for
every flepi cotorsion
R
-module
C
one has
\Ext^{1}_{R}(F,C)=0
.
Furthermore, let us say that an
R
-module
C
is
strongly
flepi cotorsion
if for every flat epimorphism of commutative rings

R\longrightarrow S
one has
\Ext^{n}_{R}(S,C)=0
for all
n\geq 1
.
We will say that an
R
-module
F
is
weakly flepi flat
if
for every strongly flepi cotorsion
R
-module
C
one has

\Ext^{1}_{R}(F,C)=0
(or equivalently, for every strongly flepi cotorsion

R
-module
C
one has
\Ext^{n}_{R}(F,C)=0
for all
n\geq 1
).
Obviously, all cotorsion
R
-modules are strongly flepi cotorsion,
hence all weakly flepi flat
R
-modules are flat.

For any fixed ring
R
, there exists, up to isomorphism, at most a set
of flat ring epimorphisms
R\longrightarrow S
(as one can see
from
[
69
, Theorem XI.2.1.]
.
So the theorem of Eklof and Trlifaj
[
20
, Theorems 2 and 10]
,

[
25
, Theorem 6.11 and Corollary 6.14]
is applicable, and it tells
us that the pair of classes (flepi flat modules, flepi cotorsion
modules) is a complete cotorsion pair in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, while the pair of
classes (weakly flepi flat modules, strongly flepi cotorsion modules)
is a hereditary complete cotorsion pair in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
The flepi flat
R
-modules are precisely all the direct summands
of transfinitely iterated extensions of the
R
-modules
S
, where

R\longrightarrow S
are flat epimorphisms of commutative rings.
The weakly flepi flat
R
-modules are precisely all the direct
summands of transfinitely iterated extensions of the
R
-modules
S

as above and their
syzygy modules
(i. e., the
R
-modules
of cocycles in projective resolutions of the
R
-modules
S
).

Lemma A.9
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring,
A
be a quite quasi-algebra over
R
,
and
F
be an
A
-
R
-bimodule whose underlying
R
-
R
-bimodule is
a quite quasi-module over
R
.
In this context, if the left
A
-module
F
is
A/R
-flaprojective
and
H
is a weakly flepi flat
R
-module, then the left
A
-module

F\otimes_{R}H
is
A/R
-flaprojective.

Proof.

This is a version of Lemma
1.41
.
One observes that the class of
A/R
-flaprojective
left
A
-modules is closed under transfinitely iterated extensions
(by Remark
1.32
),
kernels of epimorphisms in
A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
(by
Lemma
1.29
(b)),
and direct summands.
Therefore, it suffices to consider the case of
H=S
, where

R\longrightarrow S
is a flat epimorphism of commutative rings.
In this case, one has
F\otimes_{R}S\simeq S\otimes_{R}F
by
Lemma
A.1
(a), and
the left
A
-module
S\otimes_{R}F
is
A/R
-flaprojective by
Lemma
A.7
(a–b).
∎

Remarks A.10
.

We
do not know
whether the assertion of
Lemma
A.9
holds for
all flat
R
-modules
H
(in other words, whether every
left
A/R
-flaprojective
(A,R)
-bimodule whose underlying

R
-
R
-bimodule is a quite quasi-module over
R
needs to be

(A/R,R)
-robustly flaprojective).
We also do not know how ubiquitous the weakly flepi flat
R
-modules
are in general.

The following two results are known, however:

•

Over any Noetherian commutative ring
R
, all countably generated
flat modules are flepi flat (and even quite flat, which is a stronger
property)
[
30
, Theorem 2.4]
.

•

Over any Noetherian commutative ring
R
with (at most)
countable spectrum, all flat modules are flepi flat (and even quite
flat)
[
58
, Theorem 1.17]
,
[
30
, Theorems 2.4 and 3.8]
.

So, for a quite quasi-algebra
A
over a Noetherian commutative ring

R
with countable spectrum, an
(A,R)
-bimodule whose underlying

R
-
R
-bimodule is a quite quasi-module over
R
is

(A/R,R)
-robustly flaprojective whenever it is
A/R
-flaprojective
as a left
A
-module.
